Lei 13/2008, de 13 de Outubro (Legislative authorization in penal matters)

Lei 6/2009, de 15 de Julho (Amends article 141º of the Penal Code)

Decreto-Lei 19/2009, de 8 de Abril

(Approves the Penal Code)

In view of the need to construct the legal system, the drafting and approval of the Penal Code of Timor-Leste was established by political leaders as one of the legislative priorities for guaranteeing the fundamental rights and freedoms enshrined in the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste.

This legal instrument results from the work developed by a commission of Timorese and international experts that acted under government guidance and in strict observance of the limits and content established in the law of legislative authorization in penal matters approved in the National Parliament.

The normative solutions enshrined, in addition to respecting the specific social and cultural realities of the Timorese community, also incorporate suggestions made by national and international organizations, contributions from various judicial operators working in Timor-Leste, as well as lessons drawn from comparative law.

It should be emphasized that the Penal Code now approved, rather than being a definitive endpoint, constitutes instead a fundamental stage in the construction of the Timorese legal system, always open to future improvements that, in the future, the evolution of international law, judicial practice and the teaching of law may counsel. Thus, in exercise of the legislative authorization granted under articles 1º and 2º of Lei n.º 13/2008, de 13 de Outubro and under the terms provided for in article 96.º of the Constitution, the Government decrees, to have force of law, the following:

###### Artigo 1.º

Approval of the Penal Code

The Penal Code published in annex and which forms an integral part of this instrument is approved.

###### Artigo 2.º

Repealing provision

1. The Indonesian Penal Code, in force in the legal system under the terms provided in art.1º of Lei 10/2003, is repealed.

2. All legal provisions contained in separate legislation that:

- a) Provide for and punish acts criminalized by the Penal Code now approved;
- b) Establish solutions contrary to those adopted in the General Part of the Penal Code.

###### Artigo 3.º

Entry into force

This diploma and the Penal Code shall enter into force on the 60th day following its publication.

Approved by the Council of Ministers on 18 March

The Prime Minister,

(Kay Rala Xanana Gusmão)

The Minister of Justice,

(Lúcia M. B. F. Lobato)

Promulgated on 30/03/09

Publish.

The President of the Republic,

(José Ramos Horta)

Annex

PENAL CODE

I – The restoration of independence and the approval of the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste in 2002, determined the need for the country to adopt its own modern legal order, which would enshrine the fundamental rights set forth in the constitutional precepts and reflect the social reality of the country. The maintenance in force of the Indonesian Penal Code was necessary to ensure the validity of a State criminal law, but proved inadequate to the new reality of the country and, in various circumstances, determined legal solutions contrary to the constitutional principles now enshrined.

Since the Timorese people have their own specificity and identity, it was necessary to draft their own Penal Code, with a philosophy that was intrinsic to them and guided by principles and values inherent to modern societies, which would respond to the current demands placed on the Country.

A commission of Timorese and international experts, created by the First Constitutional Government, proceeded to draft a Preliminary Draft Penal Code which, despite the approval of the Legislative Authorization Law on criminal matters, was not promulgated at the end of the legislature

At the beginning of 2008, with a new executive, a new proposal for a legislative authorization law for the approval of the Penal Code was presented to the National Parliament and the work of reviewing the preliminary draft Penal Code was resumed, with the diploma being subject to amendments and subjected to a broad public discussion.

After the approval of the legislative authorization, it was the responsibility of the Council of Ministers of the Fourth Constitutional Government to approve the Penal Code.

II – The General Part constitutes Book I of the Penal Code, and integrates the fundamental principles of criminal law enshrined in the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste and in the conventions, treaties and international agreements received in the Timorese internal legal order.

As this is a code based on a Democratic Rule of Law, the General Part enshrines the principle of human dignity, respect for the individual freedom of each citizen and the responsibility of the State to intervene only when there are unsustainable violations of legal interests fundamental to life in society, and the State must, in these circumstances, assume the right to punish and the social duty to reintegrate the agent into society. Also reflective of a Rule of Law are the principle of legality, the principle of guilt and the principle of humanity.

The enshrinement of the principle of legality, as a fundamental principle of Criminal Law, provided for in article 31 of the Constitution, determines that any action or omission can only be considered a crime and be punished as such when provided for by law. The observance of this principle requires the prohibition of the application of analogy in matters of classification of crimes, and the Court cannot, through an analogical interpretation of the norms contained in the Penal Code, classify a fact as a crime, define a state of dangerousness or determine a penalty or security measure.

The principle of non-retroactivity of criminal law, which prevents the retroactive application of criminal law, except when it proves concretely more favorable to the defendant, is also a corollary of the principle of legality.

The principle of humanity, enshrined in articles 29 and 32 of the Constitution, is the guiding principle that justifies the prohibition of the application of the death penalty, as well as the determination of penalties or security measures of a perpetual nature or of unlimited or indefinite duration.

Necessity, proportionality and adequacy are guiding principles of the normative options that shape this Code and underlie the application of each penalty or security measure, the purpose of which is always to protect legal interests essential to life in society and the social reintegration of the agent.

The principle of guilt is taken into account as a prerequisite for the application of the penalty, determining that there is no penalty without guilt (nulla poena sine culpa), this principle being a form of limiting the power of the State, since the measure of the penalty can never exceed the measure of guilt. The principle of guilt is also reflected in the treatment given to error regarding unlawfulness, in the enshrinement of non-imputability by reason of age and in the verification of psychic anomaly, which exclude the criminal responsibility of the agent for lack of guilt.

With regard to the legal consequences of the punishable act, it is observed that the penalty must always be executed in a pedagogical and resocializing manner, with this Code presenting different means of applying non-institutional sanctioning measures.

When, alternatively, a custodial or non-custodial penalty must be applied, the court is required to give reasoned preference to the non-custodial penalty whenever it adequately and sufficiently achieves the purposes of punishment and satisfies the requirements of reprobation and prevention of crime (article 62).

Thus, the application of alternative penalties is favored, especially in situations of petty and medium-level crime, with particular emphasis on the fine penalty and the community service penalty, which are enshrined as ways to better ensure the intended social reintegration of the offender.

The fine penalty is set in days, thus allowing better adaptation to the agent's guilt and economic conditions, with the value set for each day of fine varying according to the economic and financial situation of the convicted person and their personal burdens. On the other hand, in order to differentiate the fine penalty, as a principal penalty, from the fine substituting the prison sentence, different rules for converting the fine into days of imprisonment are established in the event of non-payment of the fine.

In community service, as a non-institutional sanctioning measure, the Code is concerned with clarifying and systematizing some fundamental aspects of its regime, leaving its development and application in concrete terms to autonomous law.

The prison sentence should only be applied when all the others prove inadequate to achieve the objectives of prevention and reprobation. A minimum duration of 30 days and a maximum of 25 years is established for the prison sentence, and the maximum limit may be increased to 30 years in cases specially provided for by law.

A corollary of the social reintegration of the offender is the institution of suspension of execution of the prison sentence, applicable in cases where the measure of the sentence in concrete terms does not exceed 3 years and the requirements of prevention of future crimes do not prevent its compliance. The suspension of execution of the prison sentence may be conditioned on compliance with duties or rules of conduct, or subject to monitoring by reintegration services.

For those not criminally responsible by virtue of psychic anomaly, security measures of limited temporal duration are enshrined, namely the measure of internment, whenever reasons of dangerousness so advise. The application of measures of a perpetual nature is not permitted, and the obligation to cease security measures is established whenever the state of dangerousness that legitimized them ends, admitting, in the case of foreigners, that the measures may be replaced by expulsion from the country.

The Penal Code, in defense of the fundamental values and legal interests to life in society, distinguishes crimes of a public nature, which must necessarily be protected by the State, from those which, being less serious, depend on the exercise of the right to file a complaint by the holder of that right, as already enshrined in criminal procedural legislation. Thus, all those crimes that, in the Special Part of the Penal Code, determine the obligation to file a complaint in the description of the respective legal type are considered semi-public crimes.

In matters of extinction of criminal responsibility and its effects, the time limits for the prescription of criminal proceedings, penalties, security measures and accessory penalties are set in the general part, as well as the situations of suspension are defined. However, it was decided that criminal proceedings and penalties should be imprescriptible when they relate to war crimes, crimes against peace, or crimes against humanity and freedom. Finally, other cases of extinction of responsibility are regulated, such as the death of the agent, amnesty, and pardon.

III – It is recognized that the Special Part of Penal Codes is the one that generates the greatest impact on public opinion, insofar as it translates into the selection of goods, interests and values that in a given society and at a given historical moment justify being protected by criminal law, being consequently elevated to the category of criminal legal interests. In the specific case of the Penal Code of Timor-Leste, the legislator sought to embody in the normative solutions found the options that the Constitution had previously enshrined as being the collective sentiment of Timorese society.

The systematization adopted in this part follows the history of the country and reflects the fundamental interests and values that built this young nation. It is therefore not surprising that the first title of this book protects, respectively, peace, humanity and freedom as primordial values of democratic societies, respecting the hierarchy of values that the Fundamental Law points to and taking into account the international obligations assumed by the Country by subscribing to and ratifying the Statute of the International Criminal Court. Title II enshrines crimes against persons, with particular protection of eminently personal legal interests, highlighting the protection of life, physical integrity, personal freedom, sexual freedom, and the protection of private life. It should be noted that voluntary termination of pregnancy is a punishable crime under the terms of article 145 of this Code.

In this title and with regard to crimes against physical integrity, the introduction of crimes of ill-treatment of minors and ill-treatment of spouse assumes particular relevance, fundamental typifications for the affirmation of the Rule of Law and for the protection of human rights in Timorese society.

The criminalization of slavery and trafficking in persons is also highlighted, the result of the humanistic conception that presided over the drafting of this Code.

Title III provides for crimes against life in democracy, highlighting crimes against public tranquility, State security and life in society, as well as electoral crimes and crimes against public authority.

In this title, and within the scope of crimes against life in society, the provision of specific types of crimes against the environment stands out, reflecting a growing concern of society in the preservation of natural resources and the protection of the environment, punishing unsustainably harmful conduct to fauna, flora, natural habitats.

The protection of property is enshrined in Title IV of this Book, where a normative system is constructed based on the legal types of crime most common in various criminal legislations, such as theft, robbery, breach of trust and damage. These types are defined as simple or aggravated, considering the circumstances of value, the nature of the thing appropriated, the means and forms of action, violence, as well as any other circumstances likely to significantly increase the guilt or unlawfulness of the agent.

Crimes against the administration of Justice and crimes committed in the exercise of public functions are listed in Titles V and VI, which enshrine the punishment of falsity of procedural acts, forms of obstruction of judicial activity, its non-exercise as denial of justice, as well as identifying, among others, acts of bribery, malfeasance by a magistrate or official and by a lawyer or public defender. Other activities of personal favoritism in the field of justice and the classic incriminations of slanderous denunciation, simulation of crime and non-reporting of crime are also criminalized.

In the exercise of public functions in general, the conduct of officials that falls under crimes of corruption, embezzlement, abuse of power or public force, or economic participation in business by those who hold public positions or functions are criminalized. In this domain, the Penal Code expands the concept of public official to include other analogous situations such as an official of international organizations, a foreign public official who carries out activity in the country, or a person who has been called to perform or participate in the performance of an activity comprised in the administrative or jurisdictional public function.

Title VII typifies crimes of falsification of documents, technical notations, currency and sealed or stamped securities, weights and measures, marks, stamps and seals, diversifying the respective punishments according to the nature, probative or fiduciary value and public use or destination of the falsified objects, providing for the possibility of seizure and forfeiture of objects intended for their practice.

Finally, in Title VIII, crimes against the economy are defined, criminalizing money laundering, following the most recent doctrine on the criminalization of anti-economic activities, tax fraud and situations of smuggling and customs evasion, with regard to customs or border issues. The criminal punishment of disobedience to the requisition of goods ordered by the Government is maintained, as well as conduct likely to disturb, harm or prevent the performance of certain public acts such as public tender or judicial auction.

It should be noted that, throughout this Diploma, an attempt is made to find a balance for the abstract criminal frameworks, taking into account the type of crime, its seriousness, the hierarchy of legally protected legal interests by each of the norms and the maximum limit enshrined for the prison sentence.

Also characteristic of the legislative options adopted by the Penal Code is the different treatment given to more serious crime where, in general, the legal type of crime provides only for a prison sentence.

In petty and medium-level crime, as a rule, the provision of the norm already allows the court, depending on the cases, to resort, alternatively, to the custodial penalty or the fine penalty, enshrining the criminal policy orientation of recognizing the fine as an autonomous penalty instead of complementary to the principal penalty.

The approval of this Code provides the Timorese State with yet another modern and adequate legal instrument for the provision of a public-criminal service of higher quality and respect for the fundamental rights of citizens, requiring continuous training of magistrates, public defenders, lawyers, court officials and other judicial actors who in their daily lives make these legal diplomas their working instruments, strengthening the national legal order and the Democratic Rule of Law.

## BOOK I

GENERAL PART

#### TITLE I

OF THE APPLICATION OF CRIMINAL LAW

##### SOLE CHAPTER

GENERAL PRINCIPLES

###### Article 1

Principle of legality

1. No action or omission may be qualified as a crime without a law prior to its commission defining it as a crime and establishing the respective penalty.

2. Security measures may only be applied to states of dangerousness whose prerequisites are defined in prior law.

###### Article 2

Prohibition of analogy

To qualify an action or omission as a crime, define a state of dangerousness or determine the respective legal consequences, recourse to analogy is not permitted.

###### Article 3

Application of criminal law in time

1. No one may be punished for an act defined as a crime at the time of its commission if a subsequent law ceases to consider it a crime.

2. In this case, if there has already been a conviction decision, even if it has become final, the execution and its criminal effects shall cease.

3. The law subsequent to the commission of the crime applies to prior conduct whenever it proves concretely more favorable to the agent and, in cases of final decisions, if any advantage can still be obtained.

###### Article 4

Exceptional or temporary law

Exceptional or temporary law continues to apply to acts committed during its validity even if the period of duration has elapsed or the circumstances that determined it have ceased.

###### Article 5

Moment of commission of the act

The act is considered committed at the moment of the action or omission, regardless of the moment at which the typical result occurs.

###### Article 6

Place of commission of the act

The act is considered committed in the place where, in any way, the action or omission occurred, in whole or in part, as well as where the typical result was produced or should have been produced.

###### Article 7

Principle of territoriality

Unless otherwise provided by international treaty or convention and whatever the nationality of the agent, Timorese criminal law is applicable to acts committed in the territory of Timor-Leste and on board ships or aircraft registered or flying the Timorese flag.

###### Article 8

Acts committed outside national territory

Unless otherwise provided by treaty or convention, Timorese criminal law is applicable to acts committed outside the territory of Timor-Leste in the following cases:

- a) When they constitute the crimes provided for in articles 196 to 206, 229 to 242 and 307 to 313;
- b) When they constitute the crimes provided for in articles 123 to 135, 161 to 169 and 175 to 178 provided that the agent is found in Timor-Leste and cannot be extradited or it is decided not to surrender them;
- c) When they are committed against Timorese provided that the agent habitually lives in Timor-Leste and is found here;
- d) When they are committed by Timorese or by foreigners against Timorese, provided that the agent is found in Timor-Leste, the acts are equally punishable under the legislation of the place where they were committed and constitute a crime that admits extradition and extradition cannot, in the case, be granted;
- e) If they are crimes that the Timorese State, by international convention or treaty, has undertaken to try.

###### Article 9

Restrictions on the application of Timorese law

1. Timorese criminal law is only applicable to acts committed outside national territory when the agent has not been tried with a final decision in the place where the act was committed or, having been tried, evades full or partial compliance with the sanction.

2. Even if, under the terms of the preceding paragraph, Timorese criminal law is applicable, the act is judged according to the law of the country where it was committed if this is concretely more favorable to the agent.

3. In the cases referred to in the preceding paragraph, the applicable sanction is converted to the one corresponding to it in the Timorese criminal system or, if there is no correspondence, to the one that Timorese law provides for the act.

4. In the case that the agent is tried in Timor-Leste, having previously been tried in the place where the act was committed, the penalty already served abroad shall be taken into account.

5. The regime provided for in paragraph 2 does not apply to the crimes identified in subparagraphs a) and b) of the preceding article.

###### Article 10

Subsidiary application

Unless otherwise provided, the provisions of this Code are applicable to acts punishable under special legislation.

#### TITLE II

OF CRIME

##### CHAPTER I

GENERAL PREREQUISITES

###### Article 11

Action and omission

1. When a legal type of crime includes a certain result, the act encompasses not only the action adequate to produce it but also the omission of the action adequate to avoid it, unless the intention of the law is otherwise.

2. The commission of a result by omission is only punishable when a legal duty falls upon the person who omits that personally obliges them to avoid that result.

3. In the case provided for in the preceding paragraph, the penalty may be extraordinarily mitigated.

###### Article 12

Criminal liability

1. Criminal liability for the offenses provided for in this Code lies with natural persons and is non-transferable.

2. Legal persons are only criminally liable for the offenses provided for in this Code or in special legislation when and under the conditions that the law expressly establishes.

###### Article 13

Liability for acting on behalf of another

Whoever acts as the holder of an organ of a legal person, or mere de facto association, or as a representative of another is punishable, even if the conditions, qualities or relations provided for in the respective type of crime are not present in them, but rather in the person represented.

###### Article 14

Subjective attribution

Only acts committed with intent, or in cases specially provided for in the law, with negligence, are punishable.

###### Article 15

Types of intent

1. Acts with intent whoever, representing an act that fulfills a type of crime, acts with the intention of carrying it out.

2. Also acts with intent whoever represents the realization of an act that fulfills a type of crime as a necessary consequence of their conduct.

3. When the realization of an act that fulfills a type of crime is represented as a possible consequence of the conduct, there is intent if the agent acts accepting that realization.

###### Article 16

Types of negligence

1. Acts with negligence whoever, by not proceeding with the care to which, according to the circumstances, they are obliged and capable:

- a) Represents as possible the realization of an act that fulfills a type of crime but acts without accepting that realization; or
- b) Does not even come to represent the possibility of realization of the act.
  - 2. The types of negligence referred to in the preceding paragraph take the form of gross negligence whenever the circumstances show that the agent acted with frivolity or recklessness, not observing the elementary duties of prudence that were required in the case.

###### Article 17

Error as to circumstances

1. Error as to the elements of fact or law of a legal type of crime, as well as error as to prohibitions whose knowledge is reasonably indispensable for the agent to become aware of the unlawfulness, excludes intent.

2. The regime provided for in the preceding paragraph includes error as to the existence of the prerequisites of a ground for exclusion of unlawfulness or guilt.

3. The punishability of negligent conduct is reserved whenever the law provides for it and the respective prerequisites are verified.

###### Article 18

Error as to unlawfulness

1. Ignorance of the law does not exclude the unlawfulness of the conduct that violates it.

2. Error as to the unlawfulness of an act, if inevitable, excludes guilt.

3. If the error as to unlawfulness is avoidable, the penalty may be extraordinarily mitigated.

###### Artigo 19º

Agravação pelo resultado

When the penalty applicable to an act is aggravated by reason of the production of a result, the aggravation is always conditioned on the possibility of imputing that result to the agent at least on grounds of negligence.

###### Artigo 20º

Inimputabilidade em razão da idade

1. Minors under 16 years of age are criminally non-imputable.

2. To young persons over 16 and under 21 years of age, the provisions of this statute apply in all matters not covered by autonomous legislation, with respect to the application and enforcement of criminal sanctions.

###### Artigo 21º

Inimputabilidade em razão de anomalia psíquica

1. A person is non-imputable who, by reason of a mental abnormality, is incapable, at the time of committing the act, of evaluating its unlawfulness or of acting in accordance with that evaluation.

2. A person may be declared non-imputable who, by reason of mental abnormality, has significantly diminished capacity to evaluate the unlawfulness at the time of committing the act or to act in accordance with that evaluation.

3. The proven incapacity of the agent to be influenced by penalties may constitute an indication of the situation provided for in the preceding paragraph.

4. Imputability is not excluded when the mental abnormality has been brought about by the agent with the intention of committing the act.

##### CAPÍTULO II

FORMAS DO CRIME

###### Artigo 22º

Actos preparatórios

Preparatory acts are not punishable, except where the law provides otherwise.

###### Artigo 23º

Tentativa

There is an attempt when the agent begins the execution of a crime he has decided to commit, performing part or all of the acts objectively adequate to produce the result and the result does not occur due to circumstances beyond his will.

###### Artigo 24º

Punibilidade da tentativa

1. An attempt is punishable only in intentional crimes for which the corresponding penalty is imprisonment with a maximum limit exceeding 3 years and in other cases expressly determined by law.

2. Unless otherwise provided, an attempt is punishable with the penalty corresponding to the completed crime, extraordinarily mitigated.

###### Artigo 25º

Tentativa não punível

An attempt is not punishable when the inadequacy of the means employed by the agent or the non-existence of the object essential to the completion of the crime is manifest.

###### Artigo 26º

Desistência voluntária

The attempt of one who voluntarily desists from continuing the execution of the crime, prevents the completion or occurrence of the result, or seriously endeavors to prevent either, is not punishable.

###### Artigo 27º

Casos de comparticipação

In cases of co-participation, the attempt of one who voluntarily desists from continuing the execution of the crime, prevents the completion or occurrence of the result, or seriously endeavors to prevent either, is not punishable, even if the other co-participants continue the execution of the act or complete it.

###### Artigo 28º

Arrependimento posterior

In crimes without violence or serious threat against persons, if the damage is repaired, the thing is returned or the situation is legalized before notification is made or the report or complaint is received, the penalty may be extraordinarily mitigated or, depending on the circumstances, the agent exempted from penalty.

##### CAPÍTULO III

AGENTES DO CRIME

###### Artigo 29º

Agentes

Participation in the commission of a crime may take the form of authorship, instigation or complicity, and there may be several co-participants in the same act.

###### Artigo 30º

Autoria

1. An author is one who executes the act, by himself or through another person, whom he uses as an instrument.

2. They are co-authors if, by tacit or express agreement, they take direct part in the execution or act in conjunction of efforts for the commission of the same crime.

###### Artigo 31º

Instigação

One who directly and intentionally induces another to commit a crime is punishable as an author, provided there is execution or commencement of execution of the crime.

###### Artigo 32º

Cumplicidade

1. One who intentionally assists another materially or morally to commit a crime is punishable as an accomplice.

2. The penalty corresponding to the type of unlawful act, extraordinarily mitigated, is applicable to the accomplice.

###### Artigo 33º

Culpa na comparticipação

Each co-participant is punished according to his culpability, regardless of the punishment or degree of culpability of the others.

###### Artigo 34º

Ilicitude na comparticipação

1. If the unlawfulness or degree of unlawfulness of the act depends on certain special qualities or relationships of the agent, it suffices that such qualities or relationships be present in any one of them, to make the respective penalty applicable to all co-participants, except if the intention of the incriminating norm is otherwise.

2. Whenever, by effect of the rule provided in the preceding paragraph, the application of a more severe penalty results for any of the co-participants, this may, considering the circumstances of the case, be replaced by that which would apply if such rule did not intervene.

##### CAPÍTULO IV

CONCURSOS E CRIMES CONTINUADOS

###### Artigo 35º

Concurso de crimes

1. The number of crimes is determined by the number of types of crimes actually committed, or by the number of times the same type is fulfilled by the conduct of the agent.

2. For the purposes of the following article, the case in which the agent, having committed a crime, commits another before having been convicted by a final judgment is considered concurrence.

###### Artigo 36º

Punição em caso de concurso

1. In case of concurrence of crimes, a single penalty is applied, the minimum limit of which corresponds to the highest of the penalties actually applied to the various crimes and the maximum limit to the material sum of the various partial penalties.

2. The maximum limit of the concurrence framework may not exceed 600 days for a fine or 30 years for imprisonment, even in cases where the material sum of the partial penalties exceeds that legal limit.

3. In determining the single penalty, the court considers jointly the facts and the personality of the agent.

###### Artigo 37º

Concurso de sanções

1. If the penalties applied are some fines and others imprisonment, their different nature is maintained.

2. Accessory penalties and security measures, even if provided for by only one of the applicable laws or in only one of the previous decisions, are maintained.

###### Artigo 38º

Pena de prisão com execução suspensa em cúmulo jurídico

Imprisonment with suspended execution may only be legally combined with other sentences of imprisonment when:

- a) It likewise concerns sentences of imprisonment suspended in their execution and the said combination does not prevent the continuation of the suspension regime of the single penalty;
- b) In the case of combination with effective imprisonment, there exist circumstances that determine the revocation of the suspension of execution of the penalty, regardless of the combination of penalties;
- c) The suspended penalties to be combined have different suspension periods or, being equal, are in different stages of compliance and the court establishes a single suspension period in accordance with the needs of prevention and the circumstances of the case.

###### Artigo 39º

Conhecimento superveniente do concurso

If, after a final judgment, but before the respective penalty is served, prescribed or extinguished, it becomes known that the agent was in one of the situations described in the previous articles, the rules set forth therein apply.

###### Artigo 40º

Crime e outra infracção

If the same act simultaneously constitutes a crime and an administrative offense, the agent is punished as a crime, without prejudice to the application of the accessory sanctions provided for the other offenses.

###### Artigo 41º

Continued crime

1. Unless dealing with crimes that protect eminently personal interests, the multiple commission of the same type of crime or of various types of crime that fundamentally protect the same legal interest, executed in an essentially homogeneous manner and within the framework of solicitation of the same external situation that considerably diminishes the guilt of the agent, constitutes a single continued crime.

2. The continued crime is punishable with the penalty applicable to the most serious conduct that forms part of the continuation.

###### Artigo 42º

Concurrence of norms

To the fact capable of being qualified as a crime, in whole or in part, by more than one legal provision, not dealing with the situations described in the previous articles, only one incriminating norm is applied according to the following rules:

- a) The special norm applies to the detriment of the general norm;
- b) The subsidiary norm prevails over the principal norm;
- c) The broader and more complex norm absorbs that which provides for facts subsumable within its provision.

##### CAPÍTULO V

GROUNDS FOR EXCLUSION

###### SECÇÃO I

GROUNDS FOR EXCLUSION OF UNLAWFULNESS

###### Artigo 43º

Exclusion of unlawfulness

1. The fact is not criminally punishable when its unlawfulness is excluded by the legal order considered in its entirety.

2. In particular, the fact committed in the exercise of a right or in the performance of a duty, in legitimate defence, in a justifying state of necessity or with consent is not unlawful.

###### Artigo 44º

Legitimate defence

The action of the agent necessary to repel an unlawful, imminent or current aggression against any legally protected interests of the agent or of a third party is considered legitimate defence.

###### Artigo 45º

State of necessity

The fact committed as an adequate means to avert a current danger that threatens legally protected interests of the agent or of a third party is not unlawful when the following requirements are met:

- a) There is a significant superiority of the interest to be safeguarded in relation to the interest sacrificed; and
- b) It is reasonable to impose on the injured party the sacrifice of their interest having regard to the nature or value of the interest in danger.

###### Artigo 46º

Conflict of duties

1. The conduct of a person who, in case of conflict in the performance of legal duties or legitimate orders of authority, satisfies a duty or order of equal or superior value to the duty or order sacrificed is not unlawful.

2. The duty of hierarchical obedience ceases when it leads to the commission of a crime.

###### Artigo 47º

Consent

1. Apart from cases specially provided for by law, consent excludes the unlawfulness of the fact when it refers to freely disposable legal interests and the fact does not offend good morals.

2. Consent may be expressed by any means that reflects a serious, free and informed will of the holder of the legally protected interest, and may be freely revoked until the execution of the fact.

3. Consent is only effective if given by someone over 16 years of age who possesses the necessary discernment to assess its meaning and scope at the time it is given.

4. If the consent is not known to the agent, the agent is punishable with the penalty applicable to an attempt.

5. Presumed consent, which occurs when the situation in which the agent acts reasonably allows one to suppose that the holder of the legally protected interest would have effectively consented to the fact, had they known the circumstances in which it is committed, is equivalent to actual consent.

###### SECÇÃO II

GROUNDS FOR EXCLUSION OF GUILT

###### Artigo 48º

Excess of legitimate defence

1. The means that, by their kind or degree of use, are excessive for the defensive action of the agent may determine the extraordinary mitigation of the penalty that would apply to the crime.

2. The excess of means used in legitimate defence due to non-culpable disturbance, fear or fright excludes the guilt of the agent.

###### Artigo 49º

Exculpatory state of necessity

1. A person who commits an unlawful fact adequate to avert a current danger, not removable by other means, that threatens the life, physical integrity, honour or freedom of the agent or of a third party acts without guilt, when it is not reasonable to demand from them, according to the circumstances of the case, different behaviour.

2. If the danger threatens legal interests different from those referred to in the previous number, and the remaining presuppositions mentioned therein are met, the penalty may be extraordinarily mitigated or, exceptionally, the agent may be exempted from the penalty.

###### Artigo 50º

Exculpatory improper obedience

The official who complies with an order without knowing that it leads to the commission of a crime acts without guilt, when this is not evident in the framework of the circumstances represented by them.

#### TÍTULO III

ON CIRCUMSTANCES

##### CAPÍTULO ÚNICO

GENERAL RULES

###### Artigo 51º

Determination of the measure of the penalty

1. The determination of the measure of the penalty, within the limits defined by law, is made according to the guilt of the agent and the requirements of prevention.

2. In the concrete determination of the penalty, the court considers all circumstances that, not forming part of the type of crime, speak in favour of the agent or against them.

3. In the sentence, the grounds for the measure of the penalty are expressly stated.

###### Artigo 52.º

General aggravating circumstances

1. All circumstances prior to, contemporaneous with, or subsequent to the crime are considered general aggravating circumstances of the agent's liability when, while not forming part of the legal definition of the crime, they reveal a greater degree of unlawfulness of the facts, of the conduct or of the agent's guilt, thereby increasing the need for punishment.

2. The following are among those capable of constituting general aggravating circumstances:

- a) Commission of the crime with disloyalty, it being understood that this occurs in cases of betrayal, ambush, lying in wait, or disguise
- b) The crime being committed against persons by employing means, methods or forms that directly or indirectly aim to ensure commission without the danger that might result from the victim's possible defence;
- c) The crime being committed by means of fraud, deceit, abuse of power or authority, or by taking advantage of the circumstances of place or time
- d) The crime being committed in exchange for payment or receipt of a sum or reward;
- e) The crime being motivated by racist reasons or any other discriminatory sentiment based on the victim's gender, ideology, religion or beliefs, ethnicity, nationality to which they belong, by reason of sex or sexual orientation, or illness or physical disability from which they suffer;
- f) The agent having a special duty not to commit the crime, to prevent its commission or to contribute to its punishment, or availing himself, for the commission of the crime, of public authority that he holds or invokes;
- g) Where not a case of recidivism, the agent having committed one or more crimes of the same nature in the 3 years prior to the moment of commission of the one for which he is being tried, regardless of the time of conviction;
- h) The crime being committed on the same occasion as another crime as a means of facilitating the commission of another crime or crimes;
- i) Commission of the crime being facilitated by the fact that the agent enters or attempts to enter the victim's residence or uses poison, flooding, fire, explosion, shipwreck or damage to a boat or weapon
- j) The commission of the crime or taking advantage of the consequences thereof being facilitated by the participation of two or more persons;
- k) Intentionally and inhumanly increasing the victim's suffering by causing suffering unnecessary for the consummation or any other acts of spoliation, cruelty or destruction also unnecessary for the commission of the crime;
- l) The victim being or having been the agent's spouse or being in a de facto relationship of identical nature, or being an ascendant, descendant, sibling, adoptee or adopter of the agent;
- m) The victim being a particularly vulnerable person by reason of age, illness or physical or mental disability, whenever such circumstance does not form part of the legal definition of the crime.

###### Artigo 53º

Recidivism

1. A person is punished as a recidivist who, alone or in any form of participation, commits an intentional crime that must be punished with effective imprisonment exceeding 6 months, after having been convicted by a final judgment to effective imprisonment exceeding 6 months for another intentional crime, if, according to the circumstances of the case, the agent is to be reproached for the prior conviction or convictions not having served as sufficient warning against crime.

2. If more than four years have elapsed between the commission of one crime and the other, recidivism does not occur, the time during which the agent has served a procedural measure, sentence or security measure involving deprivation of liberty not being counted in said period.

3. In the case of recidivism, the minimum limit of the penalty applicable to the crime is raised by one third and the maximum limit remains unchanged, the aggravation not being able to exceed the measure of the most severe penalty applied in the previous convictions.

###### Artigo 54º

Criminal habituality

1. When the agent commits an intentional crime to which an effective prison sentence exceeding 1 year must be applied in the specific case, with the following requirements occurring cumulatively:

- a) The agent having previously committed three or more intentional crimes to which an effective prison sentence has been applied;
- b) Less than three years having elapsed between each of the crimes and the next one;
- c) The joint evaluation of the facts and the agent's personality revealing a marked or dangerous tendency toward crime; the applicable penalty is that of the crime committed with the minimum and maximum limits being raised by one third.
  - 2. The provisions of this precept prevail over the rules governing the punishment of recidivism.

###### Artigo 55º

General mitigating circumstances

1. Circumstances that precede, accompany or occur after the commission of the crime and that speak in favour of the agent are considered general mitigating circumstances of the agent's liability.

2. The following are among those capable of constituting general mitigating circumstances:

- a) The grounds for exclusion referred to in the previous chapter, whenever not all of the requirements provided for the ground for exclusion to produce effects are met;
- b) The agent acting as a consequence of facts causing violent emotion, obsession or other passionate state of identical nature, or reacting in immediate response to provocation;
- c) The agent voluntarily presenting himself to the authorities before learning of the existence of criminal proceedings against him;
- d) The agent spontaneously confessing to the commission of the crime or contributing decisively to the establishment of the circumstances in which the criminal act occurred;
- e) The existence of acts demonstrating the agent's sincere repentance;
- f) The low intensity of the intent or negligence;
- g) Reconciliation between the victim and the agent.

###### Artigo 56º

Circumstances of extraordinary mitigation

1. In addition to the cases expressly provided for in the law, the penalty provided for in the legal definition of the crime is extraordinarily mitigated whenever there exist circumstances prior to, contemporaneous with or subsequent to the crime that jointly or individually markedly diminish the unlawfulness of the conduct, the agent's guilt or the need for punishment:

- 2. For the purposes of the provisions of the preceding paragraph, the following circumstances are considered among others:
  - a) The agent having acted under the influence of serious threat or under the ascendancy of a person on whom he depends or to whom he owes obedience;
  - b) The agent's conduct being determined by honourable motive, by strong solicitation or temptation of the victim himself or by unjust provocation or undeserved offense;
  - c) The agent repairing the damage caused or diminishing its effects, at any point in the proceedings but before the commencement of the trial hearing;
  - d) A long time having elapsed since the commission of the crime, with the agent maintaining good conduct;
  - e) The agent having sensibly diminished imputability.

###### Artigo 57º

Degrees of extraordinary mitigation

1. Whenever there is ground for extraordinary mitigation of the penalty, the following is observed with respect to the limits of the applicable penalty:

- a) The maximum limit of the prison sentence is reduced by one third;
- b) The minimum limit of the prison sentence is reduced to one fifth if equal to or exceeding 3 years and to the legal minimum if less;
- c) The maximum limit of the fine is reduced by one third and the minimum limit reduced to the legal minimum;
- d) If the maximum limit of the prison sentence does not exceed 3 years it may be replaced by a fine, within the general limits.
  - 2. The extraordinarily mitigated penalty that has been specifically fixed is susceptible to substitution, including suspension, under the general terms.

###### Artigo 58.º

Concurrence of circumstances

1. The occurrence of a circumstance modifying the abstract framework of the legal type determines the application of the extraordinary mitigation regime provided for in the previous article.

2. In the case of two or more circumstances modifying the abstract framework of the legal type concurring, only one is considered under the terms of the previous article, with the remaining functioning as circumstances of a general nature in determining the measure of the penalty.

#### TÍTULO IV

LEGAL CONSEQUENCES OF CRIME

##### CAPÍTULO I

GENERAL PROVISIONS

###### Artigo 59º

Penalties and security measures

1. The application of the death penalty or custodial sentence or security measure of a perpetual nature or of unlimited or indefinite duration is not permitted.

2. The simultaneous application of penalty and security measure depriving liberty for the commission of the same act is not permitted.

###### Artigo 60º

Limits of penalties and security measures

1. The concrete penalty can never exceed the measure of guilt.

2. The security measure is based on the dangerousness of the agent who committed an act defined as a crime and lasts until it is verified that the state of criminal dangerousness that gave rise to it has ceased, and may not have a duration exceeding the maximum limit of the penalty corresponding to the crime in question.

###### Artigo 61.º

Purpose of penalties and security measures

The application of penalties and security measures aims at the protection of legal interests essential to life in society and the reintegration of the agent therein.

###### Artigo 62º

Choice of penalty and security measure

1. If both custodial and non-custodial penalties are applicable to the crime as alternatives, the court gives preference to the latter whenever it adequately and sufficiently achieves the purposes of punishment.

2. In choosing the type of security measure to be applied to the agent whose dangerousness is procedurally established, consideration is given to their personality and the mode of treatment appropriate to the case.

###### Artigo 63º

Effects of penalties and security measures No penalty or security measure has, as a necessary effect, the loss of civil, professional or political rights.

###### Artigo 64º

Execution of custodial penalties or measures

1. The agent sentenced to an effective prison sentence or subject to an internment measure may benefit from the granting of conditional release or release on probation.

2. Unless otherwise provided, after serving five-sixths of the sentence, the convicted person is mandatorily placed on conditional release.

3. The granting of conditional release requires the consent of the convicted person, except in the circumstances described in the previous number.

4. The prerequisites and conditions for granting conditional release are established in specific legislation, as well as the rights and duties of inmates and the prerequisites and conditions under which an effective prison sentence may be executed in an open regime.

###### Artigo 65º

Concurrence of custodial penalties and measures

1. When the same agent has been sentenced to an effective prison sentence and subject to an internment measure, the latter is executed before the prison sentence and deducted from it.

2. As soon as the internment measure should cease, the court places the agent on conditional release if the time corresponding to half of the sentence has been served and the release proves compatible with the defense of the legal order and social peace.

##### CAPÍTULO II

PRISON SENTENCE

###### Artigo 66º

Duration of prison sentence

1. The prison sentence has a minimum duration of 30 days and a maximum of 25 years.

2. In cases specially provided for by law, the limit of the prison sentence is 30 years.

3. The maximum limit of the sentence referred to in the previous number can never be exceeded.

###### Artigo 67º

Substitution of prison by fine

1. The prison sentence applied in a measure not exceeding 1 year is replaced by an equal period of fine, up to the maximum legal limit, whenever the requirements of preventing future crimes do not impose the serving of the prison sentence and, in view of the circumstances of the case, the court considers that it should not suspend the execution.

2. Unjustified non-payment of the substitutive fine or any installment implies the immediate serving of the prison sentence initially fixed, which may be stayed by immediate payment of the entire fine or by indicating assets as guarantee, always deducting the installments that have been paid.

3. The court must give reasons for the decision not to make the substitution whenever the penalty applied permits it.

###### Artigo 68º

Suspension of execution of prison sentence

1. Whenever the prison sentence applied does not exceed 3 years, the court may suspend its execution for a period to be fixed between 1 and 5 years, from the date the decision becomes final.

2. The decision must contain the grounds that determine the suspension, namely, the personality of the agent, the circumstances in which the crime was committed, previous behavior, their living conditions and, very especially, the predictability of their future conduct.

3. The court must give reasons for the decision not to effect the suspension whenever the penalty applied permits it.

###### Artigo 69º

Suspension of prison conditional on duties

1. The court may condition the suspension of the execution of the prison sentence on the fulfillment of certain duties, not humiliating, intended to repair the harm of the crime.

2. The following duties may condition the suspension, namely:

- a) Reparation or guarantee of reparation for the damages caused by the crime within a specified period;
- b) Public apology to the victim;
- c) Performance of certain tasks connected with the crime committed;
- d) Delivery of an amount to the State or charitable institution with significance in the reintegration of the convicted person.
  - 3. The duties imposed may not in any case represent for the convicted person obligations whose fulfillment cannot be reasonably required of them
  - 4. The duties imposed may be modified until the end of the suspension period whenever relevant supervening circumstances occur or of which the court only subsequently becomes aware
  - 5. The provisions of number 2 of the previous article are correspondingly applicable.

###### Artigo 70º

Rules of conduct

1. The court may impose on the convicted person compliance, for the duration of the suspension, with rules of conduct intended to facilitate their reintegration into society, namely:

- a) Not to exercise certain professions;
- b) Not to frequent certain environments or places;
- c) Not to reside in certain places or regions;
- d) Not to accompany, lodge or receive certain persons;
- e) Not to frequent certain associations or not to participate in certain meetings;
- f) Not to have in their possession objects capable of facilitating the commission of crimes;
- g) To present themselves periodically before the court, the social reintegration officer or non-police entities.
  - 2. The court may also, having obtained the prior consent of the convicted person, determine their subjection to medical treatment or cure in an appropriate institution.
  - 3. The provisions of numbers 3 to 5 of the previous article are correspondingly applicable.

###### Artigo 71º

Suspensão da prisão com acompanhamento

1. When simple or conditional suspension of imprisonment is insufficient to guarantee the recovery of the offender and their distancing from criminal activities, the court may decree the suspension by subjecting the convicted person to supervision by social reintegration services for the duration of the suspension period, obtaining whenever possible the agreement of the convicted person.

2. It is incumbent upon the social reintegration services, jointly with the Public Prosecutor's Office and the sentencing judge, having heard the convicted person, to prepare the social readaptation plan which, approved by the court, shall be complied with the assistance of the aforementioned official or reintegration service.

3. The readaptation plan must include all duties to which the convicted person is subject, and the court may impose the duties and rules of conduct referred to in Articles 69 and 70 or other obligations relevant to the readaptation plan, namely:

- a) Obtain treatment or admission to appropriate establishments, whenever circumstances require it
- b) Respond to summons from the magistrate responsible for execution and the social reintegration officer
- c) Receive visits from the social reintegration officer and inform them about their means of subsistence
- d) Inform the social reintegration officer about changes of residence and employment, as well as any displacement from residence when exceeding 8 days
- e) Obtain prior authorization from the magistrate responsible for execution to travel abroad
  - 4. The provisions of paragraph 2 of

###### artigo 68º.

###### Artigo 72º

Modificação do regime de suspensão

If, during the suspension period of the execution of the prison sentence, the convicted person fails to comply with the imposed duties or is tried and convicted of another crime, the court, having regard to the circumstances, may alter the initially fixed regime of suspension of execution of the prison sentence, modify the duties or solemnly warn the convicted person, according to the circumstances of the case.

###### Artigo 73º

Revogação da suspensão

1. If, during the suspension period of the execution of the prison sentence, the convicted person is tried and convicted of another crime or deliberately relapses into non-compliance with the imposed rules or duties and modification of the suspension regime is not possible or proves insufficient, the court revokes the suspension.

2. The suspension of execution of the prison sentence is always revoked if, during the respective period, the convicted person commits an intentional crime for which they are punished with an effective prison sentence.

3. The revocation of suspension does not give the convicted person the right to demand restitution of payments made during and by reason of the suspension.

###### Artigo 74º

Extinção da pena de prisão

The non-revocation of the suspension of execution of the prison sentence determines the extinction of the sentence and its effects, once the suspension period has elapsed.

##### CAPÍTULO III

PENA DE MULTA

###### Artigo 75º

Duração da pena de multa

1. The fine penalty is fixed at a minimum of 10 and a maximum of 360 days, unless otherwise provided by law.

2. Each day-fine corresponds to an amount between half a dollar and 200 US dollars, which the court fixes according to the economic and financial situation of the convicted person and their personal burdens.

3. Whenever the circumstances of the case justify it, the court may authorize payment of the fine within a period of 1 year, or permit payment in installments, with the last installment not exceeding the limit of 2 years after the date the conviction becomes final.

###### Artigo 76º

Prisão alternativa à pena de multa

The decision that directly applies a fine penalty fixes imprisonment as an alternative, for the time corresponding to the fine, reduced to two thirds.

###### Artigo 77º

Redução ou isenção da pena de multa

1. If the person convicted to a fine penalty does not comply with the penalty due to circumstances, subsequent to the conviction, that make it impossible or difficult to comply with and are not attributable to them, the court may decree the reduction or exemption from the penalty.

2. The provisions of the previous paragraph apply to the fine penalty that substitutes imprisonment.

##### CAPÍTULO IV

PENA DE TRABALHO A FAVOR DA

COMUNIDADE

###### Artigo 78º

Trabalho a favor da comunidade

1. The community service penalty consists of unpaid provision of work in a public body or to other entities that the court deems to be of community interest, provided the consent of the convicted person is obtained.

2. The duration of the work that the convicted person must provide is fixed by the court by substituting each day of imprisonment fixed in the sentence with one hour of work, never exceeding 240 hours.

3. The work may be provided during or outside normal service hours, continuously or not, without exceeding per day what is permitted under the overtime regime and always in such a way that the survival of the convicted person or their family members is not affected.

4. Unjustified non-compliance with the provision of community service entails compliance with the initially applied penalty, discounting the days of work already provided, in accordance with paragraph 2.

###### Artigo 79º

Requisitos

1. The community service penalty is applicable by the court in substitution of the prison sentence to an extent not exceeding 1 year or of the fine penalty, whenever it concludes that by this means the purposes of punishment are adequately and sufficiently achieved and, in the case of imprisonment, there are reasons of crime prevention that advise against suspension of execution or substitution with a fine.

2. The application of community service always depends on the consent of the convicted person and, in the case of substitution of a fine, may be decreed in the sentence or in a subsequent order, provided that a request is filed by the convicted person before attachment is ordered in enforcement proceedings instituted for non-payment of the fine.

3. The provisions of paragraph 3 of

###### artigo 67.º.

###### Artigo 80º

Suspensão, redução ou isenção de pena

If the convicted person does not comply with the provision of work due to circumstances, subsequent to its decree, that make it impossible or difficult to comply with, not being attributable to them, the regime provided in Article 77 applies.

###### Artigo 81º

Legislação complementar

The other conditions for application of the community service penalty and for operation of the community service execution services are the subject of special legislation.

##### CAPÍTULO V

PENA DE ADMOESTAÇÃO

###### Artigo 82º

Admoestação

If the agent is considered guilty of the commission of a crime to which corresponds in the abstract a prison sentence not exceeding 3 years or a fine, the court may limit itself to admonishing them provided that, cumulatively:

- a) The damage caused by the criminal conduct has been repaired;
- b) They are a first-time offender;
- c) Crime prevention and the recovery of the offender are satisfied with the admonition.

###### Artigo 83º

Execução da pena de admoestação

Admonition consists of a solemn and appropriate oral reprimand to the convicted person, executed by the court in a public hearing, after the decision to be applied becomes final.

##### CAPÍTULO VI

PENAS ACESSÓRIAS

###### Artigo 84º

Princípio geral

1. The law may make correspond to certain crimes the prohibition of exercise of certain rights or professions.

2. Accessory penalties are cumulative with each other and can only be applied simultaneously with a principal penalty, their duration being fixed according to the measure of guilt.

###### Artigo 85º

Temporary suspension from the exercise of public functions

1. The court that sentences someone who holds a public office to actual imprisonment must decree the suspension from the exercise of that function for the period of serving the sentence, if the convicted person is not dismissed disciplinarily.

2. During the suspension period, the effects that according to the respective legislation accompany the disciplinary sanction of suspension from the exercise of functions are applicable to the convicted person.

3. The provisions of the preceding numbers are correspondingly applicable to professions or activities whose exercise depends on a public title, authorization or approval by public authority.

###### Artigo 86º

Prohibition from the exercise of function

1. The holder of public office, public official or agent of the Administration, who, in the exercise of the activity for which they were elected or appointed, is sentenced to imprisonment exceeding 3 years may be prohibited from the exercise of those functions for a period of 2 to 5 years when any of the following circumstances occurs:

- a) The act is committed with flagrant and serious abuse of the function or with manifest and serious violation of the duties inherent to the function;
- b) The agent reveals unworthiness or manifest incapacity for the exercise of the office;
- c) The nature of the act implies the loss of the trust necessary for the exercise of the function.
  - 2. The provisions of the preceding number are correspondingly applicable to professions or activities whose exercise depends on a public title or authorization or approval by public authority.
  - 3. The time during which the convicted person is deprived of liberty as a consequence of a coercive measure, penalty or security measure does not count towards the prohibition period.
  - 4. The accessory penalty provided for in this article does not apply when, for the same acts, the security measure provided for in article 100º is applied.
  - 5. The application of the provisions of this article requires the communication of the conviction from the court to the public authority on which the official depends.

###### Artigo 87º

Expulsion

1. A foreign citizen sentenced for the commission of a crime to which corresponds imprisonment exceeding 3 years may be expelled from the national territory if they have resided there for less than 15 years:

- a) For a period up to 2 years if resident for more than 10 years;
- b) For a period up to 5 years if resident for more than 5 and less than 10 years;
- c) For a period up to 10 years if resident for less than 5 years.
  - 2. The expulsion referred to in the preceding number applies when in the specific case it is required by reasons of internal security, public health or to prevent the continuation of criminal activity.
  - 3. The expulsion penalty is executed independently of the total or partial serving of the main penalty.

###### Artigo 88º

Prohibition from driving

1. Anyone who is punished may be subject to disqualification from driving motor vehicles for a period to be fixed between 3 months and 2 years:

- a) For a crime provided for in articles 207º to 209º;
- b) For a crime committed with the use of a motorized vehicle and whose execution has been significantly facilitated by it; or
- c) For a crime of disobedience committed by refusing to submit to the tests legally established for detection of driving a motorized vehicle under the effect of alcohol, narcotics, psychotropic substance or product with analogous effect disturbing physical, mental or psychological fitness.
  - 2. The provisions of paragraph 3 of

###### artigo 86º are correspondingly applicable.

###### Artigo 89º

Revocation of the weapon carrying license

1. In case of conviction for an intentional crime committed with the use of a weapon, the court may decree the revocation of the weapon use and carrying license, considering the circumstances and the seriousness of the conduct, for a period of 2 to 8 years.

2. The provisions of paragraph 3 of

###### artigo 86º are correspondingly applicable.

##### CAPÍTULO VII

DETERMINATION OF PENALTY

###### Artigo 90º

General principles

1. Whenever the law fixes a penalty, it refers to the crime in its consummated form.

2. The framework of the abstract penalty on which the specific measure of penalty is determined is obtained:

- a) By operating on the abstract penalty corresponding to the consummated crime the aggravating circumstances modifying recidivism and criminal habituality, provided for respectively in articles 53.º and 54.º;
- b) After complying with what the preceding paragraph provides or from the abstract penalty corresponding to the consummated crime if there are no modifying circumstances, situations of extraordinary mitigation are considered if, in the case, they exist.

###### Artigo 91º

Specific determination of the penalty

1. Having found the abstract framework of the penalty under the terms of the preceding article, the court assesses all circumstances that, not being part of the type nor having been valued under the terms of the provisions of the preceding article, aggravate or diminish the responsibility of the convicted person.

2. Based on the valuation of these latter circumstances, the court fixes the exact measure of the penalty it considers necessary for the protection of legal interests essential to life in society and the social reintegration of the agent, within the limits of the framework fixed in the type or the limits resulting from the application of the provisions of the preceding article.

3. The measure of the penalty applied to the convicted person cannot, under any circumstances, exceed the measure of guilt.

###### Artigo 92º

Special case of the fine penalty

In the case of the fine penalty, the provisions of this chapter are applicable to the determination of the duration of the fine, without prejudice to what paragraph 2 of article 75º provides for the calculation of the amount corresponding to each day of fine.

##### CAPÍTULO VIII

SECURITY MEASURES

###### SECÇÃO I

INTERNMENT MEASURE

###### Artigo 93º

Requirements

When an act described in a legal type of crime is committed by a person not criminally liable under the terms of the provisions of

###### artigo 21º, they may be ordered to be interned in

an adequate establishment, whenever by virtue of the psychic anomaly, the nature and seriousness of the act committed, the court has founded fear that they will commit other typical acts to which correspond crimes against persons or crimes of common danger.

###### Artigo 94º

Duration

1. If the act committed by the person not criminally liable is punishable with imprisonment up to 3 years, the internment cannot last more than 1 year.

2. If the act committed by the person not criminally liable corresponds to a crime against persons or a crime of common danger, punishable with imprisonment equal to or exceeding 5 years, the internment has a minimum duration of 3 years, unless the release proves compatible with the defense of public order and social peace.

3. If the act committed by the person not criminally liable corresponds to a crime punishable with a penalty exceeding 8 years and the danger of new acts of the same kind is so serious as to discourage release, the internment may be extended for successive periods of 2 years until it is verified that the state of criminal dangerousness that gave rise to it has ceased.

4. The internment cannot exceed the maximum limit of the penalty corresponding to the type of crime committed by the person not criminally liable.

###### Artigo 95º

Cessation of the measure

1. The measure ceases when the state of criminal dangerousness that originated it ends or, if it continues, when the maximum duration limit of the measure is reached, except in the circumstances referred to in paragraphs 3 and 4 of the preceding article.

2. The internment measure is mandatorily subject to review every 12 months.

3. If the existence of justifying cause for the cessation of internment is invoked, the court examines the matter at any time.

###### Artigo 96º

Substitution of the internment measure

1. The internment measure may be substituted by release on probation or by expulsion from the national territory when applied to foreigners.

2. In the case of expulsion from the national territory, the provisions of article 87º are correspondingly applicable.

###### Artigo 97º

Liberdade para prova

1. Se da revisão referida no artigo 95º resultar que há razões para esperar que a finalidade da medida possa ser alcançada em meio aberto, o tribunal coloca o internado em liberdade para prova.

2. O período de liberdade para prova é fixado entre um mínimo de 2 anos e um máximo de 5 anos, não podendo ultrapassar o limite máximo fixado no nº.4 do artigo 94º.

3. A decisão de liberdade para prova pode impor ao internado regras de conduta, necessárias à prevenção da perigosidade, em termos correspondentes aos referidos no

###### artigo 70º, bem como o dever de se submeter a

tratamentos e regimes de cura ambulatórios apropriados e de se prestar a exames e observações nos lugares que lhe forem indicados.

4. O agente a quem for suspensa a execução do internamento é colocado sob vigilância tutelar dos serviços de reinserção social.

5. Se não houver motivos que conduzam à revogação da liberdade para a prova, findo o tempo de duração desta, a medida de internamento é declarada extinta.

6. Se, findo o período de liberdade para a prova, se encontrar pendente processo ou incidente que possa conduzir à sua revogação, a medida é declarada extinta quando o processo ou o incidente findarem e não houver lugar à revogação.

###### Artigo 98º

Revogação da liberdade para prova

1. A liberdade para prova é revogada quando:

- a) O comportamento do inimputável revelar que o internamento é indispensável; ou
- b) O inimputável for condenado em pena privativa da liberdade e não se verificarem os pressupostos da suspensão da execução, nos termos do nº 1 do

###### artigo 68º.

2. A revogação determina o reinternamento, sendo correspondentemente aplicável o disposto nos artigo 94º e

95º.

###### Artigo 99º

Suspensão da execução do internamento

1. O tribunal pode determinar a suspensão do internamento se for razoável de se esperar que com a suspensão se alcance a finalidade da medida de segurança e a suspensão se revelar compatível com a defesa da ordem pública e da paz social.

2. Á suspensão do internamento é correspondentemente aplicável o disposto nos nº.3 e 4º do artigo 97º.

3. A suspensão da execução do internamento não pode ser decretada se o agente for simultaneamente condenado em pena privativa da liberdade e não se verificarem os pressupostos da suspensão da execução desta, nos termos do nº.1 do artigo 68º.

4. A duração e cessação da suspensão de internamento são determinadas nos termos dos artigos 94º e 95º, respectivamente.

5. Á revogação da decisão de suspensão da medida de internamento aplica-se o disposto no artigo 98º.

###### SECÇÃO II

OUTRAS MEDIDAS DE SEGURANÇA

###### Artigo 100º

Medida de interdição profissional

1. Quando um inimputável por anomalia psíquica praticar um acto previsto num tipo legal de crime relacionado com a actividade profissional que exerce e existir fundado receio de, enquanto mantiver essa ocupação, continuar a praticar factos idênticos, o tribunal pode proibi-lo do exercício da respectiva actividade por um período de 1 a 5 anos, atendendo às circunstâncias do caso e à personalidade do inimputável.

2. decurso do período de interdição suspende-se durante o período em que o agente estiver privado da liberdade por força de medida de coacção processual, pena ou medida de segurança.

###### Artigo 101º

Proibição de condução e cassação da licença de uso e porte de arma

1. O inimputável que praticar qualquer um dos actos previstos no nº.1 do artigo 88º pode ser sujeito à inibição de conduzir veículos a motor por um período de 2 a 6 anos, sempre que a personalidade deste gerar fundado receio de se vir a praticar novos factos da mesma espécie.

2. Caso o acto praticado pelo inimputável corresponder a um crime relacionado com a utilização de arma, o tribunal pode decretar a cassação da licença de uso e porte de arma por um período de 5 a 10 anos, sempre que a personalidade deste gerar fundado receio de se vir a praticar novos factos da mesma espécie.

3. É correspondentemente aplicável o disposto no n.º 3 do

###### artigo 86º.

##### CAPÍTULO IX

OUTRAS CONSEQUÊNCIAS DO CRIME

###### Artigo 102º

Perda dos objectos do crime

1. São declarados perdidos a favor do Estado os objectos que serviram ou estavam destinados a servir para a prática de um crime, ou que por estes foram produzidos, quando pela sua natureza ou pelas circunstâncias do caso ponham em perigo a segurança das pessoas ou a ordem pública, ou ofereçam sérios riscos de serem utilizados para o cometimento de novos crimes.

2. Ficam salvaguardados os direitos da vítima e de terceiros, que não tenham concorrido para a sua utilização ou produção, ou tirado vantagem do objecto de que sejam proprietários.

3. O tribunal fixa o destino dos objectos declarados perdidos sempre que a lei o não fizer, podendo ordenar a sua total ou parcial destruição ou coloca-los fora do comércio.

4. O disposto no n.º 1 tem lugar ainda que nenhuma pessoa determinada possa ser punida pelo facto.

###### Artigo 103º

Perda de vantagens

1. Todas as coisas, direitos ou vantagens adquiridas, de forma directa ou indirecta, em consequência da prática de um crime, são declarados perdidos a favor do Estado, sem prejuízo dos direitos da vítima ou de terceiros de boa fé.

2. Se as coisas, direitos ou vantagens não puderem ser apropriadas em espécie, a perda é substituída pelo pagamento ao Estado do respectivo valor.

###### Artigo 104º

Responsabilidade civil emergente de crime

1. A indemnização de perdas e danos emergentes de um crime é obrigatória e oficiosamente apurada e arbitrada pelo tribunal sempre que tiverem sido apurados e quantificados os danos, salvo se o lesado nos termos da lei processual penal declarar que pretende deduzir o pedido em separado.

2. Os pressupostos e o cálculo da indemnização regulamse pelas normas de direito civil.

3. O responsável pela indemnização pode efectuar transacção da mesma dando disso conhecimento ao tribunal, sob pena de ineficácia do acto.

###### Artigo 105º

Privilégio do crédito do lesado

O crédito decorrente do direito do lesado à indemnização por perdas e danos emergentes de crime beneficia de preferência relativamente a qualquer outro surgido após o cometimento do facto, incluindo as custas e a quantia relativa à multa.

#### TÍTULO V

DIREITO DE QUEIXA

###### Artigo 106º

Natureza do crime

1. Os crimes podem revestir a natureza pública ou semipública, para efeitos do exercício do direito de queixa.

2. São crimes públicos aqueles cujo procedimento criminal não depende de queixa

3. São crimes semi-públicos aqueles cujo procedimento só pode iniciar-se depois de exercido o direito de queixa.

4. O direito de queixa consiste na manifestação de vontade por parte do titular do mesmo de que pretende procedimento criminal.

###### Artigo 107º

Titular do direito de queixa

Quando o procedimento criminal depender de queixa, têm legitimidade para apresentá-la os titulares do direito indicados na lei processual penal.

###### Artigo 108º

Prazo para o exercício do direito

O prazo para o exercício do direito de queixa é de 6 meses e conta-se autonomamente para cada um dos titulares do direito de queixa.

###### Artigo 109º

Renúncia e desistência da queixa

A renúncia, a desistência ou o não exercício do direito de queixa relativamente a um dos comparticipantes do crime aproveita aos restantes, nos casos em que também estes não possam ser perseguidos sem queixa.

#### TÍTULO VI

EXTINÇÃO DA RESPONSABILIDADE CRIMINAL

##### CAPÍTULO I

PRESCRIÇÃO DO PROCEDIMENTO CRIMINAL

###### Artigo 110º

Limitation periods

1. Criminal proceedings are extinguished by limitation as soon as the following periods have elapsed since the commission of the crime:

- a) 20 years, when the crimes are punishable by imprisonment whose maximum limit exceeds 12 years;
- b) 15 years, when the crimes are punishable by imprisonment whose maximum limit exceeds 7 years but does not exceed 12 years;
- c) 8 years, when the crimes are punishable by imprisonment whose maximum limit exceeds 3 years but does not exceed 7 years;
- d) 4 years, in all other cases.
  - 2. When the law establishes for any crime, alternatively, imprisonment or a fine, only the former is considered for the purposes of this article.

###### Artigo 111º

Calculation of the period

1. The limitation period for criminal proceedings runs from the day on which the act was completed or from the day of the last act of execution when it concerns an uncompleted crime, continuing crime or habitual crime.

2. In permanent crimes the limitation period is counted from the day on which the completion ceases.
