uuu) In modern and democratic societies, justice and its exercise are interests justifying the intervention of criminal protection, and the punishment of falsity of procedural acts, forms of obstruction of jurisdictional activity, its non-exercise as denial of justice and violation of the respective secrecy should be established;
  - vvv) Include incriminations relating to acts of bribery, prevarication by magistrate or official and by lawyer or public defender, as well as other activities of personal favoritism in the domain of justice, without forgetting the classic incriminations of slanderous denunciation, simulation of crime and non-reporting; www) Beyond the references contained in the last two numbers, conducts should be criminalized that in the exercise of public functions translate into situations of corruption, embezzlement, abuse of power or public force or economic participation in business by those who exercise public offices or functions;
  - xxx) In an autonomous title, prevent through adequate incriminating norms, conducts of falsification of documents, technical notations, currency and sealed or stamped securities, weights and measures, marks, stamps and seals, diversifying the respective punishments according to the nature, the probative or fiduciary value and the public use or destination of the falsified objects and provide for the possibility of seizure and loss of objects intended for the commission of these crimes; yyy) Also in the same domain of the economy, incrimination should be made of acts that constitute tax fraud or situations of smuggling and concealment with regard to customs or border issues, beyond maintaining criminal punishment of disobedience to the requisition of goods ordered by the government and behaviors likely to disturb, harm or prevent the realization of some public acts such as public tender or judicial auction.

###### Article 3º

Coordination with the Code of Criminal Procedure

The entry into force of the Penal Code referred to in this legislative authorisation law must be coordinated with the Code of Criminal Procedure and other regulatory legislation on criminal matters that is in force, as required by the legal certainty and coherence of the respective Legal Order.

###### Article 4º

Duration and scope

The legislative authorisation granted by this law has a duration of 180 days, counted from the date of its entry into force.

###### Article 5º

Entry into force

This legal instrument enters into force on the day immediately following its publication.

Approved on 23 September 2008

The Acting President of the National Parliament, Maria da Paixão de Jesus da Costa

Promulgated on 7 October 2008.

Let it be published.

The President of the Republic, Dr. José Ramos Horta

Law 6/2009, of 15 July

(First amendment to the Penal Code approved by Decree-Law No. 19/2009, of 8 April)

Life, from the moment of conception of the human being, must be protected. Taking this principle as settled, it cannot be ignored that there are situations that may justify the termination of pregnancy. However, the gravity of the goods in conflict here, that is, the good (life of the pregnant woman) and the good (life of the foetus or embryo), both with the same value, only legitimises termination in extreme situations: when the sacrifice of one life is the only and last resort to save another and there is no way of maintaining both.

It is therefore important to establish that the termination of pregnancy, the final word on which must always be the mother's, who will decide in conscience, should only be permitted when it is the only means to prevent the death of the pregnant woman. Thus, the National Parliament decrees, pursuant to

###### article 92.°, paragraph 1 of article 95.º and article 98.º of the

Constitution of the Republic, to have force of law, the following:

###### Article 1.°

Amendment to the Penal Code

Article 141.º of the Penal Code approved by article 1.° of Decree-law No. 19/2009, of 8 April, annexed thereto, shall have the following wording:

"Article 141.º

4. The provisions of the preceding paragraphs shall not apply when the termination of pregnancy constitutes, in accordance with the state of medical knowledge and experience, and after all possible actions have been carried out to save the life of the pregnant woman and that of the foetus or embryo, the only means to remove danger of death to the pregnant woman, provided that it is carried out, upon medical certification, by a doctor or other health professional under his or her direction in a public or officially recognised health establishment, and with the consent of the pregnant woman.

5. The verification of the circumstances referred to in the preceding paragraph is certified in a medical certificate written and signed by a panel of three doctors, on a date prior to that of the termination, of which the doctor who performs or supervises the performance of the termination of pregnancy is not a member.

6. Consent is given in writing in a document signed by the pregnant woman – after hearing, if possible, the spouse or the person who lives with her in conditions analogous to those of spouses – or by another person on her behalf, whenever possible with two days' notice in relation to the date of the termination.

7. In the case where the pregnant woman is a minor, consent is given by the legal representative.

8. In the case where the pregnant woman who is of age or emancipated is or becomes psychologically incapable, consent is given, respectively and successively, by the spouse or the person who lives with her in conditions analogous to those of spouses, by the legal representative, by an ascendant or descendant, or, in their absence, by any relatives in the collateral line.

9. When it is not possible to obtain the medical certificate referred to in paragraph 5, and/or it is not possible to obtain consent pursuant to paragraphs 6 to 8, and the performance of the termination is urgent and cannot be postponed, the doctor decides in conscience in view of the situation, availing himself or herself, whenever possible, of the opinion of other doctors.

10. The panel referred to in paragraph 5 is composed of doctors possessing adequate knowledge for the assessment of the circumstances that justify the termination of pregnancy, of which, whenever possible, a gynaecologist/obstetrician is a member.

11. The doctors who issue the medical certificate and the doctors who perform or supervise the performance of the termination of pregnancy must provide all necessary information to the pregnant woman and, if applicable, to the persons referred to in paragraphs 7 and 8, namely the method of termination to be used, the effects of the termination, the possible consequences for the physical and psychological health of the pregnant woman.

12. Doctors and other health professionals are recognised as having the right to conscientious objection with regard to any acts relating to the termination of pregnancy.

13. Doctors and other health professionals who invoke conscientious objection must guarantee the immediate intervention in the necessary acts, as well as the immediate care of the pregnant woman, by another health professional.

14. Conscientious objection is expressed and communicated, through a written and signed document, to the clinical head of the health establishment where the objector provides service.

###### Article 2

Entry into force

This law enters into force on the day following its publication. Approved on May 26, 2009. The President of the National Parliament, Fernando La Sama de Araújo

Promulgated on July 3, 2009. Let it be published. The President of the Republic, Dr. José Ramos Horta

Law 13/2008, on October 13 (Legislative authorization in criminal matters)

Law 6/2009, on July 15 (Amends article 41 of the Penal Code)

Decree-Law 19/2009, on April 8

(Approves the Penal Code)

To establish the legal system, those politically responsible included the elaboration and approval of Timor-Leste's Penal Code in their legislative priorities, in order to guarantee the fundamental rights and freedoms that the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste enshrines. This legal instrument comes from work that a commission, with Timorese and international technicians, developed following governmental guidance and within the limits and content that were established in the law concerning legislative authorization that the National Parliament has already approved. The normative solution adopted here respects the social and cultural reality of the Timorese community and also receives suggestions that came from national and international organizations, contributions that came from various judicial operators working in Timor-Leste, and also teachings drawn from comparative law. One must look at this Code that is being approved now, not as a final point, but as a fundamental stage to establish the Timorese legal order, remaining open to future improvement that, in due course, the evolution of international law, judicial practice and the teaching of law advise. Thus, Pursuant to the legislative authorization received by article 1, no. 2 of Law 13/2009, on October 13, and pursuant to article 96 of the Constitution, the Government decrees, to have the force of law:

Article 1

Approval of the Penal Code

The Penal Code, which is annexed to and forms part of this Decree-Law, is now approved. Article 2

Repealing norm

1. The Penal Code of Indonesia, which was incorporated into the legal order by article 1 of Law 10/2003, is repealed. 2. The legal provisions that are written in separate legislation are repealed when

a) They foresee and punish facts incriminated in the Penal Code that is now being approved; b) They enshrine solutions contrary to solutions adopted in the general part of this Penal Code. Article 3

Coming into force

This instrument and this Penal Code come into force on the day following their publication. Approved in the Council of Ministers on March 18, 2009

Prime Minister

(Kai Rala Xanana Gusmão)

Minister of Justice

(Lúcia M. B. F. Lobato)

Promulgated on 03/30/2009

Let it be published. President of the Republic

(José Ramos Horta)

Annex

PENAL CODE

1. The restoration of the independence of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste and the approval of its Constitution, in 2002, created the need for this country to adopt its own modern legal order, which enshrines the fundamental rights that are included in constitutional precepts and reflects the country's social reality. We left the Penal Code of Indonesia in force because a State must have its own criminal law, but that code is not adequate to the country's new reality and, often, presents legal solutions contrary to constitutional principles that are now in force. Because the Timorese people have their own specificity and identity, their Penal Code must be made according to their own philosophy together with the principles and values that exist in modern societies, in order to respond to the demands that the country faces. A commission, with Timorese and international technicians, that the 1st Constitutional Government established, elaborated a draft for a Penal Code; but, although the Law for Legislative Authorization concerning criminal law was already approved, the draft did not receive promulgation until the end of the legislature. When 2008 began, with another executive, a new bill for legislative authorization to approve the Penal Code entered the National Parliament, work to review the draft of this Penal Code resumed, and the instrument was amended and entered into public discussion. Having received legislative authorization, the Council of Ministers of the 4th Constitutional Government was able to approve this Penal Code. II. Book I of the Penal Code constitutes its general part, which gathers the criminal principles that are enshrined in the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste and in international conventions, treaties and agreements that the Timorese legal order receives. Because this code is based on the rule of law and democracy, its general part enshrines principles concerning human dignity, respect for each citizen's freedom and the State's responsibility to intervene only in situations in which there is unsustainable injury to legal goods fundamental to life in society, with its right to punish the perpetrator and its social obligation to reintegrate that perpetrator into society. In a State under the rule of law, the principle of legality, the principle of guilt and the principle of humanity must also exist. The enshrinement of the principle of legality as one of the fundamental principles of Criminal Law, provided for in article 31 of the Constitution, determines that an action or omission can be considered a crime and, as a crime, give rise to punishment only in situations that the law foresees. To follow this principle, one may not apply analogy to qualify any conduct as a crime and the court may not use analogical interpretation to interpret norms of the Penal Code to qualify any fact as a crime, define state of dangerousness or determine penalty or security measure. The principle of non-retroactivity in criminal law, which prevents the retroactive application of criminal law, except when it favors the accused, is also a corollary of the principle of legality. The principle of humanity, enshrined in articles 29 and 32 of the Constitution, shows the way not to apply the death penalty and also penalties or security measures of a perpetual character or with unlimited or indefinite duration. The principle of necessity, principle of proportionality and principle of adequacy also show the way for the normative options that this Code follows and serve as a basis to apply each penalty and security measure, and each penalty and security measure shall be applied to protect legal goods essential to life in society and reinsert the perpetrator into society. The principle of guilt, as a presupposition to apply penalty, determines that there cannot be penalty without guilt (nulla poena sine culpa), and becomes a limitation on the State's power, because, thus, the measure of penalty cannot be heavier than the measure of guilt. The principle of guilt also shows the way for the regime of error concerning unlawfulness, the way to enshrine lack of criminal capacity due to age and due to psychic anomaly, which remove the perpetrator's criminal responsibility because of his lack of guilt. Concerning the legal consequences of punishable fact, we see that penalty must be executed with a pedagogical and resocializing perspective, and this code has many non-institutional means to apply sanctioning measures. When there is an alternative to apply penalty depriving or not depriving liberty, the court must give reasoned preference to the penalty that does not deprive liberty when that penalty adequately and sufficiently realizes the purpose of punishment and satisfies the need to carry out reproval and prevention of crime (article 62). Thus, preference will be given to applying alternative penalty, especially in situations concerning petty and medium criminality, with strong relevance to fine and community service, which are enshrined as the best means to ensure the offender's social reinsertion. Fine shall be fixed by day, so that, thus, it can be adapted to the perpetrator's guilt and economic condition, with variation in the value fixed for each day of fine according to the convicted person's economic and financial situation and personal burdens. To differentiate fine as principal penalty from fine that substitutes imprisonment, a separate rule is also established here to regulate the conversion of fine to imprisonment in situations where the perpetrator does not pay the fine. Concerning community service, as a non-institutional sanctioning measure, this Code only wants to clarify and systematize some fundamental aspects of its regime and leave its development and application in each case to autonomous law. Imprisonment must be applied only when it is seen that other penalties are not adequate to achieve the objectives of prevention and reproval. The minimum duration of imprisonment is established here at 30 days and its maximum duration at 25 years, with the possibility to raise that maximum limit to 30 years in cases that the law especially foresees. As a consequence that comes from the offender's social reinsertion, suspension of execution of imprisonment can be applied in cases where the measure of concrete penalty does not exceed 3 years and the need to carry out prevention in the future does not preclude that suspension. Suspension of execution of imprisonment can also be applied with the condition that the convicted person fulfill obligations or rules of certain conduct or with the obligation to enter supervision service for reinsertion. This Code establishes for those lacking criminal capacity due to psychic anomaly security measures, with limited temporal duration, namely, internment measure, when their dangerousness justifies it. Measures of a perpetual character cannot be applied, and that measure must stop when the dangerousness that justified the application ceases, and, for foreigners, expulsion from the country can be applied in place of that security measure. In defense of the values and legal goods that are fundamental to living in society, this Code distinguishes crimes that are of a public nature, which the State must protect directly, from those which, because they are not so serious, the person who has the right to file a complaint must first exercise that right, as the criminal procedural law establishes. Thus, crimes will be considered semi-public which, in the special part of the Penal Code, according to the description in their legal type, create the obligation to exercise a complaint. Concerning the extinction of criminal responsibility and the effects of that extinction, the general part of this Code fixes periods for prescription of criminal proceedings, penalties, security measures and accessory penalties and also defines situations that cause suspension of that period. But it establishes imprescriptibility for criminal proceedings and penalty concerning war crimes, crimes against peace and crimes against humanity and liberty. Finally, it also regulates other situations that cause extinction of criminal responsibility, such as the death of the perpetrator, amnesty and pardon. III. We know that the special part of Penal Codes is what creates the greatest impact on public opinion, because in that part one chooses the goods, interests and values that in each society and at each historical moment can receive the protection of criminal law and, therefore, can enter the category of criminal-legal goods. In this Penal Code of Timor-Leste, the legislator included in the normative solution that it adopted the options that the Constitution has already enshrined as the sentiment of Timorese society. The systematization adopted in this part comes from the history of this country and reflects the interests and values that build this young nation. That is why title I of this book protects, respectively, peace, humanity and liberty as primordial values of democratic societies, with the respect that must be given as values that the fundamental law holds in its hierarchy and also following the international obligations that this Country has already assumed when it subscribed and ratified the Statute of the International Criminal Court. Title II enshrines crimes against persons and protects especially legal goods that are eminently personal, such as physical integrity, personal liberty, sexual liberty and protection of private life. Article 145 of this Code enshrines voluntary interruption of pregnancy as a crime. In this chapter and concerning crimes against physical integrity, there is special relevance to crimes of ill-treatment of minor and ill-treatment of spouse, fundamental typification to affirm the rule of law and protect human rights in Timorese society. Also emphasized here is the criminalization of enslavement and trafficking in persons, which comes from the humanist conception that showed the way for the elaboration of this code. Title III gathers crimes against life in democracy, with emphasis on crimes against public tranquility, State security and life in society, and also electoral crimes and crimes against public authority. In this title and in crimes against life in society, special emphasis is given to the provision for specific typical crimes against the environment with punishment for conduct that causes unsustainable harm to fauna, flora and natural habitat; this shows that society's concern about preservation of natural resources and protection of the environment is already spreading. Title IV of this Book enshrines protection of property. Here the normative system gathers the legal types of crimes that are common in many criminal legislations, such as theft, robbery, breach of trust, damage. These types are defined as simple or aggravated, as circumstances such as the value and nature of the thing appropriated, the means and form of action, violence, or other circumstances that can increase the perpetrator's guilt or unlawfulness. Titles V and VI gather crimes against the realization of justice and those that the perpetrator commits when already holding public function; here enshrinement is given to punishment for falsity in procedural acts, various obstructions of jurisdictional activity, refusal to carry out that activity as denial of justice, and also shows, together with others, acts that constitute bribery and prevarication of magistrate, official, lawyer or public defender. It also criminalizes activity concerning personal favoritism in justice and classic incriminations such as slanderous denunciation, simulation of crime and omission of criminal participation. Concerning the exercise of public function, in general, conduct of officials that constitutes crimes of corruption, embezzlement, abuse of power or abuse of public force, or economic participation in business by persons who exercise public office or function is also criminalized. In this matter, the Penal Code broadens the concept of public official to include in that concept other analogous situations such as officials of international organizations, foreign public officials who exercise activity in Timor-Leste or persons who receive appointment to perform, alone or with others, activity that falls within the scope of public administrative or jurisdictional function. In title VII, crimes of falsifying document, technical notation, currency and sealed or stamped value, weight and measure, mark, die and stamp are typified, with various punishments, according to the nature of each falsified object, probative or fiduciary value and public use or destination, with provision concerning seizure and forfeiture of objects to be used to commit those crimes. Finally, in title VIII, crimes against the economy are typified, with punishment for money laundering, as the doctrine that recently arose concerning criminalization of anti-economic activity, tax fraud and smuggling and embezzlement in customs or border areas.maintains criminal punishment for disobedience to requisition made by the Government of goods, and also conducts that may disturb, prejudice or impede any public act, such as the holding of public tenders or judicial auctions. It should also be noted that, within this diploma, a path is sought to achieve balance for each abstract penal framework, according to the type and gravity of each crime, the hierarchy of legal interests protected by each norm and the maximum limit enshrined for imprisonment. In the legislative options that arise within this Penal Code, different treatment is given to the more serious criminality, for those where, as a general rule, the legal type of crime provides only for a prison sentence. In minor and medium criminality, as a general rule, the provision of the norm gives the court the alternative to, according to each case, use a custodial sentence or a fine, with the criminal policy orientation to recognize the fine as an autonomous penalty, not as a complementary penalty to the principal penalty. With the approval of this Code the Timorese State has another legal instrument, modern and adequate, to offer better quality public-criminal service and respect for the fundamental rights of citizens, magistrates, public defenders, lawyers, justice officials and also other judicial actors, who use these diplomas in their daily work, receive the obligation to undertake continuous training, the national legal order and the State according-to-law and according-to-democracy will flourish further. BOOK I

GENERAL PART

TITLE I

APPLICATION OF CRIMINAL LAW

SOLE CHAPTER

GENERAL PRINCIPLES

Article 1

Principle of legality

1. An action or omission cannot be considered a crime before the law that already existed when that action or omission occurred defines that action or omission as a crime and establishes the respective penalty10. 2. Security measures may only be applied to dangerous states whose prerequisites have already been defined by prior law. Article 2

Prohibition of analogy

10 Respective (adj) – Concerning. Criminal law defines the crime of homicide and the respective penalty = criminal law defines the crime of homicide and the penalty concerning that crime. Analogy11 shall not be used to qualify an action or omission as a crime, define a dangerous state or determine their legal consequences. Article 3

Application of criminal law in time

1. A person shall not be punished for an act that the law defines as a crime at the time they commit that act when a subsequent law states that the act is no longer a crime. 2. In this case, if there is already a conviction decision, even if final, the execution of that conviction and its penal effects must cease. 3. The law that existed when the crime occurred shall apply to prior conduct when, in the concrete case, that law favors the agent12 and, if there is already a final decision, it is still possible to derive some advantage from that law. Article 4

Exceptional or temporary law

Exceptional or temporary law shall continue to apply to acts committed during the time that law existed even if the time concerning its duration has already passed or the circumstances that determined that law have already ended. Article 5

Moment at which the act is committed

The agent shall be considered to have committed the crime at the moment the action or omission occurs, regardless of the moment at which the typical result of that action or omission appears. Article 6

11 (2) Analogy (n) - Similarity between a case that the law does not regulate (omission) and a case that the law regulates. Analogous = that which is close to the same (as another thing or person). Similarity; relation in which two things are close to the same. 12 (3) Agent (n) – Person who commits some action. Agent of a crime = Criminal; person who commits some crime. Place where the act is committed

The agent shall be considered to have committed the act in the place where the action or omission occurs, in whole or only in part, and also in the place where the typical result of that act appears or should rightfully appear. Article 7

Principle of territoriality

Except13 for other provisions in international treaties or conventions and regardless of the agent's nationality, Timorese criminal law applies to acts committed in the territory of Timor-Leste and within ships or aircraft with Timorese registration or flag. Article 8

Acts committed in non-national territory

Except for other provisions in international treaties or conventions, Timorese criminal law applies to acts committed in places that are not Timorese territory in the following situations:

- a) When the act constitutes a crime provided for in articles 196 to 206, 229 to 242 and 307 to 313; - b) When the act constitutes a crime provided for in articles 123 to 135, 165 to 169 and 175 to 178, provided the agent is found in Timor-Leste and they cannot be extradited or there is a decision stating they cannot be surrendered; - c) When the agent commits the act against a Timorese, provided that agent habitually resides in Timor-Leste and they are found in Timor-Leste; - d) When a Timorese commits the act or a foreigner commits the act against a Timorese, provided the agent can be found in Timor-Leste, the law in the place where the act occurred punishes that act and the act constitutes a crime that admits 13 (7) Except – With the exception of; except; minus; not included in the situation already described or described below. extradition and in that case there is no authorization for that extradition; - e) When the act constitutes a crime for which the Timorese State, according to international conventions or treaties, has an obligation to try. Article 9

Limitations on the application of Timorese law

1. Timorese criminal law may only be applied to acts committed in non-national territory when the agent has not yet received a final judgment in the place where they committed the act or, if they have already received one, they were acquitted and did not serve the sanction, in whole or in part. 2. Nevertheless, notwithstanding the previous number, if Timorese criminal law is to be applied, the act shall be judged according to the law of the country where the agent committed the act, when that law favors the agent in that case. 3. In cases provided for in the previous number, the applicable sanction shall be converted to its correspondent in the Timorese penal system or, if there is no correspondence, to that which Timorese criminal law provides for that act. 4. When the agent must receive judgment in Timor-Leste and they have already received judgment in the place where they committed the act, the penalty they have already served abroad must also be taken into account. 5. The regime provided for in no. 2 does not apply to crimes identified in the previous article, paragraphs a) and b). Article 10

Subsidiary application

Except for other provisions, the norms of this Code also apply to acts punished by special law. TITLE II

CRIME

CHAPTER I

GENERAL PREREQUISITES

Article 11

Action and omission

1. When a crime's legal type also includes a result, the criminal act encompasses action adequate to produce that result and also omission of action adequate to avoid that result, except when the law has a different intention. 2. Omission that produces some result shall only be punished when the omitter themselves has a legal obligation to avoid that result. 3. In cases written in the previous number, the penalty may receive extraordinary attenuation. Article 12

Criminal responsibility

1. Criminal responsibility for infractions written in this Code falls only on individual persons and cannot be transmitted to others. 2. Only when and under the conditions that the law expressly enshrines14 can collective persons have criminal responsibility for infractions provided in this Code or in special law. Article 13

Responsibility for acting in another's name

A person who acts as holder of an organ of a collective person or de facto association or as another person's representative shall be punished, even if they do not have and the person they represent has the condition, quality or relation that is provided for in the type of crime. Article 14

Subjective imputation

Only acts that the agent commits with intent or, in cases that the law specifically provides, with negligence may give rise to punishment. 14 (12) Enshrines (v) – Establishes; provides; shows; states; says; writes

Article 15

Types of intent

1. A person acts with intent who, knowing beforehand that an act fulfills a crime's type, acts with the intention to carry out that act. 2. A person also acts with intent who knows that their behavior will produce as a necessary consequence the act that fulfills a crime's type. 3. When the agent knows that their behavior may produce the act that fulfills a crime's type, there is intent when the agent acts nonetheless and agrees with that behavior. Article 16

Types of negligence

1. A person acts with negligence who, due to not having the care that, according to the circumstances, they must use and they can use:

- a) Knows that their behavior may produce the act that fulfills a crime's type but acts even without agreeing with that behavior; or
- b) Does not know that their behavior may carry out that act. - 2. The types of negligence referred to in the previous number become gross negligence when some circumstance shows that the agent acted with rashness15 or recklessness16, due to not following the basic care they must follow in that case. Article 17

Error concerning circumstances

1. Error concerning a factual element or legal element included in a crime's legal type and also error concerning a prohibition that, reasonably, the agent must

15 (16) Rashness (n) – Unreflectiveness; levity. A person acts with rashness when they do not think well before doing something

16 (16) Recklessness (n) – audacity; excessive bravery. A person acts with recklessness when they are not afraid because they do not think about the danger their behavior may produce know in order to take awareness of the unlawfulness excludes intent. 2. The regime provided in the previous number also includes error concerning the existence of prerequisites to exclude unlawfulness or prerequisites to exclude guilt. 3. The provisions of the previous numbers do not prevent the punishability of negligent conduct when the law provides for it and its prerequisites also exist. also

Article 18

Error concerning unlawfulness

1. Ignorance of the law does not exclude the unlawfulness of conduct that violates that law. 2. Error concerning the unlawfulness of the act, when it cannot be avoided, excludes guilt. 3. When error concerning unlawfulness can be avoided, the penalty may receive extraordinary attenuation. Article 19

Aggravation due to result

When the penalty applicable to an act must be aggravated according to the result that act produces, that aggravation exists only when that result can be imputed to the agent, at least, due to their negligence. Article 20

Non-imputability due to age

1. Persons who have not yet reached 16 years have penal non-imputability. 2. The provisions of this diploma shall also apply to young people who have already reached 16 years but have not yet reached 21 years

in situations that autonomous legislation does not regulate, concerning the application and execution of criminal sanctions17. Article 21

Non-imputability due to psychic anomaly

17 (20) Sanction (n) – Punishment; penalty

1. A person shall be considered non-imputable who, due to psychic anomaly, at the moment they commit the act cannot evaluate18 the unlawfulness of that act or make a decision according to that evaluation. 2. A person may receive declaration as non-imputable who, due to psychic anomaly, at the moment they commit the act, has the capacity to evaluate the unlawfulness of that act or to make a decision according to that evaluation sensibly diminished. 3. The agent's proven19 incapacity to be influenced by penalty may constitute an indication20 of the situation provided in the previous number. 4. The agent does not lose imputability when they themselves provoke the psychic anomaly to commit the act. CHAPTER II

FORMS OF CRIME

Article 22

Preparatory act

Preparatory acts shall not be punished, except for other legal provision. Article 23

Attempt

Attempt exists when the agent begins execution of a crime they have already decided to do, and does, all or only some, of the acts that can objectively produce its result and that result does not appear due only to circumstances unrelated to the agent's will. Article 24

Punishability of attempt

18 (21) Evaluate (v) – Measure; assess; know truly

19 (21) Proven (adj) – For which there is clear proof

20 (21) Indication (n) – Table; relation between two measures; value that shows a quantifiable reality or expresses numerically a frequency or level (frequency indication, cephalic index, intelligence index, etc.). 1. In intentional crimes that may give rise to a prison sentence with a maximum limit higher than 3 years and in cases that the law determines, attempt may give rise to punishment. 2. Except for other provision, attempt gives rise to the penalty that shall be applied to the consummated crime, with extraordinary attenuation. Article 25

Non-punishable attempt

Attempt shall not be punished when it can be clearly seen that the means the agent used have no aptitude or the object essential to the crime's consummation does not exist. Article 26

Voluntary desistance

The agent's attempt shall not be punished when they voluntarily desist from continuing the crime's execution, prevent the crime from being consummated or its result from appearing or make every effort to prevent the consummation or that result. Article 27

In participation

When there is participation, the agent's attempt does not give rise to penalty when they voluntarily prevent the crime from being consummated or that crime's result from appearing, or make every effort to prevent the consummation or that result, even if the other participants continue to execute or even consummate that act. Article 28

Subsequent repentance

In crimes without violence or serious threat against persons, the penalty shall receive extraordinary attenuation or, according to circumstances, the agent shall receive exemption from penalty, when the damage has been repaired, the thing has been restituted or the situation has been legalized before there is participation or the competent authority receives denunciation or complaint. CHAPTER III

AGENTS OF CRIME

Article 29

Agent

Participation in committing a crime may be as authorship, instigation or complicity and many persons may participate together in only one act

Article 30

Authorship

1. A person shall be considered author who executes the act themselves or uses another person to execute that act. 2. A person shall be considered co-author who, according to tacit or express agreement, takes direct part in the execution or acts together with another person, joining their effort with theirs, the act commits only one crime

Article 31

Instigation

A person shall be punished as author who convinces, directly and intentionally, another person to commit a crime, provided that crime is executed or its execution begins. Article 32

Complicity

1. A person shall be punished as accomplice who, intentionally, gives material or moral help to another person to commit some crime. 2. The penalty applied to the unlawful type shall be applied to the accomplice, with extraordinary attenuation. Article 33

Guilt in participation

Each participant shall be punished according to their guilt, regardless of the punishment of the other participants or to what extent the other participants are guilty. Article 34

Unlawfulness in participation

1. When the unlawfulness of an act or its degree of unlawfulness depends on the agent's quality or some special relation, namely when a participant has that quality or relation to give rise to applying the respective penalty to all participants, except when the incriminating21 norm has a different intention. 2. When, due to the rule that the previous number provides, a more serious penalty must be applied to some participant, according to the circumstances that gave rise to that case, another penalty may be put in place of that penalty which, without that rule, would apply to them. CHAPTER IV

CONCURRENCE AND CONTINUED CRIME

Article 35

Crime in concurrence

1. To know how many crimes the agent committed, one must count how many types of crime they have already committed or how many times their conduct fulfills only one crime's type. 2. For the provisions of the following article, the situation shall be considered concurrence where the agent, having committed one crime, commits another crime again before receiving a conviction with final decision. Article 36

Punishment in concurrence

1. When several crimes are in concurrence, only one penalty must be applied, whose minimum limit is the highest concrete penalty of all the concrete penalties applied to each crime and whose maximum limit is the material sum of all the partial penalties

2. In concurrence, the framework's maximum limit shall not exceed 600 days for fine penalty or 30 years for prison penalty, even in situations where the material sum of the partial penalties exceeds that legal limit. 3. To determine the single penalty, the court shall consider, together, the acts and the agent's personality. Article 37

Sanction in concurrence

1. When some penalties applied are fines and some are imprisonment, the nature of each penalty shall remain unchanged. 2. Accessory penalties and security measures, even if only one of the applicable laws or only one of the previous decisions provides them, shall be maintained. Article 38

Suspended sentence in legal cumulation

1. A suspended prison sentence may only be included in cumulation with other prison sentences when:

- a) All those penalties are suspended prison sentences and that cumulation does not prevent the single penalty's execution from continuing to be suspended; - b) If that cumulation is done with an effective prison sentence, there are circumstances that give rise to revoking the suspension of that imprisonment's execution, even without that cumulation; - c) The suspended sentences to be cumulated have different suspension periods, or, if those periods are the same, each one's compliance is in different phases, and the court establishes only one suspension period, according to the necessity of prevention and the circumstances that gave rise to that case Article 39 Concurrence with supervening knowledge When a decision has already obtained finality but, before that penalty receives compliance, prescription or extinction, it becomes known that the agent is in some situation that the previous articles describe, the rules written therein shall be applied. Article 40

Crime with other infraction

When an act constitutes both a crime and a regulatory offense at once, the agent shall be punished for the crime, but the accessory sanction provided for the other infractions shall also be applied. Article 41

Continued crime

1. A person who commits the same type of crime several times or commits different types of crime that fundamentally protect only one legal interest, with essentially homogeneous behavior and in only one external situation that considerably diminishes their guilt, commits only one continued crime, except22 for crimes that protect eminently23 personal interests. 2. The penalty that shall be applied to the most serious behavior of others included in that continuation shall be applied to the continued crime. Article 42

Several norms in concurrence

For an act that several legal provisions qualify, in whole or only in part, as a crime and that does not fall into the situations that the previous articles describe, only one incriminating norm shall be applied according to the following rules:

- a) The special norm shall be applied with prejudice to the general norm; 22 (41) Except (prep) – except; with the exception of; minus. Not included in the described situation. 23 (41) Eminently (adv) – Truly; indeed

b) Preference shall be given to the subsidiary norm over the principal norm; c) The broadest and most complex norm consumes the norm that provides for facts subsumable24 in its provision. CHAPTER V

CAUSES OF EXCLUSION

SECTION I

CAUSES THAT EXCLUDE UNLAWFULNESS

Article 43

Exclusion of unlawfulness

1. An act shall not give rise to punishment as a crime when the legal order considered as a totality has removed its unlawfulness. 2. Namely, acts are not unlawful that a person commits when exercising their right, in legitimate defense25, in justifying state of necessity or with consent concerning a freely available legal interest. Article 44

Legitimate defense

The agent's action necessary to prevent unlawful, imminent or already executing but not yet completed aggression against legal interests protected by the legal order of the agent or another person shall be considered legitimate defense. Article 45

Justifying state of necessity

An act that the agent commits as a means to prevent current danger threatening legally protected interests of that agent or another person is not unlawful, when the following requirements exist:

24 (42) Subsumable (adj) - that falls within (the legal provision). 25 In legitimate defense – To defend themselves or to defend another person. a) The interest to be protected has sensible26 superiority when compared with the sacrificed27 interest; and

b) It is reasonable to sacrifice the injured party's interest when seeing the nature or value of the interest currently facing danger. Article 46

Conflict of duties

1. The agent's behavior is not unlawful which, when there is conflict to comply with several legal duties or legitimate orders of authority, complies with the duty or order with value equal to or greater than the duty or order that they sacrifice. 2. The duty of hierarchical obedience ends when it gives rise to crime. Article 47

Consent

1. Together with cases that the law specifically provides, consent excludes the unlawfulness of an act when concerning a freely available legal interest and that act does not offend good morals. 2. Consent may be shown by any means that expresses the serious, free and enlightened will of the holder of the legally protected interest, and may be revoked according to that holder's desire until the act's execution. 3. Consent has efficacy only when it comes from a person over 16 years old and with the necessary discernment28 to measure the meaning and scope of that consent at the moment they give that consent. 4. When the agent does not know that consent exists, they shall be punished with the penalty applied to attempt. 26 (45) Sensible (adj) – That can be felt; that can be seen. The protected interest has sensible superiority when compared with the sacrificed interest = It can be seen that the protected interest is greater than the interest that has been sacrificed. 27 (45) Sacrificed (adj) – That has been sacrificed; that has been lost. 28 (47) Discernment (n) - Judgment; capacity to fully understand and measure or see the difference of each thing. Discern (v) = Understand fully; distinguish; judge; evaluate; appreciate; measure; see the difference of each thing

5. Presumed consent shall be equated29 with effective consent, which exists when the situation in which the agent acts gives rise to reasonably thinking that, if the holder of the legally protected interest knew the circumstances in which the agent commits that act, they would indeed give consent efficaciously to that act. SECTION II

CAUSES THAT EXCLUDE GUILT

Article 48

Excess in legitimate defense

1. Means that, due to their type and degree in their utilization, become excessive for the agent's action to defend themselves may give rise to extraordinary attenuation in the penalty that shall be applied for the crime. 2. Excess in the means used in legitimate defense due to disturbance, fear or non-censurable panic excludes30 the agent's guilt. Article 49

Exculpatory state of necessity

1. A person acts without guilt who commits an unlawful act adequate to prevent current danger threatening the life, physical integrity, honor or freedom of the agent or another person, when there is no other way to remove that danger and, according to the circumstances that gave rise to that case, it is not reasonable to demand other behavior from the agent. 2. When the danger threatens a legal interest that the previous article does not cover and the other prerequisites written therein all exist, the penalty may receive extraordinary attenuation or, exceptionally, the agent may receive exemption from penalty. Article 50

Exculpatory undue obedience

29 (47) Equate (v) – Consider the same as; give the same value as. 30 Excludes (v) – Removes (from a group); distances (from a group); cleanses

(from a group)

An official acts without guilt who complies with an order when they do not know that order makes them commit some crime and the circumstances they currently see do not clearly show that their behavior is a crime. TITLE III

CIRCUMSTANCES

SOLE CHAPTER

GENERAL RULES

Article 51

Measure of penalty

1. To determine the measure of penalty, according to the limits that the law defines, the agent's guilt and the necessity to prevent must be seen. 2. To determine the concrete penalty, the court sees all circumstances that do not fall within the crime's type and favor or disfavor the agent. 3. In the sentence, the basis for the penalty's measure shall be expressly referred to. Article 52

General aggravating circumstances

1. General circumstances that aggravate the agent's responsibility shall be considered prior, contemporaneous or subsequent circumstances that do not fall within the legal type and show that the unlawfulness of the act, the agent's conduct or the agent's guilt is stronger and increase the necessity of penalty. 2. The following may constitute general aggravating circumstances, together with others:

- a) The agent executes the crime with disloyalty, which exists when the agent uses betrayal, ambush, waiting, disguise; - b) The agent commits the crime against a person by means, manner or form that, directly or indirectly, guarantee that they will not face danger that may come from defense that the victim may present; - c) The agent commits the crime with fraud, deceit, excess of power or authority or takes advantage of circumstances concerning place, time to commit that crime; - d) The agent receives payment, value or some reward to commit that crime; - e) The agent commits that crime due to racist motivation or discriminatory sentiment concerning the victim's gender, ideology, religion, ethnicity, nationality, sex or sexual orientation, illness or physical diminishment; - f) The agent has a special obligation not to commit that crime, to prevent that crime or to participate in punishing that crime or uses public authority that they have or claim to have to commit that crime; - g) In cases where there is no recidivism, the agent commits one or more crimes of the same nature within the previous 3 years starting from the moment they commit the crime for which they currently face judgment, independently of the moment they receive conviction; - h) The agent commits that crime on the occasion that they commit another crime to more easily commit one or several crimes; - i) The agent commits that crime with facility because they enter or attempt to enter the victim's residence, use poison, flooding, fire, explosion, shipwreck or breakdown in a boat or weapon; - j) The participation of two or more persons gives facility to commit that crime or take advantage of that crime's consequences; - k) The agent increases with intention and inhumanly the victim's suffering, by producing suffering that is not necessary to consummate that crime or other acts concerning spoliation, cruelty or destruction that are not necessary to commit the crime; - l) The victim is the agent's spouse or person who lives with the agent as wife and husband at that time or previously, or their ascendant, descendant, sibling, adopted person or adopter; - m) The victim is an especially vulnerable31 person due to their age, illness or physical or psychic deficiency, when that circumstance does not fall within the legal type. Article 53

Recidivism

1. A person shall be punished as a recidivist who, having received conviction with final sentence to effective imprisonment higher than 6 months, alone or together with another person, in any manner, commits an intentional crime for which gives rise to effective imprisonment exceeding 6 months, when, according to the circumstances that gave rise to that case, the agent must receive censure32 because the conviction or convictions that have already fallen upon them were not enough to prevent them from committing another crime. 2. There is no recidivism when, from the moment the agent commits one crime until the moment they commit another crime and not counting in that period the time in which they are serving procedural measure, penalty or security measure that removes freedom, 4 years have already passed. 3. When there is recidivism, the minimum limit of the applicable penalty shall increase by one third, but its maximum limit does not change and that aggravation shall not be higher than the highest penalty already applied in the previous convictions. Article 54

Criminal habituality

1. When the agent commits an intentional crime that, concretely, gives rise to effective prison penalty higher than 1 year in the circumstances that follow cumulatively33:

- a) The agent has previously committed three or more intentional crimes that gave rise to effective prison penalty; 31 (52) Vulnerable (adj) – That cannot defend themselves; weak 32 (53) Censure (n) – Criticism; reproof. We censure someone when we consider that person did something wrong. 33 (54) Cumulatively (adv) – All together at once

b) From each of those crimes to the next crime, 3 years have not yet passed; c) Evaluation of the acts and the agent's personality together shows that the agent has a tendency to commit crimes; the penalty concerning the crime committed with its minimum and maximum limits increased by one third shall be applied. 2. This provision prevails over the rule of punishment for recidivism itself. Article 55

General attenuating circumstances

1. Circumstances that arise before the agent commits the crime, arise together with the crime or arise after the crime and favor the agent shall be considered general attenuating circumstances for the agent's responsibility. 2. The following circumstances may become general attenuating circumstances, together with others:

- a) The causes of exclusion written in the previous chapter when some of the requirements provided for the causes of exclusion to produce effect do not exist; - b) The agent commits the act because something produces violent emotion, obsession or passionate state of nature similar to these or immediately reacts to provocation; - c) The agent presents themselves to authority before knowing that there is criminal procedure against them; - d) The agent confesses the crime they committed themselves or gives decisive34 contribution to knowing the circumstances that gave rise to that criminal action; - e) There are acts showing that the agent is already repentant; - f) The agent's intent or negligence is slight; - g) The victim and agent have already reconciled. Article 56

Circumstances of extraordinary attenuation 34 (55) Decisive (adj) – Strong; important

1. Together with cases that the law expressly provides, the penalty provided in the legal type shall be extraordinarily attenuated when there are circumstances that arise before the agent commits the crime, arise together with the crime or arise after the crime and, together or only alone, markedly35 diminish the conduct's unlawfulness, the agent's guilt or the penalty's necessity. 2. For the previous number's effect, the following circumstances shall be considered, together with others:

- a) The agent acts due to serious threat or due to being influenced by a person on whom they depend or must obey; - b) The agent acts due to honorable motive, because the victim themselves asked or tempted them or due to unjust provocation or offense they did not deserve; - c) The agent repairs the prejudice they produced or reduces that prejudice's effect, at some moment but before judgment begins; - d) The crime occurred already long ago and the agent continues to have good behavior; - e) The agent has sensibly diminished imputability. Article 57

Degree of extraordinary attenuation

1. When there is extraordinary attenuation of the penalty, the following rules shall be applied to the applicable penalty's limits:

- a) The maximum limit of prison penalty shall decrease by one third; - b) The minimum limit of prison penalty shall decrease by one fifth when equal to or higher than 3 years and decrease to the legal minimum when shorter than 3 years; - c) The maximum limit of fine penalty shall decrease by one third and its minimum limit shall decrease to the legal minimum; - d) When the maximum limit of prison penalty is not higher than 3 years, a fine penalty may be put in place of the prison penalty, within the general limits. - 2. The concrete penalty resulting from extraordinary attenuation may receive substitution, together with suspension, according to general rules.35 (56) Asentuadamente (adv) – Maka'as

Article 58

Concurrence of circumstances

1. When any circumstance that modifies the abstract framework of the legal type occurs, the regime of extraordinary mitigation written in the previous article shall apply. 2. When two or more circumstances that modify the abstract framework of the legal type occur together, only one shall be considered pursuant to the provisions of the previous article, the others shall be considered as general mitigating circumstances to determine the measure of the penalty. TITLE IV

LEGAL CONSEQUENCES OF CRIME

CHAPTER I

GENERAL PROVISIONS

Article 59

Penalty and security measure

1. The death penalty or penalties that deprive liberty or security measures of a perpetual character or unlimited or indefinite duration shall not be applied. 2. For a single act, a penalty together with a security measure that deprives liberty shall not be applied. Article 60

Limits of penalties and security measures

1. The concrete penalty shall not exceed the seriousness of the guilt. 2. Security measures are based on the dangerousness of the agent who committed the act provided for as a crime, and shall last until it is seen that the criminal dangerousness that gave rise to the measure has ceased, and its duration shall not exceed the maximum limit of the penalty applicable to the referred crime. Article 61

Purpose of penalties and security measures36

36 (61) Finalidade (s) - objetivu

Penalties and security measures shall be applied to protect legal goods essential to life in society and to obtain the reintegration of the agent into society. Article 62

Choice of penalty and security measure

1. When either a penalty that deprives liberty or a penalty that does not deprive liberty may be applied to a crime, the tribunal shall choose the latter when that penalty is better and sufficient to achieve the purpose of the penalty. 2. To choose a security measure to apply to an agent whose dangerousness has already been demonstrated in the process, one must consider the personality of that agent and which treatment is best for that situation. Article 63

Effects of penalties and security measures

A penalty or security measure shall not, as a necessary effect, extinguish civil, professional or political rights. Article 64

Execution of penalties or measures that deprive liberty

1. An agent who receives a sentence of effective imprisonment or is subjected to a measure of internment may obtain conditional release or release on probation. 2. Save for other provisions, conditional release must be granted to a convict who has already served two-fifths of the penalty. 3. There must be consent of the convict to grant conditional release to them, except in the situation provided for in the previous number. 4. Only the law shall establish the prerequisites and conditions for granting conditional release and also the rights and obligations of the releasees and the prerequisites and conditions for executing effective imprisonment in an open regime. Article 65

Concurrence of penalties and measures that deprive liberty

1. When an agent receives a sentence of effective imprisonment and is subjected to a measure of internment, the measure must be executed first and deducted from the imprisonment. 2. Should the measure of internment end, the tribunal shall place the agent on conditional release when they have already served the time corresponding to a portion of the penalty and their release appears compatible37 with the defense of the legal order and social peace. CHAPTER II

IMPRISONMENT PENALTY

Article 66

Duration of imprisonment penalty

1. The imprisonment penalty must last at least 30 days and shall not exceed 25 years. 2. In cases that the law specially provides, the imprisonment penalty may last up to 30 years. 3. The maximum limit of the penalty referred to in the previous number shall not be exceeded. Article 67

Substitution of imprisonment with fine

1. In place of an applied imprisonment penalty not exceeding 1 year, a fine may be imposed for the same period, up to the maximum legal limit, when the need to prevent future crime does not require the serving of imprisonment and, according to the circumstances surrounding the case, the tribunal considers that the execution should not be suspended. 2. When the convict does not pay the substitute fine or any installment thereof, they must immediately serve the imprisonment penalty that was fixed initially, but the convict may immediately pay the fine in full or present property to guarantee

37 (65) - Kompativel; ne'e fó-malu di'ak ho (buat seluk); ne'ebé la prejudika (buat seluk). Kompativel ho paz sosiál nia defeza = la prejudika paz sosiál nia defeza payment thereof to stop the serving, having deducted the installments already paid. 3. The tribunal must justify its decision not to make substitution when the applied penalty gives rise to such substitution. Article 68

Suspension of the execution of imprisonment

1. The tribunal may suspend the execution of an applied imprisonment penalty not exceeding 3 years, for a period from 1 year to 5 years, beginning on the date of the decision's final judgment. 2. The grounds for suspension must be included in the decision, namely, the personality of the agent, the circumstances surrounding the commission of the crime, their previous conduct, conditions regarding their life and, especially, the predictability regarding their future conduct. 3. The tribunal must justify its decision not to suspend the execution when the applied penalty gives rise to such suspension. Article 69

Suspension of imprisonment with duties

1. To suspend the execution of the imprisonment penalty, the tribunal, as a condition, may order the convict to fulfill certain duties, not humiliating, to repair the harm of the crime. 2. The following duties may be conditions for suspension: a) The convict shall repair or give guarantee to repair the harm caused by the crime, within a determined period; b) The convict shall present apologies to the victim in public; c) The convict shall perform some task related to the crime committed; d) The convict shall deliver money to the State or to a charitable institution with significance for the convict's reintegration. 3. The convict shall not be subjected to an obligation that orders them to do something that reasonably38 cannot be asked of them to do. 4. Until the end of the suspension period, the obligation applied to the convict may be changed when any relevant supervening circumstance occurs or has passed that the tribunal only now becomes aware of. 5. The provisions of n. 2 of the previous article also apply here. Article 70

Rules of conduct

1. The tribunal may order the convict to comply, during the suspension period, with rules of conduct that facilitate their integration into society, namely:

- a) Not to practice certain professions; - b) Not to frequent certain environments or places; - c) Not to reside in certain places or regions; - d) Not to associate with certain persons, allow certain persons to reside in their home or receive certain persons; - e) Not to frequent certain associations or participate in certain meetings; - f) Not to possess objects that may facilitate the commission of crime; - g) To present themselves to the tribunal, social reintegration technicians or non-police entities periodically. - 2. Having obtained the consent of the convict, the tribunal may also order them to undergo medical treatment or cure in an adequate institution. - 3. The provisions of n. 3 to 5 of the previous article also apply here. Article 71

Suspension of imprisonment with supervision

1. When simple or conditional suspension of imprisonment is not sufficient to guarantee the offender's recovery and deter them from criminal activity, the tribunal may apply suspension together with supervision by the service for 38 (69) Razoavelmente (adv) – tuir razaun; tuir justisa. social reintegration to the convict during the suspension period, having obtained the consent of the convict, where possible. 2. The service for social reintegration together with the Public Prosecutor and the judge who gave the sentence shall, having heard the convict, elaborate a plan for social reintegration, which, having obtained approval from the tribunal, shall be fulfilled with the assistance of an official or the service for reintegration. 3. All obligations applied to the convict must be included in the plan for readaptation; the tribunal may order the convict to comply with the obligations and rules of conduct written in articles 69 and 70 or other obligations that are of interest to the plan for readaptation, namely:

- a) To undergo treatment or internment in an adequate establishment, when circumstances require; - b) To comply with the summons of the magistrate responsible for execution or social reintegration technicians; - c) To receive visits from social reintegration technicians and provide information to those technicians regarding their means of living; - d) To notify the social reintegration technicians regarding changes to their residence or employment, and also regarding absences from their residence exceeding 8 days; - e) To obtain prior authorization from the magistrate responsible for execution before leaving for abroad. - 4. The provisions of article 68, n. 2, also apply here. Article 72

Modification of the suspension regime

When during the period that the execution of the imprisonment penalty is suspended the convict does not fulfill the obligations applied to them or receives judgment and sentence for another crime, the tribunal, having regard to the circumstances of the case, may alter the regime that was fixed for the suspension of the execution of the imprisonment penalty, modify the obligations or give a solemn warning to the convict, according to the circumstances of each case. Article 73

Revocation of suspension

1. When during the period that the execution of the imprisonment penalty is suspended the convict receives judgment and sentence for another crime or intentionally relapses into non-compliance with the rules or obligations applied to them, and it is seen that modification of the suspension regime cannot be made or such modification is insufficient, the tribunal shall revoke the suspension. 2. The suspension of the execution of the imprisonment penalty must be revoked when, during the respective period, the convict commits an intentional crime for which they receive a sentence of effective imprisonment. 3. The revocation of suspension does not give the convict the right to receive back the performance they already made during the suspension and because of the suspension. Article 74

Extinction of imprisonment penalty

When the suspension of the execution of the imprisonment penalty is not revoked, and the respective period has passed, the penalty and its effects shall be extinguished.

CHAPTER III

FINE PENALTY

Article 75

Duration of fine penalty

1. The fine penalty shall be fixed with a minimum of 10 days and a maximum of 360 days, save for other provisions. 2. One day of fine corresponds to an amount, which the tribunal fixes according to the economic and financial situation of the convict and their personal obligations, within the range of one United States dollar to 200 United States dollars. 3. When the circumstances surrounding the case justify, the tribunal may authorize payment of the fine within a period of 1 year, or allow payment by installments39, but the last installment must be paid within 2 years beginning on the day the sentence became final. Article 76

Imprisonment alternative to fine penalty

A decision that directly applies a fine penalty shall also fix alternative imprisonment, for the period corresponding to the fine, reduced by two-thirds. Article 77

Reduction or exemption of fine penalty

1. When a convict sentenced to a fine penalty does not fulfill the penalty due to circumstances that occurred after the sentence and that make impossible or difficult the fulfillment of the fine, but not imputable to them, the tribunal may decree reduction or exemption from the penalty. 2. The provisions of the previous number also apply to a fine penalty that substitutes imprisonment. CHAPTER IV

COMMUNITY SERVICE PENALTY

Article 78

Community service

1. The community service penalty is one in which the convict must perform free service for a public body or other entity that the tribunal considers to be of public interest, provided that the consent of the convict is obtained. 2. The tribunal shall fix the time for the convict to perform the service and, for this purpose, shall convert the imprisonment fixed in the sentence at one day for one hour of service, but not exceeding 240 hours. 3. The work may be performed within normal working hours or not, continuously or not, but on any day shall 39 (75) Prestasaun (s) – kuantia ida-idak ne'ebé tenke selu iha prazu ida nia laran hodi selu tusan ka enkargu (obrigasaun ka onus) ida. not exceed the time established in the regime for overtime and shall not prejudice the survival40 of the convict or the convict's family. 4. Unjustified non-compliance with the community service gives rise to serving the penalty applied initially, having deducted the days on which the work was already performed, pursuant to the provisions of n. 2. Article 79

Requirement

1. The tribunal may apply the community service penalty in place of an imprisonment penalty not exceeding 1 year or a fine penalty when it sees that by this means the purpose of punishment can be achieved adequately and sufficiently and, regarding imprisonment, reasons of criminal prevention indicate that it is better not to suspend execution or substitute with a fine. 2. There must be consent of the convict to apply community service, which, regarding a substitute fine, may be decreed in the sentence or in a subsequent order, provided that the convict presents a request before there is an order of seizure in the execution process for non-payment of the fine. 3. The provisions of article 67, n. 3, shall apply here. Article 80

Suspension, reduction or exemption of penalty

When the convict does not fulfill the work, due to circumstances that occurred after its application and that make impossible or difficult the fulfillment and are not imputable to them, the regime provided for in article 77 shall apply. Article 81

Complementary legislation

40 (78) Sobrevivénsia (s) – kondisaun materiál mínima ne'ebé presiza para atu moris. A special statute shall regulate the other conditions for applying the community service penalty and the functioning of the service to execute the work. CHAPTER V

ADMONISHMENT PENALTY

Article 82

Admonishment

When considering the agent guilty of committing a crime that gives rise to an abstract imprisonment penalty not exceeding 3 years or a fine, the tribunal may give only an admonishment to them, when, cumulatively:

- a) The harm of the criminal conduct has already been repaired; - b) The agent is a first-time offender; - c) The admonishment is sufficient to achieve criminal prevention and to rehabilitate the offender. Article 83

Execution of admonishment penalty

The tribunal shall perform the admonishment, with a solemn and adequate oral reprimand to the convict, in a public hearing, when the decision applying the penalty has already become final. CHAPTER VI

ACCESSORY PENALTY

Article 84

General principle

1. The law may establish for certain crimes prohibitions to exercise certain rights or professions. 2. One accessory penalty shall be cumulated with other accessory penalties, must be applied together with the principal penalty and its duration must be fixed according to the seriousness of the guilt. Article 85

Temporary suspension from exercising public function

1. When sentencing a person who exercises public office with effective imprisonment, the tribunal must decree suspension from exercising that function during the period they serve the penalty, when the convict does not receive disciplinary dismissal. 2. The effects that, according to the respective law, accompany the disciplinary sanction of suspension from exercising function shall apply to the convict during the suspension period. 3. The provisions of the previous numbers shall apply to professions or activities for whose exercise a public title, authorization or approval from public authority is required. Article 86

Prohibition from exercising function

1. The holder of a public office, public official or agent of the Administration who, while exercising the activity for which they were elected or appointed, receives a sentence with an imprisonment penalty exceeding 3 years may be prohibited from exercising those functions, for a period from 2 years to 5 years, when the following circumstances occur:

- a) The agent committed the act with flagrant and serious abuse of their function or with manifest and serious violation of the duties they must fulfill in that function; - b) The agent demonstrates unworthiness or manifest incapacity to exercise the office; - c) The nature of the act destroys the trust that must exist to exercise the function. - 2. The provisions of the previous number also apply to professions or activities for whose exercise a person must have a public title, authorization or approval from public authority. - 3. The time in which the accused is deprived of their liberty due to a coercive measure, penalty or security measure shall not count as a period of prohibition. - 4. The accessory penalty provided for in this article shall not apply when, due to those very acts, the security measure provided for in article 100 is already applied. - 5. When the provisions of this article apply, the tribunal's sentence must be notified to the public authority upon whom the official depends. Article 87 Expulsion

1. A foreign citizen who receives a sentence for committing a crime that gives rise to imprisonment exceeding 3 years and has not yet resided in Timor-Leste for 15 years may be expelled from the national territory:

- a) For a period up to 2 years, when they have resided here for more than 10 years; - b) For a period up to 5 years, when they have resided here for more than 5 years but not yet 10 years; - c) For a period up to 10 years, when they have not yet resided here for 5 years. - 2. The expulsion referred to in the previous number shall apply when in the concrete case it is truly necessary for internal security, public health or to prevent criminal activity from continuing. - 3. The expulsion penalty shall be executed without regard to total or partial fulfillment of the principal penalty. Article 88

Prohibition from driving

1. A disqualification from driving motor vehicles for a period from 3 months to 2 years may be applied to a person who receives a sentence:

- a) For a crime provided for in articles 207 to 209; - b) For a crime committed with a motorized vehicle and executed with facility precisely because of using a motorized vehicle; - c) For a crime of disobedience for refusing to take the test that the law establishes to know whether a person is driving a motorized vehicle under the influence of alcohol, psychotropic substances or products with analogous disturbing effects on their physical, mental or psychological aptitude. - 2. The provisions of article 86, n. 3, shall also apply here. Article 89

Cancellation of license to bear arms

1. When there is a sentence for an intentional crime committed with a weapon, having regard to the circumstances and the seriousness of the conduct, the tribunal may decree cancellation41 of the license to use and possess weapons, for a period from 2 years to 8 years. 2. The provisions of article 86, n. 3, shall also apply here. CHAPTER VII

DETERMINATION OF PENALTY

Article 90

General principle

1. When the law fixes a penalty, the penalty refers to the crime in its consummated form. 2. To obtain the abstract framework of the penalty from which the concrete penalty shall be determined:

- a) The modifying circumstances of recidivism and criminal habituality provided for in articles 53 and 54, respectively, shall be applied to the abstract penalty of the consummated crime; - b) Following the provisions of the previous subparagraph or starting from the abstract penalty of the consummated crime, when there are modifying circumstances, the situation that gives rise to extraordinary mitigation, when it exists, shall be considered. Article 91

Concrete determination of penalty

1. Having obtained the abstract framework of the penalty pursuant to the previous article, the tribunal shall evaluate all circumstances that do not enter into the legal type and do not enter into the assessment pursuant to the provisions of the previous article but aggravate or diminish the responsibility of the convict. 2. Based on the assessment of those latter circumstances, the tribunal shall fix the exact measure of the penalty it considers necessary to protect the legal goods essential to life in 41 (89) Kasasaun (s) - Anulasaun society and for the agent's social reintegration, within the framework fixed within the limits of the type or within the limits resulting from the provisions of the previous article. 3. The seriousness of the penalty applied to the convict may never exceed the seriousness of the guilt. Article 92

Special case of fine penalty

To apply the fine penalty, the provisions of this chapter shall be applied to determine the time regarding the duration of the fine, without prejudice to the provisions of article 75, n. 2, to calculate the amount corresponding to the fine for each day. CHAPTER VIII

SECURITY MEASURE

SECTION I

INTERNMENT MEASURE

Article 93

Prerequisite

When a person who is not imputable pursuant to the provisions of article 21 commits an act described in the legal type of a crime, the tribunal may order their admission to an adequate establishment, when, due to their mental anomaly and the nature and seriousness of the act committed, there is basis to fear that they may commit another typical act corresponding to a crime against persons or a crime of common danger. Article 94

Duration

1. When the act the person not imputable committed gives rise to imprisonment up to 3 years, the internment shall not exceed 1 year. 2. When the person not imputable committed an act corresponding to a crime against persons or a crime of common danger punishable with an imprisonment penalty of 5 years or more, the internment shall not be less than 3 years, except when it is seen that release does not prejudice the defense of public order and social peace. 3.
