When the person not imputable committed a crime punishable with a penalty (of imprisonment) exceeding 8 years and it is better not to release them because otherwise they may commit again a crime such as this one, the internment may be extended by further periods of 2 years, successively42, until it is seen that the state of dangerousness that gave rise to the extension has ended. 4. The internment shall not exceed the maximum limit of the penalty applicable to the crime the accused committed. Article 95

Cessation of measure

1. The internment measure ends when the criminal dangerousness that gave rise to the measure ends or, if that dangerousness remains, when the maximum duration of the measure is reached, except in the circumstances written in article, n. 3 and 4 of the previous article. 2. A review of the internment measure shall be conducted annually. 3. When there is an invocation that there is a justifying cause to end the internment, the tribunal, at a specific moment, shall examine the question. Article 96

Substitution of internment measure

1. Release on probation or, regarding a foreigner, expulsion from the national territory may be imposed in place of the internment measure. 2. The provisions of article 87 shall apply to expulsion from the national territory. Article 97

Release on probation

1. When the review referred to in article 95 sees that there is reason to believe that the purpose of the measure can be achieved in an open environment, the tribunal shall place the internee on release on probation. 42 (94) Susesivamente (adv) – Tuir-malu; beibeik. 2. The period of release on probation shall be fixed at a minimum of 2 years to a maximum of 5 years, but shall not exceed the maximum limit fixed in article 94, n. 4. 3. The decision regarding release on probation may order the internee to follow rules of conduct, necessary to prevent dangerousness, as written in article 70, to follow treatment and an appropriate43 regime of outpatient cure and to present themselves for examination and observation at the place indicated to them. 4. The agent whose execution of internment has been suspended shall be placed under the supervision of the service for social reintegration. 5. When there is no motive to revoke release on probation, and the period of release on probation has ended, the internment measure shall be declared extinct. 6. When at the moment the period of release on probation ends there is ongoing a process or incident that may give rise to revocation of the measure, only after the process or incident ends and there is no revocation may the measure be declared extinct. Article 98

Revocation of release on probation

1. Release on probation shall be revoked when:

- a) The conduct of the person not imputable demonstrates that it is truly necessary to apply internment; - b) The person not imputable receives a sentence with a penalty that deprives liberty and there are no prerequisites to suspend the execution of that penalty, pursuant to article 68, n. 1. - 2. Revocation gives rise to reinternment, and the provisions of articles 94 and 96 shall also apply here. Article 99

Suspension of the execution of internment

1. The tribunal may order suspension of internment when, reasonably, it can be expected that with the suspension the purpose of the security measure can be achieved and the suspension is compatible with the defense of public order and social peace. 43 (97) Apropriadu (adj) – Di'ak ba situasaun ida. 2. Article 97, n. 3 and 4, shall also apply to the suspension of internment. 3. The execution of internment shall not be suspended when the agent receives a sentence with a penalty that deprives liberty and there is no requirement to suspend the execution of that penalty pursuant to article 68, n. 1. 4. The duration and cessation of internment shall be determined pursuant to articles 94 and 95, respectively44. 5. The provisions of article 98 shall also apply to the decision suspending the internment measure. SECTION II

OTHER SECURITY MEASURES

Article 100

Professional interdiction measure

1. When a person not imputable due to mental anomaly commits an act provided for in the legal type of a crime related to a professional activity they exercise and there is fear that, in the course of exercising that occupation they may commit an act similar to this one, the tribunal, having regard to the circumstances surrounding the case and the personality of the person not imputable, may prohibit them from exercising that activity for a period from 1 year to 5 years. 2. The period shall be suspended during the time the agent is deprived of liberty due to a procedural coercive measure, penalty or security measure. Article 101

Prohibition from driving and cancellation of license to use and possess weapons

1. A person not imputable who commits an act provided for in article

88, n. 1, may be disqualified from driving motor vehicles for a period from 2 years to 6 years, when their personality

44 (99) Respetivamente (adv) – ida-idak ba ida-idak; tuir orden ne'ebé hatudu hela. Internamentu nia durasaun no sesasaun sei determina tuir artigu 94 no 95, respetivamente = Internamentu nia durasaun tuir artigu 94 no nia sesasaun sei determina tuir artigu 95. causes fear that they may commit again an act similar to this one. 2. When the act the person not imputable committed is related to the use of weapons, the tribunal may decree cancellation of the license to use and possess weapons for a period from 5 years to 10 years, when their personality causes fear that they may commit again an act similar to this one. 3. The provisions of article 86, n. 3, shall also apply here. CHAPTER IX

OTHER CONSEQUENCES OF CRIME

Article 102

Loss of the object of crime

1. Objects used or to be used to commit crime, or resulting from crime, shall be declared lost to the State, when, according to their nature or the circumstances surrounding the case, they cause danger to the security of persons or to public order, or may be used again to commit new crime. 2. The victim and third parties who did not contribute to using or producing those objects or extracting advantage from those objects that are their property shall nevertheless have their rights guaranteed. 3. The tribunal shall give destination to those objects already declared lost, when the law does not say anything, such as ordering the destruction of those objects or rendering them no longer able to enter commerce. 4. The provisions of n. 1 shall nevertheless apply, even when a person does not receive a penalty for that act. Article 103

Loss of advantage

1. Things, rights or advantages obtained, directly or indirectly, from the commission of crime shall be declared lost to the State, without prejudice to the rights of the victim or third parties in good faith. 2. When those things, rights or advantages themselves cannot enter into the hands of the State, the value of those things, rights or advantages shall be paid to the State. Article 104

Civil liability for crime

1. The tribunal ex officio must ascertain and fix compensation for loss and damage resulting from crime when it is already known that there is damage and the value of that damage, except when the injured party declares, pursuant to the criminal procedural law, that they wish to make a separate claim. 2. The norms of civil law regulate the prerequisites and calculation of compensation. 3. The person responsible for compensation may make a settlement regarding the compensation, but the settlement must be notified to the tribunal, otherwise the act has no efficacy. Article 105

Privilege of the injured party's credit

The credit arising from the injured party's right to compensation for loss and damage resulting from crime has preference over other credits that arose after the commission of the act, including45 costs and amounts46 regarding fines. TITLE V

RIGHT TO COMPLAINT

Article 106

Nature of crime

1. Regarding the right to make complaint, crimes may be of a public or semi-public nature. 2. Public crimes are those for which a complaint is not required to give rise to criminal proceedings. 3. Semi-public crimes are those for which a complaint is required to give rise to criminal proceedings. 4. A holder exercises their right to complaint when they indicate that they want criminal proceedings. 45 (105) Inkluindu (v) – Iha ne'e sura hotu; iha ne'e sura mós; iha ne'e tama hotu; iha ne'e tama mós. 46 (105) Kuantia (s) – Osan lubun ida. Article 107

Holder of the right to complaint

When a complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings, those whom the criminal procedural law indicates as holders of the right to complaint have standing to file the complaint. Article 108

Period to exercise right

The right to complaint must be exercised within 6 months and the period is counted separately for each holder of the right. Article 109

Renunciation and desistance from complaint

The renunciation, desistance or non-exercise of the right to complaint regarding one participant in crime benefits the other participants in cases where they also cannot be subjected to criminal prosecution without a complaint. TITLE VI

EXTINCTION OF CRIMINAL LIABILITY

CHAPTER I

PRESCRIPTION OF CRIMINAL PROCEEDINGS

Article 110

Period of prescription

1. Criminal proceedings are extinguished, by prescription, when, beginning from the moment the agent committed the crime, the following periods have passed:

- a) 20 years, regarding a crime punishable with an imprisonment penalty with a maximum limit exceeding 12 years; - b) 15 years, regarding a crime punishable with an imprisonment penalty with a maximum limit exceeding 7 years but not reaching 12 years; - c) 8 years, regarding a crime punishable with an imprisonment penalty with a maximum limit exceeding 3 years but not exceeding 7 years; - d) 4 years, in other cases. - 2. When the law establishes as an alternative47 for a crime an imprisonment penalty or a fine penalty, the former shall be considered for the provisions of this article. Article 111

Calculation of period

1. The period of prescription of criminal proceedings begins at the moment the agent consummates the act or, regarding a non-consummated crime, continuing crime or habitual crime, on the day they commit the last act of execution. 2. In permanent crimes, the period of prescription begins at the moment the consummation ends. 3. Regarding complicity, only the act of the author shall be considered. Article 112

Suspension of prescription

1. The prescription of criminal proceedings is suspended in cases the law provides and also during the time that:

- a) According to law, criminal proceedings cannot begin or continue because legal authorization or a sentence that a non-criminal tribunal must give does not yet exist or because of the referral of a prejudicial question to a non-criminal judgment; - b) The offender is serving abroad a penalty or security measure that deprives liberty; - c) Criminal proceedings are already ongoing, beginning from the moment the accused receives notification of the charge. - 2. The proceedings shall run again at the moment the cause of suspension ends. 47 (112) Alternativa (s) – posibilidade atu hili buat ida hosi buat rua. Estabelese, ho alternativa, pena prizaun ka pena multa = fó posibilidade atu hili pena prizaun ka pena multa. 3. The cause of suspension shall not exceed half the period provided for in article 110, according to each situation. CHAPTER II

PRESCRIPTION OF PENALTY AND SECURITY

MEASURE

Article 113

Period of prescription of penalty

1. Penalties shall be prescribed in the following periods:

- a) 25 years when exceeding 12 years of imprisonment; - b) 20 years when exceeding 8 years but not exceeding 12 years of imprisonment; - c) 12 years when exceeding 4 years but not exceeding 8 years of imprisonment; - d) 8 years when they are not imprisonment penalties that the previous subparagraphs provide; - e) 4 years when the penalty is a fine penalty. - 2. The period of prescription of penalty begins at the moment the decision applying the penalty becomes final. Article 114

Prescription of accessory penalty

The prescription of accessory penalty48 follows the regime of the principal penalty. Article 115

Period of prescription of security measure

Security measures are prescribed in the following periods:

- a) 15 years when they deprive liberty; - b) 5 years when they do not deprive liberty;- c) 2 years when concerning the revocation of the license to use and possess weapons.

48 (114) Accessory penalty – A penalty that is applied together with the principal penalty to a criminal, such as the prohibition to drive a vehicle or possess weapons.

Article 116

Suspension of prescription

1. The prescription of penalties and security measures is suspended in cases provided by law and during the time when:

- a) According to law, execution cannot begin or continue;
- b) Following the convict's evasion49 and during the time when he has not yet been recaptured;
- c) The convict is serving a penalty or security measure that deprives liberty;
- d) The period to pay the fine is still running;
- e) The convict cannot temporarily perform community work;
- f) Execution is underway.
- 2. The prescription runs again from the day the cause of suspension ends.
- 3. The provisions of Article 112 also apply here.

CHAPTER III

IMPRESCRIPTIBILITY

Article 117

Crimes of genocide, against peace and humanity and concerning war

Criminal proceedings and penalties for crimes of genocide, against peace and humanity and war crimes do not prescribe.

CHAPTER IV

OTHER CAUSES OF EXTINCTION

Article 118

Other causes

Criminal liability is extinguished in cases provided by law and due to the death of the agent, due to amnesty and due to pardon.

49 (116) Evasion (n) – By evasion a person has fled from prison or the place where he is detained.

Article 119

Death of the agent

The death of the agent extinguishes criminal proceedings and also criminal sanctions that have already been applied.

Article 120

Amnesty

Amnesty extinguishes criminal proceedings and stops the execution of sanctions that have not been fulfilled totally or partially, and also the effects of such sanction and accessory penalties as applicable.

Article 121

Amnesty and crimes in concurrence

Save for other provisions, amnesty applies to each crime that enters into concurrence.

Article 122

Pardon

Pardon extinguishes the penalty, totally or partially, or substitutes another penalty provided in law and more favorable to the convict in place of that penalty.

BOOK II

SPECIAL PART

TITLE I

CRIMES AGAINST PEACE AND HUMANITY

CHAPTER I

CRIMES OF GENOCIDE AND AGAINST HUMANITY

Article 123

Genocide

1. Anyone who, with intent to destroy, totally or partially, a national, ethnic, racial or religious group, commits:

- a) Homicide or serious offense against the physical or psychological integrity of such group;
- b) Acts that, in any way, prevent the elements of such group from procreating50 or living;
- c) Rape, sexual slavery, forced prostitution, forced sterilization or other sexual violence of similar gravity;
- d) Violent separation of elements of such group from such group to another group;
- e) Acts that, by violence, prevent such group from residing or continuing to reside in a geographic space that, according to tradition or history, is considered as belonging to such group;
- f) Subjection of such group to living conditions or cruel, degrading or inhuman treatment, which may one day cause its total or partial destruction;
- g) Widespread confiscation or seizure of property belonging to elements of such group;
- h) Prohibition of elements of such group from exercising commercial, industrial or professional activity;
- i) Diffusion51 of an epidemic that may cause elements of such group to die or suffer offense to their physical integrity;
- j) Prohibition, omission or impediment, by any means, of elements of such group from receiving adequate humanitarian assistance to combat an epidemic or severe food shortage,

shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 30 years.

- 2. Anyone who, publicly and directly, incites others to commit genocide shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years.

50 (123) By procreation a person has children or generation.

51 (123) Diffusion (n) – By diffusion a person spreads something (in all directions) thereby making it live or reach other people or other places. To diffuse = To spread or transmit to others or to other places

Article 124

Crimes against humanity

Anyone who, in the context of a widespread or systematic attack against a civilian population, commits acts that give rise to:

- a) Homicide or serious offense against physical or psychological integrity;
- b) Extermination, which occurs when someone subjects a population totally or partially to adverse living conditions, such as preventing such population from obtaining food or medicine, which may cause one or many persons to die;
- c) Enslavement;
- d) Deportation or forced transfer of a population, which occurs when someone makes an illicit displacement of one or many persons to another State or place by expulsion or other coercive acts;
- e) Imprisonment or other serious means that deprive a person's liberty, against the norms or principles of international law;
- f) Torture, which occurs when someone does something to cause severe physical or psychological pain or suffering to a person who has already lost his liberty or has already come under the agent's control;
- g) Rape, sexual slavery, forced prostitution, forced pregnancy, forced sterilization or other sexual violence of similar gravity;
- h) Persecution, which occurs when there is deprivation of the enjoyment of fundamental rights, against international law, of a group or collectivity that can be identified, due to political, racial, national, ethnic, cultural, religious, sexual motives or other motives universally recognized as unacceptable under international law;
- i) Forced disappearance of persons, which occurs when there is detention, imprisonment or kidnapping52

52 (124) Kidnapping (n) – By kidnapping a person seizes and holds someone and does not let him leave the place where the agent is holding him, against such person's will.

that the State or political organization carries out or gives authorization, support or agreement to carry out, and, thereafter, refuses to recognize that such deprivation of liberty exists or to give information about such situation or about where such persons are so that they cannot receive the protection of the law for a long time;

- j) Apartheid, which occurs when someone commits inhuman acts in an institutionalized regime in which one racial group exercises oppression and systematic domination over another group or several groups, with intent to maintain53 such regime;
- k) Other inhuman acts of similar character to those above with intent to cause severe suffering or serious injury or affect mental or physical health,

shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 30 years.

CHAPTER II

WAR CRIMES

Article 125

War crimes against persons

1. Anyone who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or an armed conflict of non-international character, commits against persons protected by international humanitarian law:

- a) Homicide;
- b) Torture or cruel, degrading or inhuman treatment, including biological experimentation;
- c) Serious offense against physical integrity or acts that cause severe suffering;
- d) Taking of hostages54;
- e) Compelling to serve in the enemy armed forces or conscripting or enlisting

53 (1249 maintain (v) – to make something or someone continue; to hold something or someone firmly. Police maintain security = Police keep security in place. maintain apartheid = make apartheid continue to exist.

54 (125) Taking of hostages – Holding someone as a guarantee to demand something from someone.

children under 18 years of age into a State's armed forces, military or paramilitary forces or into an armed group that is not a State's armed forces, military or paramilitary forces, or using them to participate in hostilities55;

- f) Serious, prolonged and unjustified limitation of a person's liberty;
- g) Deportation or transfer or illegal deprivation of liberty;
- h) Unjustified subtraction or destruction of patrimonial property of great value;
- i) Conviction and execution of a sentence, when there has not yet been a fair or impartial trial;
- j) Acts that offend a person's dignity, especially by humiliating and degrading treatment;
- k) Homicide or wounding of a combatant who has already laid down his arms or who, having no more means to defend himself, has already surrendered unconditionally or has in any way been removed from combat;
- l) The acts that the previous article, paragraph g), describes;
- m) Subjecting persons who are already in the hands of one belligerent party to physical mutilation or medical or scientific experimentation of any type, which is not for medical, dental or hospital treatment and is not done in their interest, which causes them to die or causes serious danger to their health;

shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years.

2. The penalty shall be aggravated by one fifth in its minimum and maximum limits when the agent commits the acts referred to in the previous number against a member of a humanitarian institution.

3. Anyone who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character:

- a) Transfers, directly or indirectly, as an occupying power, part of its own civilian population into the occupied territory or transfers the population of the occupied territory, in whole or in part

55 (125) Hostilities (n) - Fighting only

into or out of such territory;

- b) Compels a prisoner of war or other person receiving protection to serve in the enemy power's armed forces;
- c) After the fighting has ended, delays the repatriation of prisoners of war, without justified motive,

shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 30 years.

Article 126

War crimes by prohibited method

Anyone who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or an armed conflict of non-international character:

- a) Attacks the civilian population in general or civilians who do not directly participate in hostilities;
- b) Attacks civilian objects that are not military objectives;
- c) Attacks, by any means, population centers, dwellings or buildings that are not being defended and are not military objectives;
- d) Conducts an indiscriminate attack that affects the civilian population or objects of civilian character, knowing that such attack will cause loss of life, cause injury to civilians or damage to objects of civilian character, which is excessive;
- e) Takes advantage of the presence of civilians or other protected persons to prevent certain points, zones or military forces from becoming targets of military operations;
- f) Deliberately causes starvation of the civilian population as a method of warfare, thereby depriving them of objects they need to survive;
- g) Declares or threatens, in his capacity as an official, that no shelter will be given;
- h) Kills or injures enemy combatants by treachery;
- i) Conducts an attack, even knowing that such attack will cause great, lasting and serious damage to the environment and, therefore, it can be clearly seen that such attack is excessive when compared to the concrete and direct overall military advantage foreseen;
- j) Commits perfidy, understood as acts of killing, injuring or capturing, in which, with intent to deceive, one uses the adversary's good faith to make him believe that he has the right to receive or has the obligation to ensure protection that the rules of international humanitarian law provide;

shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 30 years.

Article 127

War crimes by prohibited means of warfare

1. Anyone who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or an armed conflict of non-international character, uses weapons, projectiles, materials and methods of combat that, by their nature, cause unnecessary injury or unnecessary suffering or have indiscriminate effects, against the international law applicable to armed conflict, shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years.

2. The previous number includes, namely, the use of the following means:

- a) Poison or poisoned weapons;
- b) Asphyxiating, toxic or similar gases, or any liquids, materials or analogous devices;
- c) Bullets that expand or flatten easily in the human body, such as bullets that have a hard casing that does not completely cover the core or has incisions;
- d) Anti-personnel mines;
- e) Chemical weapons;
- f) Weapons whose principal effect is to injure by fragments that cannot be located in the human body by X-rays;
- g) Incendiary weapons;
- h) Laser56 weapons that cause blindness.

56 (127) Laser (n) – A device that produces a radiation of visible light, or with close frequency, coherent, monochromatic and very strong

3. The weapons, instruments and products referred to in the previous number are those that international law considers as such.

Article 128

War crimes against property protected by distinctive insignia or emblem

Anyone who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or an armed conflict of non-international character, attacks:

- a) Personnel, installations, materials, units or vehicles participating in a mission to maintain peace or provide humanitarian assistance, under the United Nations Charter, when they are entitled to the protection that international law gives to persons or civilian objects;
- b) Buildings, installations, materials, units or vehicles, that are marked with the distinctive emblem of the Geneva Conventions or personnel authorized to use such emblems,

shall be punished with imprisonment from 10 to 20 years.

Article 129

War crimes against property

Anyone who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or an armed conflict of non-international character:

- a) Subtracts, destroys or damages patrimonial property on a large scale or of great value, without military necessity or in an illegal or arbitrary manner;
- b) Attacks, destroys or damages buildings dedicated to religious worship, education, art, science or charity, cultural or historical monuments, archaeological sites, hospitals and places where the sick and wounded are gathered, when they are not military objectives;
- c) Pillages57 any city or locality, even if taken by assault,

57 (129) Pillage (v) – To loot; to rob, plunder or carry away someone else's belongings by violence.

shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years.

Article 130

War crimes against other rights

Anyone who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or an armed conflict of non-international character, declares abolished, suspended or inadmissible in a tribunal the rights or proceedings of nationals of the enemy party shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years.

CHAPTER III

AGAINST PEACE AND FREEDOM

Article 131

Terrorist organization

1. A group, organization or terrorist association shall be considered two or more persons who, to achieve political, ideological, philosophical or confessional purposes, act in concert, with intent to prejudice national integrity or independence, impede, alter or subvert the functioning of national or international institutions, frighten or compel public authorities, international organizations or any person, group or the general population to do something, not do something or tolerate something, through serious crimes:

- a) Against life, physical integrity or liberty of persons;
- b) Against the security of transport and communications, including telegraphic, telephonic, radio or television communications;
- c) That cause with intent common danger, by fire, explosion, release of radioactive substances or toxic or asphyxiating gases, flood or avalanche, collapse of construction, contamination of food and drinking water or diffusion of disease, harmful pests, plants or animals;
- d) In which nuclear energy, firearms, explosive substances or devices, incendiary means of any nature, booby-trapped parcels or letters will be used;
- e) Acts that destroy or impede means or ways of communication, installations of public services or international installations or installations to supply and satisfy the population's vital needs, or divert them from their normal purpose;
- f) Research and development of biological or chemical weapons.
- 2. Anyone who promotes or founds a group, organization or terrorist association, enters into, participates in or supports such group, organization or association shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years.
- 3. Anyone who leads or directs a group, organization or terrorist association shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 30 years.
- 4. When the group, organization or terrorist association or the persons referred to in numbers 2 and 3 possess the means that

n. 1, paragraph d), indicates, the minimum and maximum limits of the penalty shall be aggravated by one third.

- 5. Preparatory acts to constitute a group, organization or terrorist association shall give rise to a penalty with its minimum and maximum limits reduced by half.
- 6. The penalties referred to may receive extraordinary attenuation or not give rise to punishment when the agent voluntarily abandons his activity, prevents or makes serious efforts to prevent the danger that such activity causes or the group, organization or terrorist association from continuing or informs the authorities that the group, organization or association exists, thereby enabling such authority to prevent them from committing crimes.

Article 132

Terrorism

1. Anyone who commits the crimes provided in the previous article, n. 1, paragraphs a) to c) and e), or any crime by the means referred to in that number's paragraph d) or f), with the intent referred to therein, shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years or the penalty corresponding to the crime committed with its minimum and maximum limits aggravated by one third, when such penalty is equal or more severe.

2. The penalty may receive special attenuation or not give rise to punishment when the agent voluntarily abandons his activity, removes or considerably reduces the danger that such activity causes, prevents the result that the law seeks to avoid from occurring or concretely helps to obtain decisive evidence to identify or capture other responsible persons.

Article 133

Financing of terrorism

Anyone who, by any means, directly or indirectly and intentionally, supplies, collects or holds funds or any property and also products or rights that can be converted into funds, seeks to obtain such funds, property, products or rights, with intent to use or knowing that they may be used, totally or partially, to plan, prepare or commit the acts that Article 131, n. 1, provides or to commit such acts with the intent referred to in Article 132, n. 1, shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years.

Article 134

Incitement to war

1. Anyone who, by any means, publicly and repeatedly, incites58 hatred against any race, people or nation, with intent to provoke war or to impede peaceful coexistence between diverse59 races, peoples or nations, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years.

2. Anyone who entices60 or recruits Timorese or foreign citizens to, by service rendered to a foreign group or power, make war against a State or overthrow

58 (134) Incite (v) – To give courage; to incite (someone) to do something; to give stimulus; to entice. Incite hatred against a race = to stir up hatred against a race.

59 (134) Diverse (adj) – Different; various; that are not the same; that are not only one; that have different aspects; that are not just one kind.

60 (134) Entice (v) – To incite (someone) to do something; to give courage or stimulus.

by violent means the legitimate government of another state shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years.

Article 135

Racial or religious discrimination

1. Anyone who founds, constitutes an organization or develops organized propaganda to incite or encourage racial or religious discrimination, hatred or violence, and also anyone who participates in or gives assistance, including financing, to such organizations or activities shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years.

2. Anyone who, in a public meeting, by writing for dissemination or by any means of social communication, diffuses ideas with intent to incite racial or religious discrimination or to encourage or provoke violent acts against any person or group because of his race, color, ethnic origin or religion, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to

8 years.

CHAPTER IV

COMMON PROVISIONS

Article 136

Responsibility of military commander or other superior

1. A military commander or whoever acts as a military commander who, knowing that forces under his command and effective control or under his responsibility and effective control are committing or about to commit any crime provided in this title, does not adopt necessary and adequate measures to prevent or repress such practice or to immediately report to the competent authority shall be punished with the penalty corresponding to such crime or to the crime that will still be committed.

2. The provisions of the previous number shall also apply, with necessary adaptations, to a superior regarding the control of subordinates who are under his authority and effective control.

Article 137

Definitions

For the purposes of this title, the following shall be considered:

- a) "Armed conflict of international character" one that:
- i) Occurs between states, even without formal declaration of war, even if one of such States does not recognize the state of war;
- ii) Corresponds to total or partial occupation of a State's territory, even if such occupation does not face military resistance;
- iii) Corresponds to a situation in which peoples fight against colonial domination, foreign occupation and regimes that practice segregation, when the people exercise their right to self-determination enshrined in the United Nations Charter and in the declaration on principles of international law concerning friendly relations and cooperation between States;
- b) "Armed conflict of non-international character" one that occurs in a State's territory, is prolonged in character and puts governmental authorities against organized groups or organized groups against each other, with the exception of situations in which there are disturbances and in which there are internal tensions, such as sporadic or isolated acts of violence or others of similar character;
- c) "Protected person":
- i) In international armed conflict, persons protected for the purposes of the Geneva Conventions of 1949 and Additional Protocol I, namely wounded, sick, shipwrecked, prisoners of war, medical or religious personnel and the civilian population;
- ii) In armed conflict of non-international character, the wounded, sick and shipwrecked, and also persons who do not take active part in hostilities but are in the enemy's hands;
- iii) In armed conflict of international character and in armed conflict of national character, members of the armed forces and combatants of the enemy party who have already laid down their arms or no longer have other means of defense.

TITLE II

CRIMES AGAINST PERSONS

CHAPTER I

AGAINST LIFE

Article 138

Simple homicide

Anyone who kills another person shall be punished with imprisonment from 8 to 20 years.

Article 139

Aggravated homicide

An agent who kills another person in circumstances that show special censurability or perversity, namely, because of:

- a) Use of poison, torture, asphyxiation, fire, explosives or other insidious means or means that constitute the commission of a crime of common danger, or other cruel acts, to increase the victim's suffering;
- b) Use of dissimulation or other means or resources to make the victim have difficulty defending himself or be unable to defend himself;
- c) Killing due to greed61, due to liking to kill, due to wanting to obtain sexual excitement or satisfaction, due to payment or reward or promise of payment or reward or due to other futile or base motive;
- d) Killing to prepare, execute or conceal another crime, facilitate escape or ensure impunity for the agent of a crime;
- e) Killing due to racial, religious or political hatred;

61 (139) Greed – Strong and insatiable desire; greedy (adj); eyes never satisfied (for something)

- f) Killing with premeditation, which occurs when he uses coolness62 of mind, reflection on the means to be used or maintains his intent to kill for 24 hours;
- g) When the victim is the agent's spouse, descendant, ascendant, collateral or affine up to the second degree, adoptee or person who lives with him in a relationship of hierarchical, economic or labor dependence;
- h) The victim is particularly vulnerable due to his age, illness or physical or psychological disability;
- i) The victim is a member of a sovereign organ or constitutional political organ, a member of an administrative organ, magistrate, defender, lawyer, officer of justice, official or other person who holds public service, namely that he is holding his function or because of the function he holds;
- j) The victim is a witness, declarant, expert, injured party or offended person and the agent commits such crime to impede testimony, denunciation of facts or complaint or because of his intervention in a proceeding,

shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years.

Article 140

Negligent homicide

1. Anyone who, due to negligence, kills another person shall be punished with imprisonment up to 4 years or a fine.

2. In cases where the agent acts with gross63 negligence he shall be punished with imprisonment up to 5 years.

Article 141*

Interruption of pregnancy

62 (139) Coolness of mind – cold heart

63 (140) Gross (adj) – Serious; severe. Gross negligence = serious negligence; lack of serious care. A person acts with gross negligence when he extremely lacks care when he does such thing.

1. Anyone who causes a pregnant woman to abort, by any means and without such woman's consent, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years.

2. Anyone who causes a pregnant woman to abort, by any means and with such woman's consent, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years.

3. A pregnant woman who gives consent to another person to perform an abortion, or, herself or through another person, causes an abortion to herself shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years.

4. The provisions of the previous numbers do not apply when, according to existing medical knowledge and experience and having done everything that can be done to save the pregnant woman and the fetus or embryo's life, the interruption of pregnancy is the only way to prevent death of the pregnant woman, namely that there is medical certification and a doctor or other health professional performs it, under a doctor's direction, in a public or officially recognized health establishment, and with the pregnant woman's consent.

5. Before performing the interruption a panel of three doctors must certify the circumstances referred to in the previous number, in a written and signed medical certificate; in such panel there is one doctor who performs or supervises the performance of the interruption of pregnancy.

6. The pregnant woman's consent must be given personally in a written and signed document, having heard, when possible, her spouse or person who lives with her as if married, or another person, according to such woman's request, who gives it, and, when possible, must be given two days before performing such interruption.

7. When the pregnant woman is a minor, her legal representative must give consent.

8. When the pregnant woman is an adult or emancipated and a psychologically incapacitated person or a person who suffers from psychological incapacity, her spouse or person who lives with her as if married, or her legal representative, ascendant or descendant, or, if absent, any collateral relative, respectively and successively, gives such consent.

9. When it is not possible to obtain the medical certificate referred to in n. 5 and/or it is not possible to obtain consent as written in

n. 6 to 8, and the interruption must be done quickly and cannot be delayed any longer, the doctor, considering each situation, will decide, also using, when possible, the opinion of other doctors.

10. In the panel referred to in n. 5 there shall be a doctor who has adequate knowledge to evaluate the circumstances that justify the interruption of pregnancy, and, when possible, a gynecologist/obstetrician.

11. The doctors who issue the medical certificate and the doctors who perform or supervise the performance of the interruption of pregnancy must give necessary clarifications to the pregnant woman and, when they are in such situation, to the persons that n. 7 and 8 refer to, namely about the method to be used in the interruption, the effects of such interruption itself and its consequences for the pregnant woman's physical and psychological health.

12. Doctors and other health professionals have the right to present objection according to their conscience against any act concerning the interruption of pregnancy.

13. Doctors and other health professionals who invoke objection according to their conscience must guarantee the immediate intervention of other health professionals in the acts that must be done and also immediate accompaniment of the pregnant woman.

14. Objection according to conscience must be expressed explicitly and must be communicated to the clinical head of the health establishment where the objector works.

* Law 6/2009, of 15 July, introduced this wording. The previous wording of Article 141 is as follows:

Article 141

Interruption of pregnancy

1. Anyone who causes a pregnant woman to abort, by any means and without such woman's consent, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years.

2. Anyone who causes a pregnant woman to abort, by any means and with such woman's consent, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years.

3. A pregnant woman who gives consent to another person to perform an abortion, or, herself or through another person, causes an abortion to herself shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years.

4. The provisions of the previous numbers do not apply in cases where the interruption of pregnancy constitutes the only means to avoid danger of death of the pregnant woman or the fetus or serious and irreversible injury to her body or her physical or psychological health, namely that a doctor or health professional performs it, in a health establishment, with authorization and supervision of a medical board and with the consent of the pregnant woman or her spouse.

5. Separate legislation shall regulate the provisions of n. 4

Article 142

Infanticide

A mother who kills her child during childbirth or immediately after childbirth and when she is still under the disturbing influence of childbirth shall be punished with imprisonment from 3 to 10 years.

Article 143

Exposure or abandonment

1. Whoever intentionally causes danger to another person's life:

- a) Due to placing such person in a place where he must face a situation in which he cannot defend himself alone; or
- b) Due to abandoning such person when he cannot defend himself due to his age, physical disability or illness, and the agent has an obligation to look after, care for or give assistance to such person,

shall be punished with imprisonment from 1 to 6 years.

- 2. When such fact:
- a) Causes serious offense to the victim's physical integrity, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years;
- b) Causes the victim's death, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years.
- 3. When such victim is the agent's spouse, descendant, ascendant, collateral, affine up to the second degree, adopter or adoptee, or person who lives with him as if married, the minimum and maximum limits of the penalties indicated in the previous numbers shall rise by one third.

Article 144

Incitement or assistance to suicide

1. Whoever incites another person to kill himself or gives assistance to another person to kill himself, such suicide occurring indeed in attempted or consummated form, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine.

2. When the agent commits the facts described in the previous number against any person that the previous article, number 3, indicates or against a minor under 17 years of age or against a person whose capacity to make an evaluation or determination is sensibly diminished, the penalty shall rise to imprisonment up to 5 years.

3. Anyone who, in any manner and repeatedly, makes public propaganda for suicide shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine.

CHAPTER II

AGAINST PHYSICAL INTEGRITY

Article 145

Simple offense against physical integrity

1. Whoever offends another person's body or health shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine.

2. To conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

Article 146

Serious offense against physical integrity

Whoever offends another person's body or health with intent to:

- a) Cause such person to lose any organ or limb;
- b) Seriously and permanently disfigure such person;
- c) Seriously and permanently or definitively affect such person's capacity to work, his intellectual capacity or his capacity to have children;
- d) Provoke permanent illness or incurable psychological anomaly; or
- e) Cause danger to his life,

shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years.

Article 147

Aggravation

1.Whoever, intending only to injure another person's body or health:

- a) Shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 5 years, when he/she causes the offense provided for in article 146;
- b) Shall be punished with imprisonment of 1 to 6 years, when he/she causes that person's death through negligence.
- 2. Whoever, intending only to commit the offense provided for in article 146, but causes a person's death through negligence, shall be punished with imprisonment of 4 to 12 years.
- 3. When the perpetrator commits the crimes referred to in the two preceding articles against a person whom article 139, paragraph i), deals with, because the victim holds the office referred to therein or when the victim is currently holding that office, the penalty shall be aggravated by one third in its minimum and maximum limits, when another legal provision does not provide for a heavier penalty.

Article 148

Negligent injury to physical integrity

1. Whoever injures another person's body or health through negligence shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine.
2. If the negligence is gross, the perpetrator shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine.
3. When the act causes grievous bodily harm the perpetrator shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine.
4. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

Article 149

Medical-surgical intervention and treatment

1. Interventions and treatments shall not be considered bodily injury which, according to the experience and knowledge obtained in medicine, are indicated and performed by a physician or person with legal authorization, in accordance with "leges artis", to prevent, diagnose, control or reduce illness, pain, bodily injury or fatigue or mental disturbance.
2. When, due to violation of "leges artis", danger arises to the patient's body, health or life, the perpetrator shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine.
3. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

Article 150

Bodily injury with poisonous substance

1. Whoever injures another person's body or health by administering to him/her a poisonous substance or substance harmful to physical or mental health shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 5 years.
2. When the act causes any of the consequences provided for in article 146 or causes the victim's death, the perpetrator shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years or 4 to 12 years, respectively.

Article 151

Reciprocal bodily injury

1. When two persons mutually injure each other's body or health but they did not commit the act in self-defense and there is no circumstance provided for in article 146 and none of the participants dies, they shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine.
2. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

Article 152

Participation in an affray

1. Whoever intervenes or participates with two or more persons in an affray, which causes a person's death or grievous bodily harm, but those consequences cannot be imputed to him/her with intent, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine.
2. Participation in an affray does not give rise to punishment when it occurs for a non-censurable motive, namely because the perpetrator participates to react to an attack, to defend another person or to separate the combatants.

Article 153

Ill-treatment of incapacitated persons

1. Whoever is caring for or responsible for the education of or has as a subordinate in work activity an incapacitated person, particularly vulnerable due to illness, advanced age, pregnancy, physical or mental disability, and causes injury to that person's body or health, or subjects him/her to physical or psychological ill-treatment, or cruel treatment shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years, when another legal provision does not provide for a heavier penalty.
2. When the victim is the perpetrator's descendant, collateral, relative or affine up to the second degree, adoptee, or person who lives with him/her as a spouse, the penalty referred to in the preceding number shall be increased by one third in its minimum and maximum limits.

Article 154

Ill-treatment of spouse

A person who commits physical or psychological ill-treatment of his/her spouse or of a person who lives with him/her as a spouse shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years, when another legal provision does not provide for a heavier penalty.

Article 155

Ill-treatment of minors

1. A person who is required to care for a minor under 17 years of age, has responsibility for his/her education or uses him/her as a subordinate in work activity and:

- a) Causes injury to his/her body or health, commits physical and psychological ill-treatment or cruel treatment of him/her;
- b) Subjects him/her to economic exploitation, dangerous work or work that may compromise his/her education or his/her physical, mental, spiritual, moral or social development;
- c) Subjects him/her to slavery of any kind or analogous practice;
- d) Uses, recruits or offers him/her to be used for prostitution, to produce pornographic material or perform pornographic shows; or
- e) Uses, recruits or offers him/her to be used to commit illicit acts or activities, namely in the production or trafficking of narcotics as defined by international convention,

shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years, when another legal provision does not provide for a heavier penalty.

2. Whoever, in such a situation, uses a minor to beg as a poor person, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years, when another legal provision does not provide for a heavier penalty.
3. When the victim is the perpetrator's descendant, collateral, relative or affine up to the second degree, adoptee or person who lives with him/her in a similar condition, the penalties which the preceding numbers refer to shall be increased by one third in their minimum and maximum limits.

Article 156

Aggravation by result

When the ill-treatment described in articles 153 to 155 causes the effects referred to in article 146 the perpetrator shall be punished with imprisonment of 3 to 10 years, and, when it causes a person's death, with imprisonment of 5 to 15 years.

CHAPTER III

CRIMES AGAINST PERSONAL LIBERTY

SECTION I

PERSONAL ASSAULT

Article 157

Threat

1. Whoever, by any means, threatens another person with a crime to the extent of causing him/her fear or unease or to the extent of harming his/her freedom to determine himself/herself shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine.
2. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

Article 158

Coercion

1. Whoever, by violence or threat of serious harm, compels a person to do something or not to do something or to tolerate some activity shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine.
2. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

Article 159

Aggravated coercion

When the perpetrator commits coercion:

- a) By threatening with a crime punishable by imprisonment of more than 3 years;
- b) By gravely abusing an office which he/she, as a public official, holds;
- c) Against a person who cannot defend himself/herself, due to his/her age, disability, illness or pregnancy;
- d) Against a person referred to in article 139, paragraph i),

he/she shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine.

Article 160

Kidnapping

1. Whoever, in a situation not covered by the provisions of criminal procedural law, detains, imprisons, maintains in prison or detention another person or, in any way, deprives a person of liberty shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine.
2. Imprisonment of 2 to 8 years shall be applied when:

- a) The deprivation of liberty lasts more than 72 hours;
- b) The perpetrator commits the deprivation with injury to physical integrity, torture or other cruel, degrading or inhuman treatment;
- c) The deprivation causes the victim's death, through the perpetrator's negligence, or causes the victim to commit suicide;
- d) The perpetrator commits the deprivation against a person who exercises public, religious or political authority;
- e) An agent of authority or member of a political organization promotes, authorizes or supports the deprivation.

Article 161

Abduction

1. Whoever, by violence, threat or trickery, transfers another person from one place to another with the intention to:

- a) Subject the victim to extortion;
- b) Commit a crime of assault, exploitation or sexual abuse;
- c) Obtain ransom or reward; or
- d) Compel public authority or another person to commit an act or omission or to endure some activity,

shall be punished with imprisonment of 4 to 12 years.
- 2. Imprisonment of 5 to 15 years shall be applied when there also exist circumstances provided for in article 160, number 2.

Article 162

Enslavement

1. Whoever, by any means, places another person as a slave and uses him/her in that condition, shall be punished with imprisonment of 8 to 20 years.
2. The victim's consent has no relevance, when the perpetrator uses any of the means which the following article refers to.
3. To apply the provisions of this article, a person shall be considered in a state of slavery who is subjected, even de facto only, to power corresponding to the right of ownership, or other real right, or linked to the destination of a thing.

Article 163

Trafficking in persons

1. A person who recruits, alienates, surrenders, acquires, transports, transfers, harbors or receives a person, by threat, force or other coercion, by abduction, by fraud, by deception, by abusing his/her authority or abusing that person's vulnerable situation, or by delivering or receiving payment or benefit to obtain consent from the person who has authority over another person, for the purpose of obtaining exploitation, shall be punished with imprisonment of 8 to 20 years.
2. The penalty provided for in the preceding number shall be received by a person who recruits, transports, transfers, harbors or receives a person under 17 years of age to commit exploitation, even if it does not involve the means referred to in the preceding number.
3. To apply the provisions of this article, exploitation must include, at least, exploitation of another person's prostitution or other sexual exploitation, forced labor or service, slavery or practices similar to slavery, or servitude or extraction of organs.
4. The victim's consent has no relevance, when the perpetrator uses any of the means referred to in no. 1.

Article 164

Aggravation

When the perpetrator commits the acts described in article 162 and:

- a) To facilitate his/her or another person's sexual exploitation or use of the victim;
- b) Against a victim who is not yet 17 years old;
- c) Against a victim who is in a foreign country or he/she has taken to a foreign country;
- d) Uses the victim, against his/her will, to commit a crime; or
- e) At the time when he/she exercises activity that gives him/her public or religious authority in a group, region or in the whole country,

he/she shall be punished with imprisonment of 12 to 25 years.

Article 165

Trafficking in human organs

1. A person who obtains, alienates, surrenders, acquires, transports or transfers another person's tissue, organ, substance or body part, without consent or, for this, uses threat, force or other coercion, abduction, fraud, deception, abuse of his/her authority or of the victim's vulnerable situation, or by delivering or receiving payment or benefit, or assists another person to obtain, transact, transport or store that tissue, organ, substance or body, shall be punished with imprisonment of 3 to 10 years.
2. When the acts referred to in the preceding number cause any of the consequences provided for in article 146 or cause the victim's death, the perpetrator shall be punished with imprisonment of 4 to 20 years and 5 to 20 years, respectively.
3. The victim's consent has no criminal relevance, when the perpetrator uses any of the means referred to in no. 1.

Article 166

Selling of persons

1. Whoever, in a situation not covered by the provisions of article 136, by any act or transaction, transfers a person or group of persons to another person or group of persons to receive as payment an amount or consideration, reward or other advantage shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 8 years.
2. When the perpetrator commits the acts referred to in the preceding number:

- a) Against a person who is not yet 17 years old;
- b) By abusing authority arising from family, tutelary or curatorial relationship, economic or work dependency;
- c) By taking advantage of an office or place which, with any title, he/she exercises in a prison establishment, establishment for education or correction, hospital, hospice, asylum, clinic or other health establishment or establishment for assistance or treatment; or
- d) Against an unconscious or incapacitated person, particularly vulnerable due to illness, physical or mental disability,

he/she shall be punished with imprisonment of 3 to 12 years.
- 3. The consent of the victim or the consent of a person who exercises any power over the victim has no criminal relevance.

Article 167

Torture or cruel, degrading or inhuman treatment

1. Whoever, having the function to conduct prevention, investigation, decision on any criminal offense, execution of the respective sanction or protection, custody, surveillance or accompaniment of a detained or imprisoned person, commits torture or cruel degrading or inhuman treatment of that person, in order to:

- a) Obtain from that person or from another person a confession, testimony, declaration or information;
- b) Punish that person for an act which that person or another person has already committed or he/she believes that that person or another person has committed;
- c) Frighten that person or another person,

shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 8 years.
- 2. The penalty provided for in the preceding number shall also be received by a person who, on his/her own initiative, on superior order or by agreement with the competent entity to exercise the functions referred to in the preceding number, exercises de facto those functions by committing any act referred to therein.
- 3. Torture or cruel, degrading or inhuman treatment shall be considered to exist when any act causes acute physical or psychological suffering, severe physical or psychological fatigue or there is any act using chemical products, drugs or other means, natural or artificial, with the intention of disturbing the victim's capacity to make decisions or show his/her will freely.

Article 168

Aggravation

1. A person who, as a rule and in the conditions referred to in the preceding article:

- a) Causes grievous bodily harm, as referred to in article 146;
- b) Uses means or methods of particularly severe torture, such as beating, electric shock, simulated execution, hallucinogenic substances, sexual abuse or serious threat to family members;
- c) Commits those acts to prevent or make it difficult for a person to freely exercise his/her political or associative right which the Constitution enshrines;
- d) Habitually commits the acts referred to in this article,

shall be punished with imprisonment of 5 to 15 years.

2. When the acts referred to in this article or in the preceding article cause the victim to commit suicide or die the perpetrator shall be punished with imprisonment of 5 to 20 years.

Article 169

Omission of denunciation

1. A hierarchical superior who, having learned that his/her subordinate committed any act described in articles 167 and 168, does not make a denunciation within 3 days starting from the day on which he/she obtained that knowledge, shall be punished with imprisonment of 1 to 6 years.
2. Whoever, having obtained knowledge by virtue of his/her profession and officially that a person committed any act described in articles 167 and 168, does not immediately communicate to his/her hierarchical superior or does not make the respective denunciation shall be punished with the penalty fixed in the preceding number extraordinarily attenuated.

Article 170

Freedom of assembly and demonstration

1. Whoever interferes with an assembly or demonstration in a public place or open to the public, legally authorized, such as preventing or seeking to prevent that assembly or demonstration from taking place, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine.
2. An agent of police authority who prevents or seeks to prevent, in a situation not covered by legal conditionalism, a person from exercising his/her right to assembly or demonstration described in the preceding number shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years.

SECTION II

SEXUAL ASSAULT

Article 171

Sexual coercion

Whoever compels another person to suffer or commit, with him/her or with another person, a relevant sexual act, by violence, serious threat, or, for this purpose, has previously made that person lose consciousness or unable to resist, shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 8 years.

Article 172

Rape

Whoever, by the means referred to in the preceding article, has with another person vaginal coitus, anal coitus or oral coitus or compels that person to allow the insertion of any object into his/her anus or vagina shall be punished with imprisonment of 5 to 15 years.

Article 173

Aggravation

The perpetrator who commits the sexual assaults referred to in articles 171 and 172:

- a) By abusing authority arising from family, tutelary or curatorial relationship, hierarchical, economic or work dependency;
- b) By taking advantage of an office or place which, with any title, he/she exercises in a prison establishment, establishment for education or correction, hospital, hospice, asylum, clinic or other health establishment or other establishment for assistance or treatment;
- c) Against an unconscious or incapacitated person, particularly vulnerable due to illness, physical or mental disability;
- d) Against a victim under 17 years of age,

shall be punished with imprisonment of 4 to 12 years, in the case provided for in article 171, and with imprisonment of 5 to 20 years, in the case provided for in article 172.

SECTION III

SEXUAL EXPLOITATION

Article 174

Sexual exploitation of another person

1. Whoever, with the intention to obtain profit or uses the activity as his/her daily occupation, facilitates or, in any way, contributes to another person exercising prostitution or committing other sexual acts shall be punished with imprisonment of 3 to 10 years.
2. The perpetrator shall be punished with imprisonment of 4 to 12 years, when there also exist any of the following circumstances:

- a) Exploitation of the victim who is in abandonment or economic necessity;
- b) Violence, serious threat or coercion of the victim;
- c) Displacement of the victim from the country in which he/she lives or resides to another country;
- d) Retention of the victim's identification documents.

Article 175

Child prostitution

1. Whoever commits acts of sexual exploitation referred to in the preceding article against a person who is not yet 17 years old, even with the victim's consent, shall be punished with imprisonment of 4 to 12 years, in the case provided for in the preceding article's number 1, and with imprisonment of 5 to 15 years, in a case in which there exists any circumstance falling under the preceding article's number 2.
2. Whoever offers, obtains, seeks or delivers a person who is not yet 17 years old to child prostitution shall be punished with imprisonment of 4 to 12 years, when another legal provision does not establish a heavier penalty.

Article 176

Child pornography

1. A person who, for predominantly sexual purposes, exposes or represents a person who is not yet 17 years old engaging in sexual activity, real or simulated, or, by any means, exhibits that activity or the minor's sexual organs shall be punished with imprisonment of 3 to 10 years.
2. This penalty shall also be applied to a person who produces, shares, disseminates, imports, exports, sells, possesses means of communication, instruments, documents or records for the purposes referred to in the preceding number or to divulge those acts.

SECTION IV

SEXUAL ABUSE

Article 177

Sexual abuse of minors

1. Whoever commits vaginal coitus, anal coitus or oral coitus with a child who is not yet 14 years old shall be punished with imprisonment of 5 to 20 years.
2. Whoever commits a relevant sexual act with a child who is not yet 14 years old shall be punished with imprisonment of 5 to 15 years.

Article 178

Sexual acts with adolescent

An adult who, in a situation not covered by the provisions of this section, commits a relevant sexual act with a child aged 14 to 16, by abusing his/her inexperience shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 5 years.

Article 179

Sexual abuse of person unable to resist

Whoever commits a relevant sexual act with an unconscious or incapacitated person, particularly vulnerable due to illness, physical or mental disability, by taking advantage of his/her incapacity, shall be punished with imprisonment of 4 to 12 years.

Article 180

Sexual fraud

1. A person who, by taking advantage of a person's error concerning his/her identity, commits vaginal coitus, anal coitus or oral coitus or a relevant sexual act with that person, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years.
2. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

Article 181

Sexual exhibitionism

1. A person who publicly annoys another person by committing acts of a sexual character shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine.
2. This penalty shall also be received by a person who commits vaginal coitus, anal coitus or oral coitus in front of another person, against that person's will, even if in private.
3. Attempt gives rise to punishment.
4. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

SECTION V

COMMON PROVISIONS

Article 182

Aggravation

1. The penalties provided for in this chapter's sections II to section IV shall be aggravated by one third in their minimum and maximum limits when:

- a) The victim's age is less than 12 years at the moment when the perpetrator commits the act;
- b) The perpetrator transmits to the victim venereal, syphilitic disease or acquired immunodeficiency syndrome;
- c) Due to the act the victim dies, commits suicide or attempts to commit suicide;
- d) The victim is the perpetrator's descendant, collateral, relative or affine up to the second degree, adoptee or person who lives with him/her in a condition similar to these or in a relationship that gives rise to hierarchical, economic or work dependency.
- 2. When several circumstances provided for in the preceding number concur at once, only one of those circumstances is relevant as a circumstance that modifies the abstract framework of the legal type; the others enter into the evaluation to determine the concrete penalty.

CHAPTER V

AGAINST PRIVATE LIFE

Article 183

Disclosure

1. A person who, by even lawful means, learns facts about another person's private or sexual life and, without that person's consent and without just cause, publicly discloses those facts shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine.
2. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

Article 184

Violation of secrecy

1. A person who, without consent, reveals another person's secret which he/she learned by virtue of his/her status, office, employment, profession or art shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine.
2. When the secret concerns commercial, industrial, professional or artistic activity, which he/she learned in the circumstances described in the preceding number, and causes harm to another person or to the State, imprisonment of 2 years or a fine shall be applied.
3. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

Article 185

Violation of domicile

1. A person who enters another person's dwelling without consent or, having authorization to enter, remains in that dwelling when he/she has already received notification to leave, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine.
2. When, to commit the crime more easily, he/she uses nighttime, takes advantage of a dwelling that is in a deserted place, gathers with other persons until together they are three or more persons, uses a weapon, uses violence, threatens with violence or escalade, break-in or false key, the perpetrator shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine.
3. When at the moment when the perpetrator commits the crime a person is inside the house, the penalty provided for in the preceding number's minimum and maximum limits shall be increased by one third.
4. Attempt gives rise to punishment.
5. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

Article 186

Introduction into other place closed to the public

1. A person who commits the act described in the preceding article, numbers 1 and 2, in another place that is closed or fenced with walls and to which the public cannot freely enter shall be punished with the penalties provided for in those numbers, respectively, with their minimum and maximum limits reduced by one half.
2. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

Article 187

Violation of correspondence or telecommunication

1. A person who, without consent and in a case which procedural rules do not admit, opens a parcel, letter or other writing destined for another person or obtains knowledge of their content or does not let the addressee receive that parcel, letter or other writing shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine.
2. This penalty shall also be applied to a person who, in those circumstances, intercepts or learns the content of telephone, telegraph or other telecommunication communication.
3. A person who discloses the content of a letter, parcel, sealed writing, telephone call or other communication referred to in the preceding number, even if he/she learned the content by lawful means, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine.
4. When an official in the post, telegraph, telephone or telecommunication commits the acts which the preceding numbers describe the penalty's minimum and maximum limits shall be aggravated by one third.
5. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.

TITLE III

CRIMES AGAINST LIFE IN DEMOCRACY

CHAPTER I

CRIMES AGAINST PEACE AND PUBLIC

TRANQUILITY

Article 188

Criminal association

1. A person who promotes or establishes a group, organization or association which uses as its purpose or activity the commission of crimes shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 8 years.
2. A criminal group, organization or association shall be considered a gathering of two or more persons who, over a period of time, work together to commit or have another person commit crimes, in order to disturb public order or to directly or indirectly obtain any benefit or advantage.
3. A person who joins any criminal group, organization or association, supports any criminal group, organization or association, or participates in any criminal group, organization or association's activity shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years.
4. A person who leads or directs the groups, organizations or associations which the preceding numbers refer to shall be punished with imprisonment of 4 to 12 years.
5. The penalties which the preceding numbers deal with may receive extraordinary attenuation when the perpetrator prevents or endeavors strongly to prevent the group, organization or association from continuing or informs the authority of those groups' existence in order to enable the authority to prevent those groups from committing crimes.

Article 189

Instigation to crime

1. A person who, publicly and by any means, incites persons to commit any crime shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine.
2. A person who, in private or in public, praises or gives reward to a person who committed any crime in order to incite other persons to commit crimes such as those shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine.
3. When in the cases which the preceding numbers deal with a person has already committed the crime which the perpetrator wanted to incite persons to commit, that perpetrator shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 5 years, when another legal provision does not establish a heavier penalty.

Article 190

Participation in riot

1. Whoever participates in a public riot, in which persons collectively commit violence against persons or property, shall be punished with imprisonment of 1 year or a fine, when another legal provision does not establish a heavier penalty.
2. When the perpetrator provokes or directs the riot, he/she shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years.
3. If the riot is armed, the penalty referred to in the preceding numbers shall be increased by two times in its minimum and maximum limits.

Article 191

Prevention of exercise of political right

A person who, by violence, prevents another person from exercising his/her political right shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine.

Article 192

Trafficking in influence

1.A person who, by themselves or through another person, with their authorization or ratification, requests or receives, for themselves or for another person, a patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage or a promise of such advantage, in order to abuse influence, real or supposed, that they exercise over a public entity, shall be punished with:

- a) Imprisonment of 2 to 6 years, provided that another legal provision does not establish a heavier penalty, when their objective is to obtain a favorable unlawful decision; - b) Imprisonment of 1 year or a fine, provided that another legal provision does not establish a heavier penalty, when their objective is to obtain a favorable lawful decision. - 2. A person who, by themselves or using another person, with their authorization or ratification, gives or promises a patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage to the persons referred to in the previous number in order to achieve the purpose referred to in the previous number, paragraph a), shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 4 years or a fine. Article 193

Disobedience to an order to disperse

1. A person who does not comply with a legitimate order to leave a meeting or public gathering, which a legitimate authority gives, with warning that, should they not comply, they are committing a crime, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. 2. If the disobedient person is the one who promoted the meeting or gathering, they shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. Article 194

Abuse of public signals or uniforms

1. A person who abusively uses signals or calls for alarm or rescue, or pretends to make people believe that the help of others is needed due to disaster, danger or a situation of collective necessity shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine. 2. A person who unduly or abusively uses uniforms, military dress, attire or insignia that identify an activity, authority or public or international institution as a means to more easily commit an unlawful act shall also be punished with the same penalty. Article 195

Usurpation of function

1. A person who, without authorization, exercises functions or commits acts that only a public official, military command or public security force may commit, thus presenting themselves, expressly or tacitly, as a person having such capacity, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. A person who exercises a profession that, according to law, may only be exercised by persons holding a title or fulfilling certain conditions, thus presenting themselves, expressly or tacitly, as a person holding such title or fulfilling such conditions, shall also be punished with the same penalty. 3. A person who continues to exercise a public function despite having received official notification of their dismissal or suspension from such function shall also be punished with the same penalty. CHAPTER II

AGAINST STATE SECURITY

Article 196

Treason against the homeland

A person who, by violence, threat of violence, usurpation or abuse of sovereign functions, prevents or attempts to prevent the exercise of national sovereignty in the territory of Timor-Leste or in part of such territory or endangers the integrity of the national territory, as a means to submit or surrender to foreign sovereignty, shall be punished with imprisonment of 15 to 30 years. Article 197

Service or collaboration with enemy armed forces

1. A Timorese citizen who collaborates with a foreign country or group or with their representatives or serves under the flag of a foreign country at war or in armed action against Timor-Leste shall be punished with imprisonment of 15 to 25 years. 2. Preparatory acts for the facts described in the previous number shall give rise to imprisonment of 3 to 15 years. 3. A Timorese person or resident in the national territory who commits adequate acts to aid or facilitate armed action or war that a foreign country or group carries out against Timor-Leste shall be punished with imprisonment of 10 to 20 years. Article 198

Sabotage of national defense

A person who, with the intention of prejudicing or endangering national defense, destroys, damages or renders totally or partially unusable:
