- a) Works or materials of the armed forces or works or materials allocated to the armed forces; - b) Routes or means of communication or transport; - c) Any installation related to communication or transport; or
- d) Factories and depots, shall be punished with imprisonment of 5 to 15 years. Article 199

Campaign against peace efforts

A Timorese person or resident in the national territory who, in time of preparation for or conduct of war, disseminates, by any means, to the general public, rumors or statements, their own or those of others, which they know to be false, in whole or in part, in order to prejudice Timor-Leste's peace efforts or to aid a foreign enemy, shall be punished with imprisonment of 5 to 15 years. Article 200

Violation of State secrets

1. A person who makes known to an unauthorized person or publicly discloses facts, documents, plans, objects, knowledge or information of any nature which, due to the interest of the Timorese State concerning external security or the conduct of foreign policy, must not be known by a foreign country, thus endangering such interest, shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 10 years. 2. A person who collaborates with a foreign government or group with the intention of committing the facts referred to in the previous number or recruits or assists persons who must commit such facts shall be punished with the penalty provided for in the previous number. 3. When the agent who commits the facts described in the previous number exercises political, public or military functions which, by their nature, must prevent them from committing such facts more strongly than ordinary citizens, imprisonment of 5 to 15 years shall be applied. Article 201

Diplomatic infidelity

A person who, officially representing the Timorese State, with the intention of prejudicing national rights or interests:

- a) Conducts State business with a foreign government or international organization; or
- b) Accepts commitments in the name of Timor-Leste without authorization to accept them, shall be punished with imprisonment of 5 to 15 years. Article 202

Alteration of the constitutional State

1. A person who, by violence, threat of violence or incitement to civil war, seeks to overthrow, change or subvert the constitutional State established by the constitution shall be punished with imprisonment of 5 to 15 years. 2. A person who commits the fact described in the previous number with armed violence must be punished with imprisonment of 5 to 20 years. 3. A person who makes public incitement or distributes weapons to anyone to commit the facts described above shall be punished, respectively, with the penalty for attempt. Article 203

Attack against the highest representative of a sovereign body

1. A person who commits an attack against the highest representative of a sovereign body or a person who, according to the constitution, substitutes them, or a person whose appointment has already been announced but who has not yet taken office in such position, shall be punished with imprisonment of 8 to 20 years. 2. When a crime against life, physical integrity or liberty is consummated, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment of 12 to 25 years. 3. The penalty provided for in the previous numbers shall also be applied to a person who commits the facts already described against a foreigner in the situation provided for in number 1, ambassadors and persons serving as directing bodies of international organizations, when they are in Timor-Leste. Article 204

Coercion against constitutional bodies

1. A person who, by violence or threat of violence, prevents or restricts a sovereign body from freely exercising its functions shall be punished with imprisonment of 3 to 10 years. 2. A person who commits the fact described in the previous number against district or local government bodies shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years. 3. When a person commits the fact described in number 1 against a member of such bodies, the maximum and minimum limits of the penalties referred to in numbers 1 and 2 shall be reduced by half. Article 205

Disturbance of the functioning of constitutional bodies

A person who, by tumult, disorder or shouting, unlawfully disturbs the functioning of the bodies referred to in the previous number or a person sitting in such bodies to exercise their functions shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine. Article 206

Outrage against national symbols

A person who publicly, by word, gesture or written dissemination or by means of communication with the general public, offends the national flag or anthem, the arms or emblem of Timorese sovereignty or shows lack of due respect for such symbols shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. CHAPTER III

AGAINST LIFE IN SOCIETY

SECTION I

COMMON DANGER CRIMES

Article 207

Driving without a license

A person who uses a motorized vehicle without the license required by law shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. Article 208

Driving under the influence of alcohol or psychotropic substances

1. A person who, at least negligently, drives a motorized vehicle when they have 1.2g of alcohol in each liter of their blood shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. 2. A person who, at least negligently, drives a motorized vehicle when they are unable to drive safely because they are under the influence of narcotics or under the influence of analogous products that cause physical, mental or psychological disturbance. Article 209

Dangerous driving

1. A person who drives a motorized vehicle on a public road and, due to being unable to drive safely or due to grossly violating road traffic rules, endangers the life or physical integrity of others shall be punished with imprisonment of 1 to 4 years. 2. Negligence regarding the conduct or danger shall give rise to imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. Article 210

Attack on transport safety

1. A person who commits adequate acts to destroy or reduce safety in means of transport and, thus, endangers the life or physical integrity of others shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 8 years. 2. Number 2 of the previous article also applies here. Article 211

Prohibited weapons

1. A person who, in situations not authorized by law, manufactures, imports, transports, sells or gives to others firearms, chemical weapons, biological weapons, nuclear weapons, ammunition for such weapons, substances to manufacture such weapons or for their functioning, or other types of explosives shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years. 2. When the facts described in the previous number have the purpose of using such weapons in war, imprisonment of 2 to 8 years shall be applied. 3. An agent who, without legal authorization, possesses, uses or holds firearms shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. Article 212

Intoxication and poisoning

1. A person who, at least through negligence, renders themselves non-imputable by drinking or consuming alcoholic beverages or toxic substances and in such state commits a typical unlawful act shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 5 years or a fine. 2. The penalty must not be heavier than that provided for the typical unlawful act that the agent committed. 3. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 213

Qualification to exercise certain activities

1. A person who, without legal qualification, sells, administers, prescribes or gives, in any form, to others, habitually, pharmaceutical or other products whose commerce and prescription, administration and dispensing may only be done by health professionals or entities with due license shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. When the facts referred to in the previous number endanger the life of others, imprisonment of 1 to 4 years shall be applied. Article 214

Adulterated or deteriorated products

1. A person who places for people to buy, administers or gives, in any form, food, pharmaceutical or other products, which, due to being deteriorated, adulterated or contaminated, may endanger life shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 8 years. 2. When the facts described in the previous number cause the victim's death due to consuming such products, the penalty shall be 3 to 12 years. SECTION II

CRIMES AGAINST THE ENVIRONMENT

Article 215

Against the environment

1. A person who, contrary to legal or regulatory provisions protecting the environment, provokes or causes, directly or indirectly, emission, discharge, radiation, extraction or excavation, landfill, noise, vibration, injection or deposit in the atmosphere, in soil, subsoil or terrestrial, maritime or underground water, including in border zones, or water abstraction, which may seriously prejudice the balance of natural systems, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. When the agent intentionally releases, emits or introduces ionizing radiation or other substances into the air, land or maritime, continental, surface or underground water, in quantities that cause one or more persons to suffer serious bodily harm requiring medical or surgical treatment or causing irreversible sequelae, they shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 8 years, and, if causing someone's death, they shall be punished with imprisonment of 5 to 15 years. Article 216

Aggravation

1. When an industrial or commercial establishment commits the activities provided for in the previous article and any of the following circumstances exist:

- a) The industry or commercial activity operates clandestinely without license or administrative authorization; - b) Does not comply with the express order of the competent administrative authority to correct or suspend the activity referred to in the previous article; - c) Does not comply with safety rules or procedures provided for in legal or regulatory provisions; - d) By any means intentionally prevents the inspection process of the competent administrative authority or falsifies information about the consequences of the industry or commercial activity; - e) Produces irreversible or catastrophic environmental deterioration, the maximum and minimum limits of the penalty provided for in the previous number shall be increased by one third. - 2. Criminal liability shall also be borne, under the previous number, by the person sitting as individual owner, the person who legally represents the collective person owning the offending industrial or commercial establishment, the person acting as legal representative of such collective person, and also, in irregularly constituted companies, the partners or members who authorized such actions. Article 217

Against flora and fauna

1. A person who, with serious prejudice to the environment, cuts, burns, picks, collects or illegally traffics in flora species classified as threatened or endangered, or their seeds, by destroying or seriously altering their natural habitat, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. The penalty provided for in the previous number shall also be applied to a person who introduces or facilitates the entry of non-indigenous species and, thus, prejudices the biological balance, contrary to law or provisions of a general nature to protect fauna or flora species. Article 218

Threatened or endangered species

1. A person who hunts or fishes threatened species or commits activities that prevent the development of such species or make their reproduction or migration difficult, contrary to law or provisions of a general nature to protect wild fauna species, or commercializes or traffics in such species, in whole or in part, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. An agent who commits the acts referred to in the previous number:

- a) In terrestrial or maritime zones declared protected natural zones; - b) Against species or subspecies classified as endangered shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 5 years or a fine. Article 219

Illegal fishing

1. A person who engages in fishing in national maritime waters without a license for fishing from the competent administrative authority shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. When the agent is a collective person, criminal liability shall be borne, under the previous number, by the person who, by law, represents such collective person, the person acting as legal representative of such collective person, and also, in irregularly constituted companies, the partners or members who authorized such actions. 3. Fishing for domestic subsistence shall not be punished under number 1. Article 220

Unlawful means of fishing

A person who uses firearms, explosives, toxic substances or other instruments or similar arts with destructive efficacy for marine fauna to capture fish resources in national terrestrial or maritime waters shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 5 years or a fine. Article 221

Prohibited burning

1. A person who, in a season not for burning or without necessary administrative authorization, carries out burning that causes destruction of forests, plantations or crops shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. 2. An agent who carries out legal burning but, through negligence, provokes the prejudice referred to in the previous number shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine. SECTION III

OTHER CRIMES

Article 222

Prevention or disturbance of procession, ceremony or worship

1. A person who, by violence or threat of violence or coercion of any form, prevents or disturbs the performance of a procession or funeral ceremony or the exercise of religious worship shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. 2. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 223

Profanation of objects or places of worship or veneration

1. A person who, in order to cause alarm or disturb social peace, profanes a place or object of religious worship or veneration shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. 2. A person who offends or insults others because of their faith or religious function, in a manner likely to cause alarm or social disturbance shall be punished with the same penalty. 3. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 224

Destruction, theft, concealment or profanation of corpse

1. A person who steals, destroys or conceals a corpse or cremated remains or the ashes of a corpse, against the will of the person having rights or without the will of such person and in situations not authorized by law, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. 2. A person who profanes a corpse or cremated remains or the ashes of a corpse by acts that show lack of respect for the deceased shall be punished with the same penalty. 3. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 4. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 225

Failure to comply with maintenance obligation

1. A person who has the obligation to provide maintenance and has the condition to comply with such obligation but does not comply with such obligation to the point of endangering the person entitled to maintenance from lacking means to satisfy their fundamental needs, even though another person's help has removed such danger, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 226

Abduction of minor

1. A person who abducts or refuses to return a minor to the person who assumed the function of supervising such minor or causes such minor to run away shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. If such facts are done with violence or threat of violence, imprisonment of 1 to 4 years shall be applied. 3. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 227

Failure to provide assistance

1. A person who, in time of grave necessity, namely due to disaster, accident, public calamity or common danger, which endangers the life, physical integrity or liberty of others, does not provide necessary assistance to remove such danger, by personal action or promotion of assistance, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine. 2. When the person who omits necessary assistance is the one who caused the situation provided for in the previous number, such omitter shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. 3. The omission of assistance shall not give rise to punishment when there is grave danger to the omitter's life or physical integrity or when, due to another relevant reason, such assistance cannot be required from them. 4. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 228

Refusal of medical assistance

A doctor or health professional who refuses assistance in their profession in a situation where there is danger to the life of others or serious danger to the physical integrity of others, for which there is no other way to remove it, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. CHAPTER IV

ELECTORAL CRIMES

Article 229

Fraud in census

1. A person who prevents another person, whom they know has the right, from registering themselves, enters facts they know to be false, omits facts they must record or, by any means, falsifies the electoral census shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. When the agent uses violence or provokes cunning deceit to prevent someone from registering themselves, imprisonment of 2 to 6 years shall be applied. 3. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 230

Obstruction of candidacy

A person who, by any means, prevents another person, party or political force, whom they know has the right, from running in an electoral act shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 8 years. Article 231

Ineligible candidate

1. A person who presents their candidacy knowing that they do not have the capacity to be elected shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine. 2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 232

Fault in electoral register

A person who, being charged with preparing the electoral register or guaranteeing its correctness, does not prepare or make such correction or prevents their legal substitute from doing so, thereby preventing the electoral act from being held, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. Article 233

Unlawful electoral propaganda

1. A person who uses means of propaganda prohibited by law or continues propaganda when the established period has already passed, or conducts propaganda in a prohibited place shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine. 2. A person who prevents another person from exercising their right to conduct electoral propaganda or unlawfully destroys electoral propaganda shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. Article 234

Obstruction of freedom to choose

1. A person who by violence, threat of violence or fraudulent artifice compels another person, against their will, not to vote or to vote in a determined direction or buys or sells votes shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. The same penalty shall also be applied to a person who, charged with assisting a blind person or a person entitled to such assistance, does not respect the direction of the vote communicated to them. 3. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 235

Disturbance of electoral act

1. A person who, by any means, disturbs the functioning of a voting assembly shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine. 2. The agent shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years when they cause such disturbance:

- a) By violence or threat of violence; - b) By tumult or popular gathering near the voting assembly; - c) By intentional cutting of electrical power; - d) By the absence of persons indispensable to such act when such absence causes great difficulty for the electoral act to begin or continue. 3. The provisions of the previous numbers shall also be applied to an agent who commits such facts when, after voting has ended, the counting of results is ongoing. Article 236

Obstruction of oversight of electoral act

1. A person who, in any form, prevents the representative of a legally constituted party or political force and competitor in the electoral act from exercising their competence to conduct oversight shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 237

Fraud in voting

1. A person who votes despite not having the right to vote or votes repeatedly despite having already voted in such electoral act shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. A person who intentionally allows someone to commit the facts provided for in the previous number shall be punished with the same penalty. 3. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 238

Fraud in scrutiny

1. A person who, in any form, falsifies the count of votes in the act of ascertaining or publishing electoral results shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years. 2. A person who, with fraudulent intent, substitutes, destroys, suppresses, violates, falsifies or conceals ballots or the count or documents concerning the election shall be punished with the same penalty. Article 239

Refusal of electoral office

A person who, having been appointed to sit at a voting assembly table and, unjustifiably, refuses to assume or abandons such function shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine. Article 240

Violation of secrecy in scrutiny

A person who in an electoral act with secret scrutiny violates secrecy by taking or disseminating knowledge about whom another person voted for shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 1 year or a fine. Article 241

Duty of neutrality or impartiality

A public official in the electoral administration or collaborator of the electoral administration who violates their duty of neutrality and impartiality shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. Article 242

Aggravation

When a person who commits any crime provided for in this chapter performs a public function, namely in government, national parliament, or armed forces, as a judicial magistrate or in the Public Prosecutor's Office, in police forces or other administrative bodies, the penalty provided for in each type shall be doubled at its minimum and maximum limits. CHAPTER V

AGAINST PUBLIC AUTHORITY

Article 243

Obstruction of public authority

1. A person who, by violence or serious threat against a public official or agent of military, militarized or police forces, does not allow them to commit an act concerning their function or makes them commit an act against their duty shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years. 2. When the act referred to in the previous number is actually done or actually prevented, imprisonment of 2 to 8 years shall be applied. Article 244

Disobedience

1. A person who does not comply or continues not to comply with a legitimate order or mandate regularly communicated, which a competent authority or official gives, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine:

- a) When a legal provision so determines; or
- b) When such authority or official informs the agent that, should they not comply with such order or mandate, they will have criminal liability. - 2. In situations where a legal provision qualifies a fact as qualified disobedience, imprisonment of 1 to 4 years shall be applied. - 3. Disobedience to a concrete prohibition or interdiction that a criminal sentence determines and also disobedience to an accessory penalty or security measure that does not deprive liberty shall give rise to the penalty referred to in number 1. Article 245 Freeing a prisoner

1. A person who, by illegal means, frees a person who has been legally deprived of their liberty or, by any means, helps such person to escape shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years. 2. When such facts are committed with violence, weapons or with the collaboration of more than two persons, imprisonment of 2 to 8 years shall be applied. Article 246

Escape

1. A person who, by law, has been deprived of their liberty and has escaped shall be punished with imprisonment of 1 to 4 years. 2. When escape is achieved by means described in the previous article, number 2, imprisonment of 2 to 6 years shall be applied. Article 247

Official's assistance in escape

1. An official who helps someone commit the facts provided for in the previous articles shall be punished with the penalty established therein plus one third at its minimum and maximum limits. 2. When the official who has the obligation to hold or conduct surveillance of the escapee is the one who provides assistance to commit such facts, the maximum and minimum limits of the provided penalties shall be further increased by half. 3. When escape occurs due to gross negligence of the official who must guard the escapee, imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine shall be applied. Article 248

Prisoner mutiny

1. Persons who, having been deprived of their liberty by law, having agreed and joining their efforts with persons in the same circumstances as theirs, attack or threaten with violence persons who have the obligation to watch or guard them, in order to obtain their escape or the escape of others or to force such person to commit some act or not commit some act, shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 8 years. 2. When by the conduct described in the previous number some of the agents or some other persons actually escape, imprisonment of 3 to 10 years shall be applied. Article 249

Diversion or destruction of objects in public power's hands

A person who destroys, damages or renders unusable, in whole or in part, or in any form, steals from public power which received or controls documents or other movable objects, or things arrested, seized or subject to precautionary measures shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 6 years, when another rule does not give rise to a heavier penalty. Article 250

Breaking of seal, stamp or public notice

1. A person who opens, breaks or renders unusable, in whole or in part, a seal or stamp, which a competent official legitimately placed to identify or make inviolable something or to certify that arrest, seizure or precautionary measure has already fallen upon something, shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. A person who, in any form, intentionally, prevents the addressee from knowing a public notice that a competent official has posted shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 2 years or a fine. TITLE IV

CRIMES AGAINST PROPERTY

CHAPTER I

AGAINST OWNERSHIP

Article 251

Simple theft

1. A person who, with unlawful intention to make theirs or another's, steals another person's movable thing shall be punished with imprisonment of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 3. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 252

Aggravated theft

1. An agent who commits the acts referred to in the previous article shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 8 years, when:

- a) The movable thing of another person has great scientific, artistic or historical value or something important for economic or technological development; - b) The movable thing of another person is a vehicle, which a vehicle or passengers in a collective vehicle are carrying, or held at a dock or station for embarkation or disembarkation; - c) The movable thing of another person is something to be used in religious worship or in veneration of the memory of the deceased and held in a place for worship or in a cemetery; - d) The agent takes advantage of nighttime to commit theft more easily; - e) The victim suffers considerable prejudice from such appropriation; - f) The agent enters a dwelling, public service installation, commercial or industrial establishment with the intention of committing theft; - g) The agent uses false keys, climbing or breaking in to realize their intention; - h) The agent takes advantage of the victim's special vulnerability in disaster, accident or public calamity;- i) The agent takes advantage of the special trust that arises from their relationship with the victim or with the place where the subtracted thing is kept by its owner;
- j) The agent commits theft as their daily occupation128;
- k) Three or more persons, including129 the agent, commit the crime;
- l) The subtracted thing has a value exceeding 1,000 American dollars but not exceeding 5,000.

128 (252) Commits theft as their daily occupation = Commits theft as a way of life.
129 (252) Including (v) – Together with. Three persons, including the agent = three persons, together with the agent.

2. When the subtracted thing has a value exceeding 5,000 American dollars, a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years shall be applied.

3. When several of the circumstances referred to in number 1 accompany one conduct, only the circumstance that has the strongest aggravating effect shall be considered in order to determine the abstract framework of the applicable legal type, and the other circumstances shall be valued as circumstances of a general character in order to determine the measure of the sentence.

4. When the stolen thing has a value less than 50 American dollars, the circumstances set out in number 1 function only as aggravating circumstances of a general character.

Article 253

Robbery

1. Anyone who, with illegitimate intent to make their own or another's, subtracts or compels the delivery to them of another's movable thing, with violence against a person, threat with imminent danger130 to the person's life or physical integrity, or placing the person in a situation where they cannot resist, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years.

2. When, together with the agent's conduct, any of the circumstances set out in the previous article, number 1, also occurs, a prison sentence of 4 to 12 years shall be applied.

3. When their conduct creates danger to the victim's life or serious offense to the victim's physical integrity, the agent shall be punished with a prison sentence of 5 to 15 years.

4. When their conduct causes someone's death, the agent shall be punished with a prison sentence of 5 to 20 years.

Article 254

Violence following subtraction

Anyone who, having just been caught committing theft, acts as set out in the previous article in order to retain the appropriated thing or prevent the appropriated thing 130 (253) Imminent (adj) - That which is about to occur immediately from being restored shall be punished with the sentences established for the crime of robbery, respectively.

Article 255

Theft for use of vehicle

1. Anyone who uses an automobile or motorized vehicle, aircraft, boat or bicycle without the authorization of the person who has the right shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine.

2. Attempt is punishable.

3. A complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

Article 256

Abuse of trust

1. Anyone who, illegitimately, makes their own a movable thing that was delivered to them under a title131 that does not transfer ownership shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine.

2. Attempt is punishable.

3. A complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

Article 257

Aggravated abuse of trust

1. When the movable thing has a value exceeding 1,000 American dollars, the agent shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years.

2. The minimum and maximum limits of the sentence set out in the previous number and in article 256 shall be increased by one third when the agent received the thing for deposit by order of law, by reason of their office, employment or profession, or as guardian, curator or depositary.

Article 258

Simple damage

1. Anyone who, totally or partially, destroys, damages or renders unusable another's thing shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine.

131 (256) Title (n) – Quality; basis

2. Attempt is punishable.

3. A complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

Article 259

Aggravated damage

1. Anyone who, totally or partially, destroys, damages or renders unusable a thing:

- a) That is for public use or of public utility;
- b) Of important scientific, artistic or historical value or of great importance for technological or scientific development;
- c) Concerning means of communication or transport of great social importance;
- d) Thus causing damage exceeding American dollars
- e) Belonging to another, that is for use in religious worship or to venerate the memory of the deceased and kept in a place of worship or in a cemetery, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years.

Article 260

Damage with violence

When the agent commits the facts set out in articles 258 and 259 with violence against someone or with threat of imminent danger to life or physical integrity, or by placing the person in a situation where they cannot resist, a prison sentence of 4 to 12 years shall be applied.

Article 261

Usurpation of immovable property

1. Anyone who, with violence or serious threat against another, invades or occupies another's immovable thing, with intent to exercise a right of ownership, possession, use or easement that is not protected132 by law, judgment, contract or administrative act, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 1 to 4 years.

132 (261) Protects (v) - Protects

2. When the means used by the agent constitutes a crime punishable with a heavier sentence than that imposed133 by the previous number, that sentence shall be applied.

Article 262

Alteration of boundary markers

1. Anyone who, with intent to make their own or another's, totally or partially, another's immovable thing, uproots or alters a boundary marker or any sign intended to establish boundaries concerning ownership of immovable property shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 1 year or a fine.

2. A complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

Article 263

Arson

1. Anyone who, intentionally, causes a fire in a house, building, establishment, means of transport, forest, plantation or other property and, thereby, creates danger to life, physical integrity or patrimonial property with a value exceeding 5,000 American dollars shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years.

2. When the facts set out in the previous number concern public property or property in which a public service operates, a prison sentence of 2 to 10 years shall be applied.

3. If the agent commits these facts through negligence, they shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine, when another legal provision does not provide for a heavier sentence.

4. When only the danger referred to in number 1 arises through negligence, a sentence of 2 to 6 years shall be applied.

5. In the cases provided for in number 4, a complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

Article 264

Active repentance

When, having committed the crimes provided for in articles 251, 252, 256 to 261 and 263, n. 4, but before the trial hearing begins, the agent commits any act to repair, 133 (261) Imposes (v) – Provides; establishes integrally or partially, the damage they caused, the sentence may be subject to extraordinary mitigation.

Article 265

Definition

For the purposes of this code, the following shall be considered:

- a) "Breaking" the breaking134, fracture or destruction, total or partial, of a device that serves to close or prevent entry, from the outside or inside, to a house or to an enclosed place that depends on that house;
- b) "Scaling" entry into a house or enclosed place that depends on that house, through a place not ordinarily used as an entrance or through a device to close or prevent entry or passage;
- c) False key
  - i) A key that is imitated, counterfeited or altered;
  - ii) A genuine key when, fortuitously135 or surreptitiously, it leaves the hands of the person who has the right to use it; and
  - iii) A lock pick136 or instrument that can be used to open a lock or other security device.

CHAPTER II

AGAINST PATRIMONY IN GENERAL

Article 266

Simple fraud

1. Anyone who, with intent to obtain illegitimate enrichment for themselves or for another, through error or deception concerning a fact that they astutely provoked, makes another commit an act that causes patrimonial damage to that person or to another shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine.

2. Attempt is punishable.

134 (256) Breaking (n) – Act that breaks something
135 (265) Fortuitously (adv) – Casually; accidentally
136 (265) Lock pick (n) – Metal instrument to open locks or padlocks

3. The provision of article 264 also applies here.

4. A complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

Article 267

Aggravated fraud

1. Anyone who, through their conduct described in the previous article:

- a) Causes damage with a value exceeding 2,000 American dollars;
- b) Commits fraud as their daily occupation;
- c) Places the victim in a difficult economic situation, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years.

2. The provision of article 264 also applies here.

Article 268

Computer fraud

1. Anyone who, with intent to obtain illegitimate enrichment for themselves or for another, causes patrimonial damage to another, by interfering with the result of computer data processing or making incorrect structuring of a computer program, not using data correctly or completely, using data without authorization or intervening in any way in processing without authorization, shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to

3 years or a fine.

2. Attempt is punishable.

3. A complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

Article 269

Aggravated computer fraud

When the conduct described in the previous article causes any of the circumstances described in article 267, n. 1, the agent shall be punished with a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years.

Article 270

Extortion

1. Anyone who, with intent to obtain for themselves or for another illegitimate enrichment, compels another, with violence or threat of important harm, to make a patrimonial disposition137 that causes damage to that person or to another, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years.

2. When any circumstance provided for in article 252 occurs, the agent shall be punished with the sentence provided for in that article.

3. A complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

Article 271

Simple receiving

1. Anyone who, without seeking to know whether or not it has legitimate provenance, acquires or receives, under any title, a thing which, by its nature or by the person who has, holds or offers it, or by its price or condition concerning its sale or offer, a person of ordinary care would have reason to suspect that the thing comes from criminal conduct against another's patrimony shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine.

2. A complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

Article 272

Aggravated receiving

1. Anyone who, with intent to obtain patrimonial advantage for themselves or for another, knowing that the thing was obtained by another through a crime against patrimony, receives, gives as pledge, makes their own under any title, holds, keeps, delivers or contributes to delivering the thing to another, or in any way seeks means for the thing or its value or product that comes directly from the thing to remain in their hands or another's hands shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years.

2. A prison sentence of 3 to 10 years shall be imposed on an agent who commits the facts set out in the previous number when:

137 (270) Patrimonial disposition – a person makes a patrimonial disposition when they deliver to another some patrimonial property.

a) They commit receiving as their daily occupation to live or habitually;
b) The property, value or product has a value138 exceeding 2,000 American dollars;
c) At the time of committing the receiving they knew that the crime was committed in any of the circumstances set out in article 253, n. 1.

Article 273

Assistance to criminal

1. Anyone who, after a crime against patrimony, assists the agent of that crime to benefit from the thing they obtained as such or benefit that comes directly from the appropriated thing shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 1 year or a fine.

2. A complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

Article 274

Harmful administration

1. Anyone who, when under an obligation to direct or administer another's interest, service or patrimonial property, even if they are also a partner of the company or legal person that owns the interests, services or properties, causes economically significant patrimonial damage by intentionally violating rules concerning control or management or acting with serious violation of the duty concerning their function, shall be punished with a prison sentence of

1 to 4 years.

2. If the property, interest or service referred to in the previous number belongs to the State, a legal person of public utility, a cooperative or popular association, the agent shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years.

3. These sentences shall also be applied to anyone who makes their own or allows another to make their own, illegitimately, a thing that they can direct only according to the purpose that the person who administers another's patrimony must follow.

Article 275

138 (272) Value (n) - Price

Negligent administration

1. Anyone who, under the conditions described in number 1 of the previous article, causes patrimonial damage through not acting with the care that, according to the circumstances accompanying their conduct and as they are able, they are obliged to use, shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 1 year or a fine.

2. If the property or interest is that set out in number 2 of the previous article, a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine shall be applied.

3. A complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

Article 276

Intentional bankruptcy or insolvency

1. Anyone who, by any means, leads a company into bankruptcy or places it in insolvency, with intent to harm creditors, when that bankruptcy or insolvency is declared, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to

8 years.

2. When the fact described in the previous number concerns a public enterprise or cooperative, the minimum and maximum limits of the sentence shall be increased by one third.

Article 277

Negligent bankruptcy or insolvency

1. Anyone who causes bankruptcy139 or insolvency140 through serious carelessness141 or imprudence142, manifest prodigality143 or exaggerated expenditure, or through gross negligence in their activity, when that bankruptcy or insolvency is declared, shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine.

139 (277) Bankruptcy (n) – Situation in which a merchant or commercial company enters when they cannot fulfill their obligations because debts are greater than their patrimony

140 (277) Insolvency (n) – Situation in which someone enters when they cannot pay their debts because their patrimony is not sufficient to pay.

141 (277) Carelessness (n) – Lack of care

142 (277) Imprudence (n) – Lack of care; careless act; not thinking beforehand (before doing something)

143 (277) Prodigality (n) – The quality of a person who spends arbitrarily

2. A complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings.

TITLE V

CRIMES AGAINST THE ADMINISTRATION OF JUSTICE

Article 278

Falsehood in testimony or declaration

1. Anyone who, in testimony as a party, having taken an oath and having received a warning concerning the penal consequences that will fall on them for false testimony, gives false declaration concerning a fact about which they must give testimony shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine.

2. A suspect or accused shall be punished with the same sentence for a declaration concerning their identity and criminal record.

3. When, through the conduct described in the previous numbers, someone loses their liberty, the agent shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years.

Article 279

Falsehood in testimony, expertise, interpretation or translation

1. Anyone who, as a witness, expert, technician, translator or interpreter before a court or before an official competent to receive as means of proof testimony, report, information or translation, gives testimony, presents a report, gives information or makes a false translation shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 4 years or a fine.

2. Anyone who refuses to give testimony or present a report, information or translation when they do not have just cause144 shall be punished with the same sentence.

3. When, having taken an oath and having received a warning concerning the penal consequences that will fall on them, the agent commits the fact referred to in n. 1, a prison sentence of up to 5 years shall be applied.

144 (279) Cause (n) – Reason. Just cause – Reason that justifies someone not doing or not committing some act or not committing the act within the time period.

4. When the conduct set out in the previous numbers causes someone to lose their liberty, the agent shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years.

Article 280

Equivalence to desistance

The repentance and retraction of an agent who commits any fact described in the previous article before the falsehood is considered in a decision or the falsehood causes damage to another is equivalent to desistance.

Article 281

Bribery

1. Anyone who convinces145 or attempts146 to convince another, through a gift or promise concerning patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage, to commit the fact referred to in article 278 or 279 and that person indeed commits that fact, shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 4 years.

2. When the fact referred to in articles 278 and 279 is not actually committed, the agent shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine.

Article 282

Denial of justice

1. An official who, in procedural inquiry, jurisdictional process, proceeding for contraventions or disciplinary matters, knowingly, decides or does not decide, promotes or does not promote, investigates or does not investigate or commits an act in their function, contrary to law, shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine.

2. When an official commits the fact described in the previous number with intent to harm or benefit someone, they shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 5 years.

145 (281) Convinces (v) – Uses strong rational argument or logical argument to make another follow one's thinking; persuades; leads; convinces.

146 (281) Attempts to convince (v) – Pressures someone to convince them to do something; endeavors to do or achieve something.

3. When the conduct described in the previous numbers causes someone to lose their liberty, the agent shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years.

4. When the agent commits the previous conduct through gross negligence, the minimum and maximum limits of the sentence shall be reduced to their half.

Article 283

Coercion of magistrate

1. Anyone who, by violence, threat of important harm or by any means prevents a judge or public prosecutor from freely exercising their function shall be punished with a prison sentence of

1 to 4 years.

2. When, in order to commit these facts, the agent uses a political, public, military or police position that they hold, a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years shall be applied.

3. When, through the conduct described in the previous numbers, a magistrate omits or commits any act contrary to express law and that act causes damage to another, a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years shall be applied.

Article 284

Obstruction of jurisdictional activity

1. Anyone who, by any means, opposes, creates difficulty or obstructs a judicial decision with res judicata from being complied with or executed shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 5 years.

2. When the agent who commits the facts provided for in the previous numbers exercises a political, public or military function which, by its nature, should prevent them from committing those facts, they shall be punished with a prison sentence of

2 to 8 years.

Article 285

Calumnious denunciation

1. Anyone who, by any means, before an authority or publicly, with knowledge that the imputation is false, denounces or spreads suspicion that someone committed a crime, with intent to subject that person to criminal proceedings shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine.

2. When the agent makes false imputation concerning a contravention or disciplinary offense, the sentence shall be subject to extraordinary mitigation.

3. When an official who has an obligation to conduct criminal proceedings wilfully promotes the fact described in the previous numbers, the minimum and maximum limits of the applicable sentence shall be increased by one third.

Article 286

Non-reporting

Anyone who, having knowledge that someone committed a public crime and having an obligation to make a report, does not report it shall be punished with the sentence concerning that crime they became aware of with its minimum and maximum limits reduced by two thirds.

Article 287

Prevarication of magistrate or official

1. A judge, public prosecutor or official who, in any phase of a jurisdictional process, with intent to benefit or harm another, commits any act that falls within their functional power or does not commit an act that they have an obligation to commit shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years.

2. When the fact described in the previous number causes someone to lose their liberty or causes illegal imprisonment or detention, a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years shall be applied.

Article 288

Prevarication of lawyer or public defender

1. A lawyer or public defender who intentionally harms a case entrusted to their advocacy shall be punished with a prison sentence of 1 to 4 years.

2. A lawyer or public defender who, in a single case, advocates or exercises defense for persons whose interests are in conflict, with intent to benefit or harm some of them, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years.

Article 289

Simulation of crime

1. Anyone who, without saying who committed it, denounces a crime or makes a competent authority suspect that someone committed a crime, when they know that there was no crime, shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine.

2. If this fact concerns a contravention, contraordination or disciplinary offense, the agent shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 1 year or a fine.

3. When an official who has an obligation to institute proceedings concerning the facts set out in the previous numbers commits those facts, the minimum and maximum limits of the applicable sentence shall be increased by one third.

Article 290

Personal favoritism

1. Anyone who, totally or partially, prevents, frustrates147 or eludes the authority's evidentiary or preventive activity, with intent or knowledge to avoid the person who committed a crime from being punished or receiving a security measure, shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine.

2. Attempt is punishable.

3. When an official who intervenes or has competence to intervene in the process or has an obligation to execute a sentence or security measure or to order its execution commits that favoritism, a prison sentence of 2 to 5 years shall be applied.

4. The acts set out in number 1 do not give rise to punishment when:

- a) The agent commits that fact also to avoid a sentence or security measure from being applied or executed on them;
- b) The person that the agent wishes to favor with this conduct is the agent's spouse, descendant, ascendant, adopter, adopted or relative or in-law up to 147 (290) Frustrates – makes something not produce its result or effect; makes something fail the second degree, or a person who lives with them as husband or wife.

Article 291

Violation of secrecy of justice

1. Anyone who, contrary to legal determination and without just cause, releases to the public the content of an act of penal procedure that falls under secrecy of justice or an act whose publicity a decision has already prohibited, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 1 to 4 years.

2. When the agent commits that violation through social communication media, a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years shall be applied.

TITLE VI

CRIMES IN THE EXERCISE OF PUBLIC FUNCTION

Article 292

Passive corruption for illicit act

1. An official who, themselves or through another, with their authorization or ratification, requests or receives, for themselves or for another, patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage or promise of that advantage, even if they do not have a right to it, in order to commit or not commit any act contrary to the obligation they have due to the position they hold, even if the act or omission has already occurred before the request or acceptance arises, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 3 to 15 years.

2. When the agent, before committing that fact, voluntarily refuses the offer or promise they had already received or returns the advantage, or, if the advantage is a fungible thing, its value, they shall not be punished.

3. The sentence shall be subject to special mitigation when the agent indeed helps obtain decisive proof to identify or capture other responsible persons.

Article 293

Passive corruption for licit act

1. An official who, themselves or through another, with their authorization or ratification, requests or receives, for themselves or for another, patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage or promise of that advantage, even if they do not have a right to it, in order to commit or not commit any act not contrary to the obligation they have due to the position they hold, even if the act or omission has already occurred before the request or acceptance arises, shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine.

2. The same sentence shall be imposed on an official who, themselves or through another, with their authorization or ratification, requests or receives, for themselves or for another, patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage or promise of that advantage, even if they do not have a right to it, from a person who has already requested, is about to request or will later request something that they must exercise the function they hold in order for that person to obtain.

Article 294

Active corruption

1. Anyone who, themselves or through another, with their authorization or ratification, gives or promises an official or another with that official's knowledge, a patrimonial advantage that the official does not have the right to receive, with the purpose indicated in article 292, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years.

2. When with the conduct described in the previous number the agent wishes to obtain the purpose indicated by article 293, they shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine.

Article 295

Embezzlement

1. An official who, contrary to law, makes their own or another's money or movable thing, public or private, that is entrusted to them or comes into their hands or they can hold by reason of their function, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years, when another legal provision does not order application of a heavier sentence.

2. An official who lends, pawns or, in any way, encumbers the value or object referred to in the previous number shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine, when another legal provision does not order application of a heavier sentence.

3. When the money or movable things referred to in the previous numbers have a value exceeding 5,000 American dollars, a prison sentence of 4 to 12 years or 2 to 5 years shall be applied, respectively.

4. When the money or movable things referred to in number 1 have a value less than 50 American dollars, a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine shall be applied.

Article 296

Embezzlement by use

An official who uses or allows another to use for purposes not included in its destination a vehicle or other movable thing of significant value, that comes into their hands or they can hold by reason of their function, in order to obtain for themselves or for another illegitimate benefit or cause damage to someone, shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 2 years, when those things come into their hands or they can hold by reason of their function.

Article 297

Abuse of power

An official who abuses power or violates an obligation, arising from the function they hold, in order to obtain benefit for themselves or for another or cause damage to another shall be punished with a prison sentence of 1 to 4 years, when another legal provision does not order application of a heavier sentence.

Article 298

Abuse with public force

An official who, having competence to use, requisition or order the use of public force, uses, requisitions or orders the use of that force to prevent execution of law, regular mandate of justice or legitimate order of public authority shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years, when another legal provision does not order application of a heavier sentence.

Article 299

Economic participation

1. An official who must intervene in a contract or operation or other activity, by reason of the public position they hold, and takes advantage of that condition to obtain for themselves or for another, directly or through another, patrimonial advantage or, in any way, illicit economic participation, and, thereby, causes damage to the public interest that they must administer, supervise, defend or realize, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years.

2. When this conduct causes the State damage exceeding 10,000 American dollars, a prison sentence of 3 to 15 years shall be applied.

Article 300

Refusal to cooperate

An official who, having received a request from a competent authority to provide cooperation for the administration of justice or for other public service, and refuses that cooperation or, without justified reason, does not provide that cooperation, shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine.

Article 301

Aggravation

1. When the holder of a political position or any magistrate commits the crimes referred to in this title, the maximum limit of the respective sentence shall be increased by one third.

2. Special legislation shall establish non-penal effects and procedure concerning the situations referred to in the previous numbers.

Article 302

Concept of official

1. For the purposes of penal law, the expression official includes:

- a) Civil servant;
- b) Administrative agent;
- c) Members of the armed and police forces;
- d) Anyone who, even if provisionally or temporarily, with remuneration or gratuitously148, voluntarily or obligatorily, receives a function to hold or hold with another activity in an administrative or jurisdictional public function, or, in similar circumstances, holds or holds with another a function in an organism of public utility or participates in those functions.

2. For the purposes of penal law, the provisions of this title shall also apply to anyone who exercises a political, governmental or legislative function.

TITLE VII

FALSIFICATION CRIMES

CHAPTER I

FALSIFICATION OF DOCUMENT

Article 303

Falsification of document or technical notation

1. Anyone who, in order to cause damage to another or to the State or to obtain illegitimate benefit for themselves or for another:

- a) Fabricates false document or technical notation, falsifies or alters a document or abuses another's signature to make a false document;
- b) Falsely149 inserts in a document or technical notation a juridically relevant fact;
- c) Falsely attests150 concerning the state or physical or psychic quality of a person, animal or thing, based on scientific knowledge;

148 (302) Gratuitously (adv) – Free of charge.

149 (303) Falsely (adv) – In a false manner

150 (303) Attests (v) - Declares in order to make people believe; certifies.

d) Uses a document or technical notation referred to in the previous paragraphs, that another fabricated, falsified or issued shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine.

2. Technical notation falsification shall be considered as an action that causes disturbance to a technical or automatic apparatus used to influence the result of technical notation.

3. Attempt is punishable.

Article 304

Aggravated falsification

1. When the facts referred to in the previous article, n. 1, concern an authentic document or document with the force of an authentic document, sealed will, money order, bill of exchange, check, other document transmissible by endorsement or technical notation concerning the identification, total or partial, of an automobile, aircraft or boat, the agent shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years.

2. When an official commits the facts set out in the previous article or in article 309, number 1, when they are currently holding their function, a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years shall be applied.

Article 305

Falsification of public document

An official who, in the function they hold, in order to cause damage to another or to the State or to obtain illegitimate benefit for themselves or for another:

- a) Omits a fact that the document that the law gives public faith must certify or authenticate; or
- b) Intercalates an act or document in the protocol, register or official book not according to legal formality shall be punished with a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years.

Article 306

Use of another's identification document

Anyone who, with intent to cause damage to another or to the State, uses another's identification document shall be punished with a prison sentence of up to 1 year or a fine.

CHAPTER II

FALSIFICATION OF CURRENCY

Article 307

Counterfeiting of currency

1. Anyone who counterfeits currency or depreciates legitimate metallic currency, with intent to put it into circulation as genuine, shall be punished with a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years.

2. When, together with the facts described in the previous number, the agent also puts that currency into circulation, the maximum limit of the sentence shall be increased by one third.

3. Anyone who, by agreement with the counterfeiter, offers to people to buy, puts into circulation or, by other means, diffuses that currency shall be punished with a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years.Article 308

Passing counterfeit currency

Whoever, in cases not covered by the previous article, acquires for the purpose of putting into circulation or already puts into circulation, sells or, by any means, disseminates counterfeit or debased currency, as genuine currency, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years.

Article 309

Counterfeiting of stamped or sealed values

1. Whoever practices counterfeiting or falsification of stamped or sealed values that only the Timorese State may manufacture or supply, in order to sell, use or, in any other way, put into circulation as legitimate, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years.
2. Whoever practices the acts described regarding postal stamps or other means of postal franking used by the postal service of Timor-Leste shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine.
3. Whoever uses the falsified products referred to in the previous numbers, knowing that these products are falsified, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine.
4. Attempt is punishable.

Article 310

Counterfeiting of seals, stamps, marks or cancellations

1. Whoever, with the intention to use as authentic or intact, acquires, counterfeits or falsifies the seal, stamp, mark or cancellation of any authority or public department shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years.
2. Whoever uses the objects referred to in the previous number, when he knows that they are falsified or without authorization from the person entitled to give it, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine.
3. When the counterfeiter himself uses the objects already referred to, the maximum limit of the penalty referred to in number 1 shall be aggravated by one third.
4. Attempt regarding the acts described in number 2 is punishable.

Article 311

Weights and measures

1. Whoever, with the intention to prejudice another person or the State, falsifies or, by any means, alters, or, having practiced these acts, uses weights, measures, scales or other instruments for measurement shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine.
2. Attempt is punishable.

Article 312

Seizure and forfeiture

Counterfeit, falsified or differentiated currency and equivalent objects, and also weights, measures or instruments to be used to practice the crimes provided for in this title shall be seized and rendered unusable or destroyed.

TITLE VIII

CRIMES AGAINST THE ECONOMY

Article 313

Money laundering

1. Whoever, knowing that any property or product comes from the crimes of terrorism, trafficking in weapons or nuclear products, trafficking or sale of persons, pornography with minors, corruption, fraud or extortion, tax fraud, trafficking in protected species and trafficking in human organs or tissues or other serious crime, which someone committed, with any form of participation, and giving rise to a penalty with a maximum limit higher than 4 years imprisonment:

- a) Converts, transfers, assists or facilitates any operation to convert or transfer that property or product, totally or partially, directly or indirectly, in order to conceal or disguise the illicit origin of that product or to assist the person implicated in any of these infractions to escape from the legal consequences arising from his conduct;
- b) Conceals or disguises the nature, origin, location, disposition, movement or ownership of or rights concerning that property or product;
- c) Obtains or receives that advantage by any title or uses, holds or preserves that advantage, shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years.
- 2. The penalty for the acts written in the previous number, subparagraphs a) to c), shall continue to apply even if the agent practiced the act constituting the predicate crime in non-national territory or does not know where the crime was practiced or whether the crime was actually practiced.

3. The knowledge, intention or purpose that must enter as a constitutive element of the crime may be recognized from effective and concrete factual circumstances.
4. Prior conviction for the practice of the predicate crime is not necessary to demonstrate and prove that the proceeds have an illicit origin.
5. The predicate infraction also includes crimes that the agent practiced in non-national territory when such acts constitute a crime in the State where the agent practiced the crime and in the territory of Timor-Leste.
6. The crime of money laundering does not apply to the person who committed the predicate crime.
7. Attempt at money laundering gives rise to conviction and may also give rise to a reduction in penalty as a general rule.
8. The penalty provided for the typical illicit acts described in number 1, subparagraphs a) to c) may not be higher than the maximum limit of the penalty provided for the corresponding predicate infraction.

Article 314

Tax fraud

1. Whoever, in order not to pay or to cause another not to pay, totally or partially, any tax, fee or pecuniary fiscal obligation that must be paid to the State, because of

a) Not declaring facts giving rise to taxation or which are necessary to make the assessment;
b) Incorrectly declaring facts that serve as a basis for taxation;
c) Preventing by any means or concealing necessary elements to fiscalize the activity or facts giving rise to taxation, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years.
2. When the amount owed that has not yet been paid is higher than 5,000 American dollars, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years.

Article 315

Illicit importation and exportation of goods or merchandise

1. Whoever exports or imports goods or merchandise which, according to law, must have a license from any entity to import or export, without having the license, or not passing through customs shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine.
2. When the agent practices the act through negligence, a penalty of imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine shall apply.

Article 316

Smuggling

1. Whoever does not pass through customs or through other mandatory control goods or merchandise entering or leaving the territory of Timor-Leste whose importation or exportation shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years or a fine.
2. When the goods or merchandise have a value higher than 10,000 American dollars or the agent practices the acts described in the previous number habitually, a penalty of imprisonment from 2 to 8 years or a fine shall apply.
3. When the conduct referred to in the previous numbers concerns goods or merchandise that must have a license to import or export or whose importation or exportation is prohibited by law, the minimum and maximum limits of the penalty referred to in the previous numbers shall be aggravated by one third.

Article 317

Customs evasion

1. Whoever imports or exports goods or merchandise but avoids paying totally or partially the customs duties or fees that he must pay because of the entry or exit of that merchandise shall be punished with imprisonment from 1 to 4 years or a fine.
2. When the goods or merchandise have a value higher than 10,000 American dollars or the agent practices the acts described in the previous number habitually, a penalty of imprisonment from 2 to 6 years or a fine shall apply.

Article 318

Exemption from penalty

The agent who practices the acts described in the previous articles may be exempt from penalty when he voluntarily pays the customs duties or fees to be assessed and his conduct is only occasional.

Article 319

Irregular administration of public funds

1. Whoever gives to public funds an application that is not the same as that which the law establishes shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine.
2. When that irregular application is not made for a public purpose that the law establishes, a penalty of imprisonment from 2 to 6 years shall apply.

Article 320

Disobedience to requisition of goods

1. Whoever does not comply with the requisition of goods considered indispensable to establish economic activity or public consumption, as ordered by the Government, shall be punished with imprisonment from 1 to 4 years or a fine.
2. Negligent conduct shall give rise to a penalty of imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine.

Article 321

Destruction of goods relevant to the economy

1. Whoever, by any means, destroys, damages or renders unusable goods that have a relevant interest for the national economy or, in any way, removes these goods from the legal obligation that must exist because of the interest of the national economy shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine.
2. Negligent conduct shall give rise to a penalty of imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine.

Article 322

Exploitation of illicit games

1. Whoever, in any way, exploits games of fortune or chance in places other than those authorized by law and without due legal authorization or ensures winning through error, deceit or any device, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine.
2. Games of fortune or chance are those whose result is uncertain because it is based only or mainly on luck.

Article 323

Disturbance of public acts

Whoever, with the intention to impede or prejudice the result of a judicial auction or against a judicial auction or public tender, manages, through gifts, promises, violence or threats, to make someone not submit a bid or not compete or, even submitting a bid or auction, that person does not practice these acts with freedom, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine.

Law 13/2008, on 13 October

Legislative authorization in criminal matters

The constitutional mechanism regarding legislative authorization is written in article 96, n. 1, subparagraph a), in the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste. This constitutional mechanism makes the legislative process go faster: the Parliament defines the general orientation of legislative policy that will shape the definitive diploma; the Government will make the harmonization of each aspect regarding legal technique, according to the orientation coming from the National Parliament. Thus there will continue to be a separation in the competence of each constitutional organ to exercise legislative power. This law for legislative authorization fixes its content and precise extension in order to guarantee respect for the fundamental rights, freedoms and guarantees of citizens in Criminal Law. The solutions presented also follow the general principles that the international legal instruments that Timor-Leste has already ratified consecrate, and also the social and cultural context of the country. The content of this legislative authorization also articulates with the Code of Criminal Procedure and complementary legislation already approved and in force for some time. With this approval, the Indonesian Penal Code will no longer apply subsidiarily in Timor-Leste and legislative diplomas that came into force after 20 May 2002 and have any norm defining crimes, penalties or security measures will be revoked or harmonized. Thus, based on articles 95, n. 1, and 96 of the Constitution, the National Parliament decrees, to be valid as law, the following:

Article 1

Object

Authorization is given to the Government to approve the Penal Code and to revoke current legislation on this matter, namely regarding the definition of crimes, penalties and security measures and their prerequisites.

Article 2

Meaning and extension

1. The Penal Code elaborated on the basis of this legislative authorization shall follow constitutional principles and norms and also the norms in international instruments that bind the Timorese State in this matter.
2. The meaning and extension of this authorization are as follows:

- a) Establish a legal-penal system that protects legal goods essential for life in Timorese society and reintegrates the delinquent into society;
- b) Consecrate the principle of legality and prohibition of analogy regarding the application of penal law, but with the possibility of applying the law that, after the practice of the crime, comes into force when, in a concrete situation, it favours the agent;
- c) Stipulate that exceptional or temporary law shall apply to acts practiced during the time the law is in force even if the judgment takes place at a time when the law is no longer in force;
- d) Establish norms defining the place and moment in which a person practices a crime;
- e) Consecrate the principle of territoriality in the application of Timorese penal law, indicating each limitation on this principle and situations in which the law applies in non-Timorese territory;
- f) Regulate the general prerequisites of crime and, in this, equate omission with action, except when the law has another intention, when any result enters the legal type of crime and the agent has an obligation to avoid that result;
- g) Establish that, to have objective imputation, there must be a causal nexus between conduct and typical result;
- h) To have objective imputation there must be intent or negligence, and the various types of intent (direct, necessary and eventual) and the various types of negligence (conscious and unconscious) shall be defined;
- i) Provide that criminal responsibility for crimes provided for in the Penal Code may not be transmitted and its agent is only a natural person, but also admit that there may be responsibility for acting in the name of another person and special legislation may consecrate the criminal responsibility of legal persons;
- j) Consecrate the non-imputability of minors under 16 years old and also the requirements for non-imputability due to psychic anomaly;
- k) Admit that the penalty may be aggravated when the agent's responsibility, at least for negligence, must embrace a result that does not enter the type;
- l) Consecrate error regarding circumstances as a basis to remove intent and error regarding unlawfulness as a basis to remove culpability;
- m) Regarding the various forms of crime, establish as a principle that preparatory acts may not give rise to punishment, and also rules regarding the punishment of attempt in intentional crimes giving rise to imprisonment higher than 3 years;
- n) Define the concept of attempt, the punishment of attempt up to where, cases in which there is impossible attempt, voluntary desistance and subsequent repentance, in singular acting or in participation;
- o) Establish the various forms of authorship, instigation or complicity, and define the responsibility of participants in culpability and unlawfulness;
- p) Provide for and define prerequisites for situations in which many crimes enter into concurrence, many sanctions enter into concurrence, crimes enter into concurrence with another infraction and many norms enter into concurrence;
- q) Establish rules regarding punishment for crimes that enter into concurrence, even with supervening knowledge, and also admit that accumulation may give rise to imprisonment up to 30 years and a fine up to 600 days;
- r) Define continuous crime and rules regarding its punishment with the penalty applicable to the heavier conduct;
- s) Consecrate causes that exclude unlawfulness and culpability, and systematically autonomize each one and define prerequisites for the application of each one;
- t) Include in one title various circumstances that, even if not entering the legal type, have relevance to determine the agent's criminal responsibility;
- u) Enumerate general aggravating circumstances and also general attenuating circumstances;
- v) Provide for a general clause regarding extraordinary attenuation, together with special cases provided for in law and delimit their consequences in the determination of the penal framework, with a model that includes various degrees of attenuation;
- w) As a general modifying aggravating circumstance, consecrate recidivism and criminal habituality with their prerequisites and consequences for the penalty;
- x) Establish a system regarding penalties and security measures and establish their difference in the agent who practiced the act with capacity or not to obtain culpability; in the first case the concrete penalty may not be heavier than the agent's culpability and in the second case it must be based on the agent's dangerousness;
- y) Enunciate determining principles to choose the penalty and security measure and also determining regarding the effect of penalties and security measures and regarding the counting of time in which there is deprivation of liberty before conviction;
